‘Give him the Heisman’: Opposing coach praises Jeremiah Smith after Ohio State loss

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Nebraska picked up the biggest win of Matt Rhule's tenure on Saturday, beating No. 14 Ohio State 31-28 in Lincoln and likely ending the Buckeyes' College Football Playoff hopes. But even after the win, Rhule had plenty of praise for the opponent's biggest star.

Rhule said that Ohio State wide receiver Jeremiah Smith should win the Heisman Trophy, and that he's never competed against a player as "talented and special."

Nebraska jumped out to a 21-7 lead in the second quarter, but Ohio State battled back to tie the game at 21 in the third quarter. The Huskers then pulled away in the fourth quarter on a 33-yard field goal and a 26-yard touchdown pass, before holding on after a late Buckeyes touchdown.

Smith did his part, finishing with 165 receiving yards and two touchdowns, including a 67-yard score on Ohio State's first drive. But the Buckeyes managed just 76 rushing yards, while Nebraska ran for 176.

The loss drops Ohio State to 7-4, and it's the Buckeyes' latest setback in a frustrating season. Smith drew criticism earlier this month when one longtime Ohio State reporter called him "a petulant child" for his frustration on the sideline during the team's loss to Oregon.

For Nebraska, which also improved to 7-4, it was the program's first win over Ohio State since 2011.

While the Buckeyes might have lost the game, Rhule didn't hesitate to praise Ohio State's biggest during his on-field interview with Fox's Jenny Taft after the game.

"Jeremiah Smith, give him the Heisman. I mean, we doubled him," Rhule said.

He doubled down during his postgame press conference.

"I don't think I've ever competed against a player as talented and special — and I've had a chance to play against a lot of them — than that kid Jeremiah Smith. That man, he is unbelievable, the way he can absolutely just tilt a game," Rhule said.

Smith is widely considered the top player in college football. While Ohio State has fallen out of the College Football Playoff conversation in recent weeks, Smith has remained at or near the top of the Heisman Trophy odds. Rhule seems to think it would be well-deserved if he took home the hardware next month.

The post 'Give him the Heisman': Opposing coach praises Jeremiah Smith after Ohio State loss appeared first on The Comeback: Today's Top Sports Stories & Reactions.

Simeone thrilled with Atletico’s position despite lack of goals from strikers

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Diego Simeone has called for perspective amid concerns over the form of Atletico Madrid's strikers, insisting he is "excited" by his side's progress despite their lack of goals.

Ahead of Sunday's Madrid derby against Getafe at the Estadio Coliseum, Simeone reiterated his confidence in his forwards, pointing to the contributions of Atletico's other attacking players as a reason for optimism.

Indeed, Julian Alvarez, Jonathan David and Alexander Sorloth have combined for just four LaLiga goals between them this season, leaving much of the goalscoring burden on Atletico's wide players, with Alex Baena (five goals), Giuliano Simeone (four) and Alejandro Grimaldo (four) leading the way.

Despite their strikers' struggles, Atletico sit third in LaLiga, five points behind second-placed Real Madrid and leaders Barcelona.

"On the goals, it's all about perspective," Simeone said. "I'm excited that we're where we are without the strikers scoring.

"Hopefully, the goals will come, and then we can build on all the good things we're doing.

"We're going to face Getafe, who are a very tough opponent on their home turf. We need to play a strong game and take the match to where we can hurt them.

"We need to maintain what we've been doing and continue managing our playing time so that we can show our ability to compete at the level we need to."

The Atletico boss also provided updates on defenders Robin Le Normand and Cristian Romero, both of whom have featured regularly for Atletico this season.

"We have a lot of confidence in [Le Normand]," Simeone added.

"We have a very good relationship, we often talk about what's been happening to him. In football, mistakes are more damaging to defenders, but with his personality he'll do well.

"Cuti Romero also is working very hard to recover as quickly as possible. Even without playing, he's very important. We need the best Cuti Romero."

Getafe, meanwhile, are 15th ahead of Sunday's kick-off, though Jose Bordalas' side remain unbeaten at home in the league this season (W3 D3), and have not suffered defeat across their last eight LaLiga matches.

— Atlético de Madrid (@atletienglish) November 21, 2026

Enes Unal has scored five goals in 12 games against Atletico in LaLiga – his highest tally against a single opponent in the top-flight.

Lee Kang-In has found the net three times in eight LaLiga outings against Getafe.

Against no other opponent has he scored more goals across his career in Europe's top five leagues, along with three against Angers for Paris Saint-Germain in Ligue 1.

Atletico head into this clash as favourites, having won 10 of their 13 away matches against Getafe in LaLiga (D2 L1).

Los Colchoneros have not managed more away wins against any other opponent in the league (also 10 against Celta Vigo).

Simeone's side also arrive in strong away form, having won their last four LaLiga matches on the road. They have only managed five or more consecutive away victories in a single campaign on two previous occasions – in April 2014 and February 2021, both of which were title-winning seasons.

Simeone himself boasts an impressive record against Getafe, having won 22 of his 27 LaLiga meetings with them (D4 L1). Victory on Sunday would see him equal Luis Aragones' record for the most wins by a manager against a single opponent in the competition, with the latter having beaten Espanyol 23 times.

Getafe, though, have taken points from three of their last seven league meetings with Atleti (W1 D2 L4), having done so in just two of the previous 20 (D2 L18).

Bordalas' side will also take confidence from their recent form at the Coliseum, where they are unbeaten in eight LaLiga matches (W5 D3) – their longest such run since going 11 games without defeat between May and December 2023 (W7 D4).

Arteta sweating on Raya’s fitness after Arsenal’s thriller with Newcastle

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Mikel Arteta was left sweating over the fitness of goalkeeper David Raya after the Arsenal shot-stopper was forced off in Saturday's 2-2 draw against Newcastle United.

Raya was replaced in the 70th minute by Kepa Arrizabalaga at St. James' Park after clutching his left arm and looking in some discomfort after a challenge with William Osula.

By the time Raya was replaced, Arsenal found themselves 2-1 down after goals from Harvey Barnes and Malick Thiaw overturned Eberechi Eze's 33rd-minute opener.

However, Arsenal hit back 12 minutes from time through Noni Madueke, ensuring they remained six points clear of second-place Brighton at the top of the Premier League.

Arteta, however, remained concerned about the fitness of his goalkeeper, who has kept the joint-most clean sheets (seven, level with Bart Verbruggen) in the league this season.

The Gunners face Borussia Dortmund in the Champions League on Tuesday before welcoming Enzo Maresca's Manchester City to the Emirates Stadium five days later.

"Unfortunately, he had to come off, which is never good news, and he will get assessed now," Arteta said of Raya's injury.

"He will probably get an MRI scan tomorrow, to see what exactly it is. At the moment, I don't know."

Arsenal's result saw them end a 21-game run of Premier League games without a draw (W18 D3) since a 2-2 draw with Wolves back in February.

Backed throughout a resilient away display Safe travels home, Gooners https://t.co/PvE3TEdYPZ

In an entertaining affair in the second half, Arsenal were edged in the expected goals (xG) battle, registering a total of 1.48 to Newcastle's 1.73 from one fewer attempt (12 to 13).

Newcastle had just three shots (0 on target), one touch in the opposition’s box, and won 46% of their duels in the first half, while facing 11 shots themselves (three on target).

In the second half, they had nine shots (three on target), 14 touches in the opposition’s box, and won 52.5% of their duels, facing just two shots from Arsenal (one on target).

And Arteta was left to rue a glut of chances while Arsenal were on top in the first half, conceding: "My feeling is that we have lost two points today. I say 'we', because we were exceptional in the first half.

"The level of aggression and intensity we put into the defensive aspect, the quality we showed with the clarity to understand the jumps and the way they were making every adjustment man-to-man, the aggression to attack the right spaces, and we should have been three or four up.

"When you don't do that, then in the second half, you know the opponents will try to react, and that’s what they did to us.

"You have to increase the level to cope with that, rather than stay at the same level. Right from the beginning, we're not at the races – we start to lose duels, start to lose second balls, we give the ball away and have really bad circulation balls against their press, and we give them belief.

"We did both, giving them a goal from a throw-in that was very disappointing, and then we gave them a corner, and left their best set-piece player free – we get punished.

"After that, we came back to being that exceptional team; we could break the press, run, create infinite situations where we could score a goal, we scored the second one, and we had infinite situations with overloads to go and kill the game and win it.

"We lacked that moment of magic to win the game in the end."

‘That was Newcastle DNA-par excellence’ – Jaissle delighted after Arsenal draw

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Matthias Jaissle believes he saw "Newcastle United DNA-par excellence" in their second-half display against Arsenal.

The Magpies held the Gunners to a 2-2 draw at St. James' Park on Saturday, after recovering from a dismal first-half performance.

After Eberechi Eze gave Arsenal a 1-0 lead at the break, Harvey Barnes and Malick Thiaw turned things around for Newcastle, though Noni Madueke salvaged a draw for the reigning champions.

Newcastle had just three shots (none on target), one touch in the opposition’s box, and won 46% of their duels during the first half, while facing 11 shots themselves (three on target).

In the second half, they had nine shots (three on target), 14 touches in the opposition box, and won 52.5% of their duels, facing just two shots from Arsenal (one on target).

Barnes' equaliser was the first goal the Gunners have conceded when leading in the Premier League this season, and Jaissle was delighted with how his players responded after the break.

"We played in the second-half exactly how we want to play football."  Matthias Jaissle on this evening's 2-2 draw with Arsenal at St. James' Park https://t.co/eQzA6p3wuf

— Newcastle United (@NUFC) November 21, 2026

"Mixed feelings," he said. "Like the first home match here against Liverpool, where a little bit more was possible.

"We also need to be realistic and humble. The first half was not good. The second half was excellent, so if you combine these two then I think a point is fair.

"Second half, the momentum and the energy in the stadium, you get the feeling that a little bit more is possible.

"We encouraged them to do the high press in the right way, with a front-footed approach, not hesitating, not doubting, just pure belief in the way we want to do it.

"You saw that in the second half, that was Newcastle DNA-par excellence. A point against the best team in the Premier League is something we can build on."

District 5-2A champ North Star faces big state playoff test in Westinghouse

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

North Star High School football coach Shane Smith made a straightforward observation about the Westinghouse team his Cougars will face in the PIAA Class 2A playoffs Saturday.

"This is a machine like we haven't seen all year long," Smith said of the undefeated and state sixth-ranked Bulldogs. "They are very big and very fast. On tape, they look like a very physical football team.

"We definitely have to play the best game we've played all year long."

Kickoff is at noon at Bedford High School.

North Star (10-2) won its first District 5 title in 15 years Nov. 13, defeating Berlin Brothersvalley 16-7 in the Class 2A title game at Somerset Area High School.

District 8 Westinghouse (10-0), the Pittsburgh City League champion, has outscored opponents 489-81, an average of 48.9 to 8.1 points a game.

The Bulldogs beat Karns City 50-21 Nov. 13, marking the third straight game Westinghouse scored exactly 50 points. Coach Donta Green's squad tallied a season-high 81 points against Brashear and followed that with 66 points against Taylor Allderdice.

"We're going to go in with the best game plan possible, but we know the test that lies ahead of us," Smith said. "We are going to have to play very disciplined, fundamental football."

Westinghouse quarterback Omar Pack ran for 170 yards and scored three touchdowns against the Gremlins Nov. 13.

Running back Jordan Reid's 131-yard rushing performance in the subregional helped the Bulldogs collect 413 rushing yards.

"We have to tackle better than we have all year long," Smith said. "Their team speed is just off the charts. If we give them a little crease, it's going to go to the house.

"We have to match their physicality. Fundamental, physical football is going to be the approach this week."

North Star is led by senior Kasey Yoder, fourth in the area with 1,413 passing yards, 22 touchdowns and eight interceptions. In the District 5-2A title game, Yoder carried 17 times for 94 yards, including a touchdown. He only threw three passes for a net of two yards.

On defense, Yoder closed the game with a 45-yard interception return to the end zone.

Junior back Will Eshleman ran for 85 yards on 15 carries against Berlin Brothersvalley, as North Star earned its first District 5 championship since a repeat title performance in 2011.

"We had a big win last week – first District 5 (title) in a number of years," Smith said. "We're going to run with the momentum we have. We've had a good week of practice. The attitude is upbeat and positive."

Westinghouse has had success against District 5 opponents, winning in the playoffs five consecutive seasons.

In 2025, the Bulldogs beat Meyersdale (44-0) in the Class 1A playoffs after previously earning 2A wins over Windber (2021, 2024) and Berlin Brothersvalley (2022, 2023).

Westinghouse reached the Class 2A state final in back-to-back seasons, falling each time to Southern Columbia in the 2022 (37-22) and 2023 (21-20) championship games.

Prior to the current stretch, the Bulldogs struggled against District 5, dropping seven straight playoff games from 2013-19 against Bedford (2018, 2019), Berlin Brothersvalley (2016, 2017) and Chestnut Ridge (2013, 2014, 2015).

"We're going to rely on Kasey Yoder, and we're hoping Will Eshleman has a nice game for us," Smith said. "Westinghouse's team fronts are very big. This will be the first time our guys will be tested by somebody bigger than us.

"I'm anxious to see how our big guys perform as the 'little' guys this week."

Mike Mastovich is a sports reporter and columnist for The Tribune-Democrat. He can be reached at 814-532-5083. Follow him on Twitter @Masty81.

Atlético: Diego Simeone flies to the aid of Julián Alvarez

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

While Julián Alvarez had a particularly difficult evening against FC Barcelona, ​​Diego Simeone wanted to publicly defend his attacker at a press conference. Despite two clear missed opportunities and the whistles coming down from the stands of the Metropolitano, the Argentinian received the support of his coach after the defeat of Atlético (1-2). The Madrid technician believes that his team delivered a convincing performance, but paid for its lack of efficiency against a much more clinical Barça.

Far from wanting to overwhelm his number 19, Simeone even insisted on his importance in the rojiblanco project. “He played a crucial match, involved in three actions and providing an assist to Giuliano. In the second half, he had this opportunity to lob the goalkeeper, then this cross for Baena. What more could you ask for? »he defended. Before lending his support: “he’s an extraordinary player, we love him, we need him, we need the best Julián, and we’re going to work with him. » A clear message from the technician, determined to maintain his confidence in the Argentine striker despite this missed evening.

MVG still a force to be reckoned with ahead of Grand Slam semi-finals

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Debutant Kevin Doets faces three-time champion Gerwyn Price in the first semi-final before Michael van Gerwen and Gian van Veen renew their rivalry on the big stage; watch the climax of the Grand Slam of Darts in Wolverhampton live on Sky Sports

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Michael van Gerwen has shown he is still a force to be reckoned with as he prepares to face fellow Dutchman Gian van Veen in his first major semi-final in over a year.

With Luke Littler and Luke Humphries already at home, Van Gerwen will be looking to get his hands on some TV silverware for the first time since the 2025 World Series of Darts Finals.

Van Gerwen, who threw perfection in the opening leg against Tom Sykes to record his first televised nine-darter on Sky Sports in 14 years, takes on Van Veen on Sunday, with debutant Kevin Doets going head-to-head with three-time champion Gerwyn Price in the first semi-final.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

"There has been a lot of criticism about me in the last couple of years, and rightly so, because I wasn't performing. Then you need to make sure you pull your finger out," said Van Gerwen, who traded nine-darters with Sykes on a historic night in Wolverhampton.

"To reach the semi-final is nice, but then to also do it the way I did it today.

"This means the world to me. It has been a battle; I have to work hard, I know that, but then to also show it, it's magnificent.

"I have been feeling good, and to also produce what I am doing, I hope I can do even more later in the tournament. I am feeling good, and when I feel good, I can do anything; I know that.

"I showed some real class and some real ability, and moving on that gives me goosebumps, that gives me the energy, and I hope I can show more tomorrow."

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Van Veen defeated 2022 runner-up Nathan Aspinall in a bruising battle to set up a meeting against his Dutch compatriot.

"As a Dutchman, a Grand Slam semi-final against Michael is always a dream coming true, but as you said, the way he played today, it was phenomenal," admitted the former European champion.

"If he does that on Sunday, I'm going to be in a whole lot of trouble, but hopefully he doesn't, and hopefully I'll step up my game and then it's going to be a totally different game and very close.

"Michael played phenomenal. Fair play to him, but as I said, it's going to be a totally different game on Sunday and hopefully I'll be ready.

"I will put 100 per cent focus in that semi-final and then as soon as I hopefully get that job done, then we will worry about the final.

"If you worry already about the final before the semi, you might lose quite convincingly."

Three-time world champion John Part speaking on Sky Sports:

"Michael has handled Gian well this year. He hasn't dropped a match to him. There's been nothing really hugely consequential about any of the matches they played.

"Michael's going to be feeling really good and that's the problem for Gian.

"He's going to have to come out, grind into the match, but he'll hopefully want to spark into the flowing, really better average stuff, the A-game type stuff.

"That was pretty good quality overall for the length of format tonight, but he'll think, ' oh, I can do so much better'. And he'll want to. And against Michael, who better to do it against."

Price will take on Doets with the Welshman into an eighth successive televised semi-final at the PDC this year, although he has yet to break his duck and is without a major victory since 2022.

'The Iceman' is chasing a fourth Grand Slam title and first in five years, but Dutchman Doets is enjoying a brilliant breakout season, claiming his first senior PDC title at Players Championship 13 in May before reaching his maiden European Tour final at the Austrian Darts Open.

He powered past Jermaine Wattimena to reach his first televised PDC semi-final, with his red-hot form impressing Wayne Mardle.

"Kevin Doets won 11 of the last 13 legs against an opponent averaging a ton. That's how good he is," said Mardle.

"I feel really strongly about Kevin Doets' future.

"It wouldn't surprise me if he deals with Gerwyn Price and goes on and wins it.

"He's playing so well so often, and you've got to play good enough often enough, and he does.

Evening Session (7.30pm)The Final[First to 16 legs]

Zlatan Ibrahimovic Named English Icon He Wished Played in Europe

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Over the years, Zlatan Ibrahimovic earned himself a fearsome reputation. On the pitch, he was never afraid to clash with anyone – including teammates and managers.

In retirement, as a pundit, he's been just as sharp-tongued when appropriate. However, that doesn't mean he's afraid to dish out deserved praise. Indeed, the Swedish icon – who won league titles with Ajax, Inter Milan, Barcelona, AC Milan, and Paris Saint-Germain across his distinguished career – is known for occasionally recognising greatness.

During his playing days, he actually expressed disappointment that one 'fantastic' icon, who just so happened to be one of the best English footballers of all time, never left to play for a club in Europe.

Zlatan felt Ronaldo 'took all the glory' for himself.

The man in question is Steven Gerrard. The Liverpool legend will forever be remembered as one of the greatest midfielders in football history. Most famously, he won the Champions League with the Reds and also earned 114 caps with England.

While many European giants would have loved to have signed Gerrard, he remained committedto Liverpool during his prime years. Ibrahimovic lamented this, once claiming he wanted to see the Englishman play abroad.

In 2012, ahead of the Three Lions' clash with Sweden, with Gerrard set to win his 100th cap, Zlatan spoke about how much respect he had for the midfielder:

"He's a fantastic player, always someone who plays at a high level. He's a good fighter and has good skills also and is strong with the ball.

"He feels more like an international player than a normal English player. Whenever I play against Steven the coach always says, 'Be careful with that player because he can make the difference.'

"He is respected throughout Europe but I would like to see Steven in, of course Liverpool is a big club, but in a big international club. I would like to see that also for England because it is very exciting when someone goes abroad and shows who he is in another competition.

"For me a fantastic player can make a difference in every country, wherever he plays, and I'm pretty sure that Steven would do that also. Of course, he is playing in one of the best leagues in the world now but he's a fantastic player."

Ibrahimovic was also asked to clarify what he considers a 'typical English player' to be, the then-PSG forward replied:

"Someone with a big heart, always fighting, aggressive, who never gives up. For me Steven Gerrard has more international skills than a normal player. England are used to playing 4-4-2 and the big teams in the Premier League are playing 4-3-3 with a lot of foreign players. It is developing like every other competition and every other country. They are a fantastic team with fantastic players."

Sadly for Ibrahimovic, and many football fans, the Swede didn't get his wish. Gerrard would never play for a European club, spending the bulk of his career at Liverpool.

However, the Englishman would leave Anfield in 2015 to spend one season in the United States. Coincidentally, he joined LA Galaxy, the same MLS side Zlatan would also later play for. Their paths did not cross in America, though, with the striker joining Galaxy in 2018, two seasons after Gerrard retired.

Ibrahimovic also came to England for a while, playing for Manchester United from 2016 to 2018. During his time at Old Trafford, he did at least get to share the pitch with another English icon he 'liked more than anyone', Wayne Rooney.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Kicker Smyth elevated to Saints' playing roster

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Kicker Charlie Smyth has been elevated to the New Orleans Saints' playing roster and has signed a new deal with the NFL franchise.

The former gaelic footballer, from Mayobridge in Northern Ireland, has started the last two NFL games for the Saints after being promoted from the practice squad.

The 25-year-old was handed a second opportunity to be the Saints' kicker after Dan Carlson, who started the season as first choice, was released at the start of November due to mixed form.

Smyth helped the Saints to an overtime win over the Cleveland Browns on his return to the team and then kicked a late 55-yard field goal in the 20-17 defeat by the Carolina Panthers on Sunday.

NFL rules state a player from a practice squad cannot be used more than three times in a season, and Smyth has been rewarded for his impressive return.

He made his NFL debut for New Orleans last November but was released by the franchise in August.

Smyth was retained on their practice squad due to having an exemption as he came through the NFL's international player pathway.

His first game since signing his new deal is at the Chicago Bears on Sunday at 18:00 GMT.

The Best is Yet to Come for Caleb Williams and the Bears’ Offense

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Chicago Bears' bye week landed as close to the halfway point of the NFL season as possible, with 17 games on the schedule, giving us an opportunity to check back in with the team's offensive playmakers ahead of this week's matchup with New Orleans. A chaotic first few weeks saw slow starts from ascending pass-catchers Rome Odunze, Luther Burden, and Colston Loveland, absences from Caleb Williams and Kyle Monangai, and worst of all, a season-ending injury to Cole Kmet. Despite the waves created by the acquisition of defensive star Jeffery Simmons, this team was built with Ben Johnson's offensive identity at the forefront. This latest edition of Benchmarking Da Bears will serve as a reminder of just that:

Comp Player: 2022 Jared Goff (Detroit Lions)

Caleb Williams came out of the gate on fire in Carolina, leading the Bears to a 59-point offensive explosion in their 2026 debut. Unfortunately, the following week he faced a torrential downpour, Brian Flores' Vikings defense, and a hamstring injury that would keep him out for three games. Since his return, the Bears have not trailed. That launched Williams into the MVP conversation, and even had him near the front of the crowd until an ugly 4 interception game against Tampa Bay took a little wind out of his sails. Still, Williams has been playing extremely well, and there's reason to believe he can keep up this high-level of play in the latter half of the season.

Comp Player: 2025 D'Andre Swift (Chicago Bears)

One half of the Bears' admired tandem, Swift has been the lone back standing in recent weeks as Kyle Monangai continues to recover from his bout with turf toe. Swift hasn't looked completely healthy, which has reflected in his yards per carry numbers, but he's managed to power through and produced some much-needed scores for Chicago anyway. Swift has already set a new career high in rushing touchdowns (10), with a hat trick in Seattle serving as a monument to his and the offense as a whole's ability to commit to the run game.

Comp Player: 2023 Sam LaPorta (Detroit Lions)

Speaking of injuries, I'd be remiss not to mention the loss of Cole Kmet. Kmet went down for the year in the Bears' Thursday night victory over the New England Patriots, leaving them with gaps to fill in both the blocking and receiving game. It would be a stretch to say they've found a solution yet, but Colston Loveland could help if he's able to find some of the end-of-season form he showed last year. He's started slow in back-to-back seasons; can we expect him to elevate once again?

Comp Player: 2024 Jameson Williams (Detroit Lions)

Comp Player: 2022 Amon-Ra St. Brown (Detroit Lions)

Comp Player: 2022 Kalif Raymond (Detroit Lions)

The Bears' top trio of receivers have taken turns stepping up to the plate this season. Despite a disappointing start, Rome Odunze has shown a lot of flashes in recent weeks, particularly when it matters most. He's helped seal the game against New England and secure an early lead against Tampa Bay – which turned out to be pretty key. While his catch rate and lack of scores are less than ideal, his yardage is there, and as he grows more comfortable, we can hope to see continued improvements from Rome. In similar ways, Raymond started the year off as the Bears' go-to pass-catcher and Burden has had sporadic bursts throughout. If any or all of these three can find some consistency in the latter half of the year, the offense can certainly elevate to a new level at the right time.

The Saints are the first team up after the bye week for Chicago, but after that, they start what has shaped up to be the most intimidating stretch of any teams' schedule this season. In the NFC North, they may need to do exactly what Ben Johnson's preached and play their best football starting in November and December in order to even secure a spot in the playoffs.

3 Falcons defenders hit with 4 fines from Week 10 loss to Chiefs

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Falcons racked up nine penalties in last week's lackluster loss to the Kansas City Chiefs, but they were all procedural — there weren't any flags for actions that typically lead to fines. But that didn't stop the NFL from issuing four fines total to three different defensive players stemming from last Sunday's action.

Defensive lineman Brandon Dorlus was the lucky guy who got hit with two fines, each for $7,114. One was for unsportsmanlike conduct/taunting, and one was for a late hit on a quarterback.

DeMarcco Hellams, who slid into the starting spot opened up by the Falcons' decision to trade Jessie Bates III to the Jaguars before the NFL's deadline, owes the NFL $6,509 for unnecessary roughness/a hit on a defenseless player, and linebacker Harold Perkins was also fined $5,203 for unnecessary roughness/use of the helmet.

The NFL splits the fine money it collects from players between the NFL Foundation to support the health, safety and wellness of athletes at all levels of football and the Professional Athletes Foundation to support NFL Legends in need.

Jake Matthews, $11,941: Unnecessary roughness, face mask, Week 2

Cameron Thomas, $8,333: Unnecessary roughness, use of the helmet, Week 3

Chris Blair, $5,972: Unnecessary roughness, blindside block, Week 6

Drake London, $12,537: Unnecessary roughness, striking/kicking/kneeing, Week 8

Drake London, $23,882: Unnecessary roughness, use of the helmet, Week 9

Brandon Dorlus, $7,114: Unsportsmanlike conduct, taunting, Week 10

Brandon Dorlus, $7,114: Late hit on a QB, Week 10

DeMarcco Hellams, $6,509: Unnecessary roughness, hit on a defenseless player, Week 10

Harold Perkins, $5,203: Unnecessary roughness, use of the helmet, Week 10

But they bounced back with a win over the Clippers and remain the only team ranked top five in offensive and defensive efficiency. The Lakers maintain their spot behind the Spurs despite Wednesday's home loss to the Jazz ending their winning streak at nine games and the overall struggles they've had during the homestand: a two-point victory over the one-win Kings on Friday and needing overtime to beat the Hornets earlier in the week. They're 6-1 in their last seven games despite Luka Dončić and Austin Reaves, who's missed the last four games, only being available at the same time for three-fourths of a game over the last two weeks. Nikola Jokic is coming out of his early-season slump. Getty Images

Three-time league MVP Nikola Jokić is coming out of his early-season slump (at least by his lofty standards), and the Nuggets are starting to look like the team most expected to factor in the dark-horse contender conversations after beating the Celtics and Rockets this past week. (Last week, 10th)

Man City Make Significant Change to Pre-Match Preparations for Fulham Clash

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

MATCH PREVIEW: Manchester City vs Fulham (Premier League)

The Premier League giants will be looking to bounce back from a notably poor run in the English top-flight after the final international break of the calendar year as Fulham visit the Etihad Stadium.

Prior to the latest pause for national team commitments, Manchester City followed up a record-breaking seven successive wins to start Enzo Maresca's era at the club with three defeats in a row.

Aston Villa were the side to break that run with a 2-1 victory at Villa Park, before Brighton & Hove Albion took all three points from the Etihad in a 0-1 win, and Nottingham Forest scored their first home goals of the season with a 3-1 triumph at the City Ground.

Those results leave Manchester City in third behind the aforementioned Seagulls and Premier League champions Arsenal, who hold a six-point advantage over the Sky Blues coming into the next portion of domestic fixtures.

And as Enzo Maresca and Manchester City look to end a run of poor results on the pitch, changes have seemingly been made to the way the club prepare for matchday action at the Etihad Stadium.

That is according to TNT Sports' broadcast of Manchester City against Fulham on Saturday lunchtime, with Enzo Maresca and his squad staying overnight at The Medlock Hotel within the Etihad Campus and connected to the Etihad Stadium itself ahead of matchday.

Stephen A. Smith admits ‘special’ player has changed his MVP vote recently

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The talk around who is leading the way for the MVP award is already heating up, with a number of players proving themselves to be playing at the absolute elite level.

There are several players who fall into the conversation, but the two main names, for many people, are Brock Purdy and Joe Burrow. Josh Allen is certainly still in the conversation, but Stephen A. Smith has narrowed it down to the quarterbacks for the San Francisco 49ers and the Cincinnati Bengals.

Smith had previously claimed his vote would go to Purdy following his excellent start to the season. However, the ESPN analyst has now admitted he has changed his mind over recent weeks and would give Burrow the advantage.

If the voting was now, it would be incredibly difficult to separate these two quarterbacks, with both of them showing their quality through the first half of the season. The advantages could be earned through their upcoming matchups against their expected postseason rivals.

"Three weeks ago, it would have been Brock Purdy. I think Brock Purdy has been big-time this year. What I'm seeing from Joe Burrow right now, though, is special," Smith said on First Take.

"When I watch Joe Burrow throw this football, the man is just special. When he's lined up behind centre, he's a true difference-maker. You can see why Cincinnati went to back-to-back AFC Championship games and reached a Super Bowl with him as quarterback. Again, I've been Brock Purdy, Brock Purdy, Brock Purdy all season long because of the way he's been playing, and I know he hasn't had Mike Evans, which is another reason why I would give him the edge."

Burrow has led the Bengals to a five-game winning streak, with some favourable games ahead of them before the end of the season. A meeting with Patrick Mahomes in December is one many will have marked on their calendars, though.

"When you look at Joe Burrow, though: A fifth consecutive game with at least four total touchdowns (the only other quarterback in NFL history to accomplish that was Peyton Manning in 2004). Seven consecutive games completing better than 70% of his passes," Smith added.

"It's a phenomenal thing to watch right now, and the Cincinnati Bengals are winning football games. Taking all of that into consideration, Josh Allen and Brock Purdy obviously come to mind, but right now, I've got to give Joe Burrow the edge."

Sassuolo, D'Aversa: "Berardi c'è, Volpato e Adzic no. Il Genoa è in fiducia, per noi è fondamentale"

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

12.20 - È il giorno della vigilia in casa neroverde. Roberto D'Aversa presenta Sassuolo-Genoa, la sua prima partita ufficiale sulla panchina neroverde: segui qui, in diretta, tutte le parole dell'allenatore dal Mapei Football Center. Start previsto ore 12.30.

Ore 12.38 - Inizia la conferenza stampa di Roberto D'Aversa.

Come stanno Adzic, Berardi e Volpato? "Adzic non ci sarà, credo che per le valutazioni entrerà in ballo poi la Juve che è la società d'appartenenza. Volpato non farà parte dei convocati, Berardi ha lavorato durante la sosta, ha fatto una settimana con noi".

Che sensazioni ha in vista dell'esordio? "Quando si riparte dopo la sosta c'è sempre un'incognita. Chiaro che per noi la gara di domani è importante, bisogna cercare di fare un risultato positivo per partire con il piede giusto. Il Genoa ha perso la gara con il Milan ma hanno inciso molto gli episodi e la squadra veniva da 3 vittorie consecutive, dunque il focus deve essere su una squadra in fiducia, con De Rossi che è stato bravo a superare il momento di difficoltà, ci sarà un seguito di tifosi del Genoa numeroso, ma per noi la gara è determinante, molto importante, per proseguire nel lavoro".

Come ha trovato il Sassuolo? "Quando c'è un cambio di allenatori i giocatori stessi sono i primi a sentirsi responsabili perché è una sconfitta per tutti. Se pensiamo però che il semplice cambio possa risolvere ogni problematica rischiamo di fare brutte figure, ognuno di noi deve far sì che le cose vadano a migliorare. Per uscire da questa situazione, da un periodo non bello sotto l'aspetto dei risultati, bisogna lavorare duramente la settimana, perché la partita è una conseguenza di quello che si fa ogni giorno e in queste due settimane noi abbiamo lavorato con un carico maggiore all'inizio, ma è chiaro che c'erano tante situazioni di infortunati o di gente che stava rientrando, ma noi dobbiamo lavorare e portare a regime alcuni giocatori che scendevano in campo stringendo i denti e questo è da ammirare e io penso che per giocare ci si debba allenare e non trovare la condizione giocando, dunque è importante avere tutti al 100%".

Virgil van Dijk has ‘no idea’ whether he will extend contract at Liverpool

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Van Dijk is approaching the final six months of his contract at Anfield

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Liverpool captain Virgil van Dijk remains uncertain of whether he will remain at the club beyond the end of the season.

The 35-year-old centre-back is approaching the final six months of his contract, which expires next summer.

He believes that he can keep playing at the top level for “many more years” but says he has “no idea” whether that will be at Anfield as Liverpool’s decision-makers continue to ponder over a fresh offer.

"The main thing for me is that I feel good and I'm looking after myself and I still have many more years in my opinion," Van Dijk told Sky Sports.

"Whatever's going to happen after this season, we'll see. At this moment of time, if anyone says that or put out that they know what I'm going to do next season, it's untrue because at this moment of time, speaking to you, I've got no idea either.

"But I'm relaxed with it because if you know me, I only focus on what I can control and that's at the moment the Manchester United game. Then we'll focus to the Champions League and whatever happens in the new year coming, we'll see then.

"But it's been an incredible journey, almost 10 years this summer and it's been a big part of my life and I'll forever be grateful for anything that happened over those years.

"If it's getting extended, we'll see then. If not, then I'm going to say goodbyes in the summer."

Van Dijk has won two Premier League titles, including the club’s first in 30 years, as well as a Champions League with Liverpool, stamping his mark as one of the greatest centre-backs in the English top flight’s history since his £75m arrival from Southampton in 2018.

He is one of the last remaining figures from Jurgen Klopp’s side that won every trophy there was to win between 2019 and 2022, with Mohamed Salah and Andy Robertson notably bidding emotional farewells at the end of last season.

Asked about the prospect of having to say goodbye to Anfield himself, Van Dijk said: "Well, yeah, probably for me the most and the family because it's been an incredible time.

"But I don't look too far in the future and that's been, in my opinion, also a little bit of a success. I won't change it now either.

Liverpool host Manchester United for new manager Mauricio Pochettino’s first game on Sunday afternoon.

Conte wants to ‘take over a club that is already organised’

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Antonio Conte revealed his ambition to "take over a club that is already organised" after receiving what he felt was unfair criticism during his time with Napoli.

The Italian head coach is currently without a role after leaving Napoli at the end of last season.

Conte guided Napoli to Serie A success in 2024-25 before helping them finish second last term, while they also won the Supercoppa Italiana.

Napoli have struggled this season under Massimiliano Allegri, sitting 10th in the league after 11 games, with a clash against Torino next up on Saturday.

Conte, who was reportedly considered for the Manchester United job, is hoping for a role at a club which is in a better position.

"There is one thing I'd love to do before ending my career as a coach, which is to take over a club that is already organised," Conte told the Cronache di Spogliatoio podcast.

"Often, it involved returning them to their history, a dimension that perhaps they had forgotten.

"It happened at Juventus, who got back to winning after seven years, Inter hadn't won the Scudetto for 10, and Chelsea were coming off a disastrous season when I arrived, finishing in 10th place, but then won the Premier League with me.

"The same was true at Napoli, where they had finished 10th, then I arrived and took them to victory.

Il report dell'allenamento di oggi  https://t.co/kyiSHtQynC

— Official SSC Napoli (@sscnapoli) November 20, 2026

"It is inevitable that when you arrive with a curriculum like this, you are accompanied by big expectations. People and the media expect you to win.

"I still remember when I arrived at Napoli, a team that finished 10th, a beautiful city, their objective was to qualify for the Champions League.

"I thought I could just enjoy my work and the city for a while, but the moment I signed, the media completely changed the message. I don't know how many other coaches would be treated that way."

Napoli are looking to get back to winning ways in Serie A, having failed to win their last two (D1 L1), and come up against a Torino side who are just one point and one place behind them in the table despite a three-game winless streak (D1 L2).

Scott McTominay has scored three goals in two Serie A games against Torino; only against Inter (four) has he netted more goals across Serie A and the Premier League.

Torino could become the first opponent against whom the Scottish player scores in three consecutive appearances in his career across these two leagues.

Giovanni Simeone made 83 Serie A appearances for Napoli between 2022 and 2025, his most for any club in the competition, but scored just six goals for them.

Simeone has also scored five goals against Napoli in the league, and only against Lazio (10), Juventus and Torino (both six) has he scored more in the competition.

Napoli are unbeaten in 20 of their last 22 Serie A matches against Torino (W15 D5), scoring 42 goals (1.9 per game on average); Torino's only two victories in this period both came at the Olimpico Grande Torino (1-0 in October 2025 and 3-0 in January 2024).

Torino's last away win against Napoli in Serie A was in May 2009, a 2-1 comeback with goals from Rolando Bianchi and Alessandro Rosina for the Granata, after Inacio Pia's opening goal.

Since then, Napoli have remained unbeaten in all 14 subsequent home games against Torino (W9 D5), and only against Genoa (16) and Udinese (15) do they boast a longer active home unbeaten run against current Serie A teams.

However, Napoli have won just five of their first 15 games in all competitions this season (W5 D3 L7 in 2026-27); the last time they had so few wins in their opening 15 matches of a single season was in 2003-04 (W2 D10 L3), when they were in Serie B.

Torino have gained exactly 14 points after 11 games for the fifth time in their last six Serie A seasons, including all three of their most recent campaigns; the only exception in this period was the 15 points they earned in 2023-24.

Tom Hanks’ Acclaimed WWII Miniseries Officially Celebrates a Streaming Milestone

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

It's no secret that Tom Hanks is one of the biggest movie stars in the world, and although he's now 70 years old, he's showing no signs of slowing down in this later stage of his career. Hanks is a two-time Oscar-winner for his performances in Philadelphia and Forrest Gump, and he's also been nominated plenty of other times in his career for other daring projects like Saving Private Ryan. Saving Private Ryan is one of the many exceptional WWII projects Hanks has given to the genre over the years, but most would agree it features one of the best performances of his entire career, even if he ultimately lost the Oscar. Another Tom Hanks WWII project that immediately comes to mind is Band of Brothers, the 2000 miniseries that he produced along with Steven Spielberg.

Hanks and Steven Spielberg even worked together just a few years ago for the Apple TV WWII show, Masters of the Air, which is the perfect mash-up of Saving Private Ryan and Top Gun: Maverick. He can even be seen starring in the smash-hit Apple TV WWII movie, Greyhound, which has a sequel on the way expected to be released before the end of 2027. Earlier this year, Tom Hanks fans were treated to one of the most underrated projects of his career, WWII with Tom Hanks, which allows the Oscar-winning star to truly expression his passion for the subject matter. Not only is WWII with Tom Hanks one of the most-watched shows in the world on the History Channel, but the series has also been in the top 10 on VOD platforms like Apple TV for over 150 days now.

Tom Hanks is attached to star in several upcoming projects, but only one has a firm spot on the release calendar. Hanks is teaming up with Callum Turner, Amy Madigan, and Colman Domingo for the new sports movie, The Comebacker, which is coming to theaters on July 30, 2027. Callum Turner was being eyed as one of the favorites for the role of the next James Bond, but Robert Pattinson was officially announced as the next 007 earlier this week. The Comebacker is based on the short story by Dave Eggers with Marielle Heller writing and directing the film.

Check out WWII with Tom Hanks on VOD platforms like Prime Video and Apple TV, and stay tuned to Collider for more updates and coverage of Hanks' future projects.

Sony Bravia 9 II Review (2026): Adjustable and Bright

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The current flagship television from Sony, the Bravia 9 II, is an RGB TV model (Sony calls it “True RGB”) that brings to life even the darkest scenes in Game of Thrones. Much brighter than the midrange Bravia 7 II, the 9 II's best qualities are related to its black levels, picture quality, and excellent viewing angles that allow me to see Jon Snow and the GoT dragons perfectly, even when I'm sitting off to the side.

This premium Sony television carries a premium price of $3,600 for the 65-inch model, but that’s still $1,400 less than the LG Micro RGB Evo, which retails for $5,000. I ended up preferring the overall picture quality of that LG model and some other OLED models, but—after my usual rigorous testing—I felt the Bravia 9 II came close to matching the high caliber of those other options. In fact, it’s just a notch or two lower than the absolute best television you might find.

The Sony Bravia 9 II is all black, sits on two feet, and includes four HDMI ports (only two are HDMI 2.1, though, which allows for higher-bandwidth streaming and sound at 48 Gbps). Setup was a breeze; Google TV was up and running in less than five minutes, which is an impressive feat, given that both Samsung and LG televisions typically add a few extra steps to the install process, such as authenticating your login (with Samsung) or relying on an app (with the LG ThinQ).

The premium Sony Bravia 9 II also offers exceptional sound. It includes two upfiring tweeters for more realistic Dolby Atmos than the Bravia 7 II, which lacks these extra channels. When I watched House of the Dragon, the room-filling audio made me feel like I was flying a dragon or piloting a ship.

Viewing angles on the Bravia 9 II are about the best I’ve seen on any television. I tested this element by camping out in a chair and playing video games off to the side, and there wasn’t a hint of screen glare. These perks are, again, a sign of its premium quality, verified through my usual benchmarks. Skin tones looked varied and vivid; one person had a little more red in her face than what I noticed on the LG Micro RGB Evo or the Samsung Micro RGB R95H.

In a demo-reel benchmark test I use for all televisions, I was shocked by the Bravia 9 II's stellar performance. I’ve watched the same short segment with a fence in front of a winter landscape countless times to test various models, but here, the green grass in the background looked supremely vivid and clear. It’s because the Bravia 9 II has such exceptional screen brightness, color accuracy, and absolutely no glare.

The Sony remote is just OK. I wish the Home button were centered; since it's not, I kept clicking the mic for the Google Gemini voicebot (which is in the center) by mistake. I prefer the bare-bones Samsung remote or the one included with an Apple TV 4K streaming device. Just give me a Netflix button and volume controls, and I’m good. Still, the layout of this remote mostly makes sense, and I like that it’s backlit.

The Bravia 9 II didn’t pass all of my tests for performance while I watched shows and movies, but it did allow me to tweak more settings than I’m used to. For example, the TV automatically calibrated colors and tones for Spider-Man: Brand New Day, but it still looked too dark. I was able to manually adjust the black level and used Vivid mode to make Spidey’s suit look extra red.

However, compared to the picture quality of an OLED like the Samsung S95H, the Bravia 9 II's deep blacks looked less realistic. That's because on OLED TVs, the LEDs can be turned off entirely. The Bravia 9 II performed OK for Toy Story 5, but in darker scenes late in the film, I noticed the Samsung had inky blacks that reminded me of a high-end projector.

My main takeaway is that it's nice that you can tweak settings on the Bravia 9 II to great effect, thanks to an RGB panel that’s super-bright. The movies Awake and The Creator tested my patience a little in that regard. I adjusted the black level to precisely 61 (out of 100) and could then see faces, a predawn mountain scene, and a beach landing with better clarity. Knowing you can achieve those results is nice; actually doing it does take time.

While playing Resident Evil: Requiem to test brightness, picture quality, and latency, I confirmed that it's possible to adjust the black level to liven up dark scenes. I also noticed that the latency, at a 120-Hz refresh rate when I used one of the HDMI 2.1 ports, was almost nonexistent. That helped with every slight twitch when a seven-headed monster from the dark underworld of hell started attacking me.

Similar games, like Senua's Saga: Hellblade II, looked too gray and washed out on the midrange Bravia 7 II and some LED models I’ve tested. I also played End of Abyss, a game that takes place almost entirely in dark hallways, and the Bravia 9 II rendered those scenes with inky blacks—granted, not as black as an OLED would, but better than many LED TVs.

Bravia 9 II lived up to its flagship-quality promise for sportscasts, making my beloved Minnesota Vikings' purple jerseys look vivid and clear and the grass bright green—all without displaying any glare at all. News programs were bright, with strong color accuracy, but not oversaturated—a good compromise. In fact, it was so clear, I felt as if I could reach out to clip an MS Now analyst's nose hairs.

The Bravia 9 II is near the top of the leaderboard of the best televisions to buy right now. You'll just want to consider the technology it uses. RGB models use red, green, and blue LEDs to create realistic colors that, in theory, improve color accuracy and vividness. Unlike an OLED TV, an RGB TV still shines those LED colors through an LCD panel. So you have to pick your poison. The Bravia 9 II looked super bright and vivid, especially during NFL games. It’s just a hair less colorful and vivid compared to the LG Micro RGB Evo.

Knowing you can tweak the settings for the results you want and that anyone in your household will be able to see America’s Got Talent from any angle in the room is a major plus. But is the Sony Bravia 9 II worth $3,600 when so many TVs cost around $2,000? I would say it depends on what you watch. I want to see dragons at night and monsters in dark hallways, but for some animated shows, sports, and network TV, you might just want your device to work well, right out of the box, without worrying about cinematic quality. Yes, a flagship lets you do a lot more, but at a much steeper price.

Marco Materazzi’s disturbing parallel between Zidane’s headbutt and the death of Jesus de Miguel

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Twenty years after the 2006 World Cup final, Marco Materazzi has still not turned the page on his altercation with Zinédine Zidane. A few hours before the clash between Italy and France, this Thursday (8:45 p.m.) in the League of Nations, the former Italian defender returned to the famous headbutt received in the chest, drawing a parallel with the recent death of Jesus de Miguel. The Tenerife striker died on November 3 after a violent collision with opposing goalkeeper Jesus Owono. A tragedy which pushed Materazzi to wonder about the consequences that Zidane’s gesture in 2006 could have had. The death of Jesus de Miguel made me thank God that nothing serious happened. In the case of this goalkeeper, we are talking about involuntary homicide following a game action… What if that had happened to me? “, he confided to La Gazzetta dello Sport. A surprising comparison, as the circumstances of the two incidents are different. During the 2006 World Cup final, Zidane deliberately hit Materazzi after a verbal provocation from the Italian, causing his exclusion before the Nazionale’s victory on penalties. Last October, the former number 10 of the Blues returned to this episode by admitting that he was not proud of his gesture, without directly apologizing to his former opponent.

A position that Marco Materazzi continues to regret. “ In his place, I would have said: “I made a mistake, but I was provoked. Judge for yourselves what happened, I apologize.” He would have passed for a great lord, and Materazzi for the usual moron “, explained the former Inter Milan player. The Italian world champion also recounted an unexpected meeting with Zidane in November 2010, in Milan, on the eve of a match between AC Milan and Real Madrid. According to his account, he spontaneously went to shake hands with the Frenchman, who replied: “ Don’t worry, these things happen, it’s in the past. » Materazzi, however, regrets that Zidane then claimed to have taken him for a simple supporter. Despite this persistent dispute, the Italian does not hide his admiration for the coaching qualities of the former playmaker. I like everything about his approach. He knows the players, understands the champions, gives them space and creates empathy. It’s fundamental to winning “, he stressed. He particularly praised his ability to manage the big personalities of the Real Madrid locker room, citing Cristiano Ronaldo, Gareth Bale, Marcelo and Luka Modric, with whom Zidane won numerous trophies.

NBA team grades: Report card for all 30 teams after the first month

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The first month of the 2026-27 NBA season is in the books, and it's time to take stock of what's happened so far. Let's hand out some early grades.

It might be too soon to draw sweeping conclusions, but there's been enough basketball played to make preliminary judgements about the contenders falling short of expectations and the surprising teams surpassing what most experts anticipated. Evaluating each team's performance goes beyond its record, with roster construction, player development and overall trajectory factoring into whether a season is trending in the right direction.

Injuries to several stars and key contributors have shaped the league conversation through the opening month. Some teams have responded well to the adversity. Others have already dug themselves a hole that could prove difficult to escape. As a result, the grades cover a wide range of outcomes: six teams earned an A from USA TODAY Sports through the season's first month, while 10 received a D or F.

Here are grades for all 30 NBA teams now that the first month of the 2026-27 season is complete.

NBA PANIC METER: Which teams should worry after first month of season?

Records as of games played on Nov. 20. Preseason record projections via USA TODAY Sports.

The Hawks are right in the thick of the Eastern Conference playoff race again with guard Nickeil Alexander-Walker leading their backcourt, but there have been some troubling early signs. Jalen Johnson's production has dipped across the board from last season's breakout campaign, veteran closer C.J. McCollum is sidelined for at least another month and Atlanta has benefited from the NBA's second-easiest schedule to date. The Hawks' issues are flying under-the-radar, but they've struggled to show they belong among the East's top contenders when facing the NBA's best teams thus far.

After thriving without Jayson Tatum during much of last year's regular season, the Celtics haven't missed a beat in their first games since trading Jaylen Brown this past offseason. Tatum has elevated his playmaking, and Paul George looks rejuvenated as his new running mate in Boston. The Celtics have the NBA's best NET rating as of Nov. 18 and even got the better of Brown in their first matchup against the Philadelphia 76ers.

The Nets' roster is a mess because they've used seven first-round draft picks the past two years and none has demonstrated franchise-player potential to this point. Their top three guards (Egor Demin, Nolan Traore and Mikel Brown Jr.) haven't played much together yet due to minutes restrictions and skillset overlap. Their past four losses have come by an average of 24.5 points, and Julius Randle was spotted yelling at his teammates on the bench before calling their Nov. 18 loss to the Chicago Bulls "one of the worst games I've been a part of." After years of stockpiling assets, the Nets' rebuild still doesn't appear to have a clear path forward.

The Hornets perhaps expected to take a step back after trading LaMelo Ball this past offseason, but their start has muted much of the momentum generated from last year's encouraging run to the playoffs. Kon Knueppel lasted just nine minutes in his season debut before suffering a hamstring injury, and he's one of several key players to have missed games due to injury. The team, meanwhile, has the worst offensive rating in the league after finishing with the NBA's fifth-most efficient offense last season. Brandon Miller is shooting less than 35% from the field as the top offensive option, and Naz Reid, the primary acquisition in the Ball deal, was benched four games into the season by Hornets coach Charles Lee for "not meeting Hornets' standards." Just one month into the season, nearly everything that could have gone wrong for Charlotte has.

The Bulls are starting to emerge from a 1-9 start to the season, though their recent surge is happening right as No. 3 overall pick Caleb Wilson was diagnosed with a broken hand that will keep him out for the next few months. Wilson showed intriguing flashes in his early appearances, but it's still unclear whether Chicago's core featuring Josh Giddey and Matas Buzelis is viable long-term. The Bulls feel closer to escaping the bottom-half of the Eastern Conference than they have been because of Wilson, but his injury means they'll have to wait longer to figure out exactly what they have.

The Cavaliers are off to a rougher-than-expected start to the season, and it's particularly concerning because their starters are the reason they're scuffling along. James Harden is showing his age more regularly, struggling to finish at the rim and get to the free throw line. Evan Mobley has already suffered a toe injury. Peyton Watson, the team's main offseason acquisition, looked completely out of sync before missing action recently with a back injury. Rookie Meleek Thomas and forward Mario Hezonja have been bright spots off the bench, but Cleveland's core has failed to resemble the group that reached the Eastern Conference finals last season.

Cooper Flagg appears to have taken another step forward in his development after winning Rookie of the Year, and his fit alongside Kyrie Irving has been better than many expected under new coach Dusty May. They've positioned the Mavericks as a legitimate play-in contender despite playing one of the tougher schedules in the league through 15 games. Their early success together strengthens the case for Irving to remain in Dallas beyond the NBA trade deadline. With a first-year NBA coach and a 19-year-old franchise star, the Mavericks are outperforming expectations through the season's first month.

The Nuggets remain a threat in the Western Conference playoff race, but there are some early signs that Denver's reign as a legitimate title contender could be waning. Nikola Jokic's 3-point shot has largely disappeared from his repertoire, a development that began after he returned from a wrist injury last season. His points, rebounds and assists per game have decreased compared to recent years. Jamal Murray's strong play has been offset by relatively uninspiring starts for veterans Aaron Gordon and DeMar DeRozan. It's no coincidence the team has gone from first in the league in offensive rating last year to 10th as of Nov. 19. If Jokic can't return to his MVP form and cover some of the roster flaws like he so often has over the past five years, it's hard to envision this group being a championship contender.

The Pistons were a trendy regression candidate in the East, but they've already got wins over the Boston Celtics, Miami Heat, Philadelphia 76ers and San Antonio Spurs through the first month of the season. Cade Cunningham is back in the MVP conversation, and Ausar Thompson is becoming one of the league's most disruptive two-way playmakers. Early injuries to Jalen Duren and offseason addition Isaiah Joe have taken a little shine off Detroit's strong start, but guard Daniss Jenkins is beginning to blossom into the secondary perimeter scoring option Cunningham has lacked in previous seasons.

The Warriors got an unexpected gift when rookie Yaxel Lendeborg quickly emerged as the leading NBA Rookie of the Year candidate and helped solve the team's void on the wing with Jimmy Butler sidelined. They reeled off eight-straight wins with Lendeborg serving as Steph Curry's sidekick. But Golden State dropped five games in a row after Curry suffered an oblique injury on Nov. 11, a development that has exposed Draymond Green's limitations and the lack of offense creation on the roster beyond Curry. Even so, Curry's eventual return and the prospect of Butler coming back gives Golden State more intrigue and upside than most expected entering the season.

The Rockets have looked like NBA title contenders through the season's first month, with the return of point guard Fred Van Vleet helping jump start the league's second-most efficient offense. Houston's turnovers are way down and its 3-point shooting is among the best in the NBA at the moment. Alperen Sengun, meanwhile, is on pace to break his career highs in points and assists as part of the Rockets' deep front court, and Kevin Durant remains one of the NBA's elite scorers. This appears to be one of the NBA's most complete rosters, blending ascending young talent with veterans who have extensive playoff experience. Considering the Rockets also possess the assets to make another significant move before the trade deadline, they may be San Antonio's biggest threat in the Western Conference.

The Pacers were hoping to rebound from last season's injury-induced tank, but they lost star Tyrese Haliburton to a hamstring injury after just four games. Indiana is one of the worst shooting teams in the league after one month, and it's wasting another strong start from Pascal Siakam. The lack of shooting has complicated Ivica Zubac's transition to the lineup, with poor spacing creating fit issues for a player Indiana spent considerable assets to acquire. He's on pace for his worst statistical season in six years. The Pacers entered the season expecting to rejoin the Eastern Conference contender tier, but they look much closer to a lottery team than a playoff threat right now.

The Clippers are in rebuilding mode and off to an uneven start, with the expectation they could be sellers at the trade deadline. The good news is that Darius Garland, the centerpiece of last year's James Harden trade, appears to have rediscovered his All-Star form after an injury-riddled 2025-26 season. The development of first-round pick Keaton Wagler alongside Garland is likely more important than the results the rest of the way, and he's shooting better than 40% from 3-point range since Nov. 2. The Clippers are one of the league's better shooting teams overall, but they've offset that strength by ranking among the NBA's most turnover-prone teams. They're likely headed for the lottery, but that's exactly where most anticipated them to be.

The Lakers are off to a fantastic start with Luka Dončić a leading MVP candidate and on pace to surpass Michael Jordan for the highest scoring average (38.1) since the 1976 NBA/ABA merger. Walker Kessler is also providing the dominating interior presence Los Angeles hoped for when it traded for him this offseason and gave him a huge contract. Kessler is averaging a double-double, while Austin Reaves is scoring more efficiently than at any point in his career. If Dončić continues to play at this level, the Lakers will be in the NBA title discussion come playoff time.

NBA MVP RANKINGS: There's a new No. 1, as Luka Dončić cedes ground

The Grizzlies have been the biggest surprise of the 2026-27 season thus far, vaulting toward the top of the Western Conference standings when many expected them to begin a rebuild after trading away Ja Morant, Jaren Jackson Jr. and Desmond Bane. Instead, the team's recent draft picks, particularly Cameron Boozer and Cedric Coward, have helped spearhead a balanced and remarkably deep roster that already looks like a playoff contender. A recent injury to veteran Ty Jerome looms large, but Cam Spencer has filled the void admirably thus far. Memphis owns the NBA's best offensive rating as of Nov. 20. Few saw that coming.

NBA observers were in panic mode when the Heat began the Giannis Antetokounmpo era with four losses in a row, but Miami is 9-2 since then with an increasingly potent offense built around pace and space. Antetokounmpo is leading the Heat with the fourth-highest scoring average in the NBA (28.2 points), and Davion Mitchell leads the NBA in assists per game. Only the Spurs' starters have a better NET rating than the Heat lineup featuring Antetokounmpo, Bam Adebayo, Klay Thompson, Mitchell and Pelle Larsson among groups that have played at least 65 minutes together. One month into the season, Miami appears to have already discovered the lineup it can trust most when playoff games are on the line.

The Bucks were expected to be bad this season after trading Giannis Antetokounmpo, but they're off to the worst start in franchise history with the league's worst record and own the worst NET rating in the Eastern Conference as of Nov. 20. With veterans like Myles Turner, Kyle Kuzma and Gary Trent Jr. taking up salary cap space after being acquired in prior seasons, this rebuild appears to only just be beginning. Milwaukee entered the post-Giannis era without a clear franchise cornerstone, and the first month of the season has done little to change that. It will be painful for Bucks' fans this season.

The Timberwolves have done a fine job keeping pace in the competitive Western Conference after losing Anthony Edwards to a knee injury that required arthoscopic surgery. LaMelo Ball, Ayo Dosunmu, Jaden McDaniels and Rudy Gobert have formed a well-rounded core that could be dangerous if Edwards returns before the playoffs. There is no timetable on his recovery, though Edwards told reporters on Nov. 19 he is planning to to come back this season. Minnesota's ceiling ultimately depends on his health.

The Pelicans got off to a bad start, won four games in a row and now appear headed nowhere fast once again. Dejounte Murray's recent trade request brought renewed attention to one of the NBA's most confusing roster situations. New Orleans hasn't been able to win consistently despite Zion Williamson enjoying one of the healthiest stretches of his career, and Trey Murphy III is increasingly viewed as one of the league's more attractive trade targets. Jeremiah Fears and Derik Queen are intriguing young players, but both have contributed to the defensive issues that have left New Orleans among the NBA's worst teams on that end of the floor. Murray, meanwhile, is owed enough money that moving him won't be simple. If this start continues, Joe Dumars would have to consider a roster overhaul.

The Knicks deserve the benefit of the doubt after a sluggish start coming off an NBA championship, though the early-season struggles of Josh Hart and Mikal Bridges, as well as the ongoing contract situation involving Karl-Anthony Towns, bear watching. Jalen Brunson and Towns continue to form one of the NBA's most potent duos, and New York remains firmly positioned in the Eastern Conference playoff picture thanks in large part to one of the league's best defenses. The Knicks proved last season they can reach another level when the games matter most.

The Thunder are doing their best to stay afloat in the crowded Western Conference standings playing without Jalen Williams, Chet Holmgren and Cason Wallace due to injuries. Shai Gilgeous-Alexander remains one of the league's best players, and OKC's deep reservoir of first-round picks is paying off with younger players like Ajay Mitchell and rookie Aday Mara successfully stepping into larger roles. The Thunder have survived a difficult opening month and came out looking capable of remaining in the playoff mix. But bad injury luck could prevent them from contending for a title this season.

The Magic hoped to emerge as Eastern Conference contenders, but they finished the opening month of the season below .500 and lost star Paolo Banchero to injury after just two games. A recent four-game winning streak that included a victory over the Boston Celtics showcased the potential of a group built around Franz Wagner, Desmond Bane, Anthony Black and a healthy Jalen Suggs. But the franchise is still no closer to determining whether Banchero and Wagner can thrive together as a long-term foundation. Until Banchero returns, that question will remain unanswered, and Orlando appears destined to be part of the crowded pack of Eastern Conference teams battling to avoid the play-in tournament.

After considerable preseason hype and past stumbles out of the gate for LeBron James-led superteams, the Sixers finished the first month of the 2026-27 season tied with the Spurs for the NBA's best record. James and Jaylen Brown have blended in well with Tyrese Maxey, Joel Embiid and VJ Edgecombe, though the team's starting lineup had a surprising -4.9 NET rating playing together through 16 games. It speaks to some of the unexpected depth Philadelphia enjoys, with Dean Wade, Anfernee Simons and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope forming a versatile rotation. One month into the season, the 76ers look like the team to beat in the Eastern Conference.

The Suns have rebounded from a rough start to the season despite playing one of the toughest schedules in the league, and Devin Booker remains among the NBA's top 12 scorers. But their two most-used lineups have been outscored by an average net rating of 15.5 points per 100 possessions. Jalen Green is having the least productive season of his career, and centers Oso Ighodaro and Khaman Maluach have looked overmatched while replacing the injured Mark Williams. As currently constructed, Phoenix looks capable of reaching the play-in tournament. But unless the Suns can find an upgrade before the trade deadline, their ceiling appears to be a first-round playoff exit.

The Trail Blazers have taken a step forward with wins over the Lakers and Thunder already. Damian Lillard is providing a perimeter scoring punch, and his presence hasn't come at the expense of Deni Avdija. It's turned a lackluster offense into one with a top-10 NET rating thus far. It remains to be seen how Ja Morant's return from a hamstring injury affects the rotation, but early returns suggest Portland's crowded backcourt can co-exist. This might not be a legitimate title contender, but the Trail Blazers look as feisty as they have at any point since Lillard led them to the Western Conference Finals in 2019.

The Kings appear to be the most dysfunctional team in the league, with a core of expensive veteran players who don't seem interested in remaining in Sacramento but lack significant trade value. Rookie Darius Acuff Jr. has been a bright spot, on pace to average the most points by a first-year player since Michael Jordan. But the Kings haven't translated that production into wins and they have the league's worst NET rating. Meanwhile, last year's breakout rookie, 23-year-old center Maxime Raynaud, has yet to play due to injury. This directionless franchise is no closer to finding its way.

The Spurs have looked every bit like a team on a mission to avenge last June's NBA Finals loss, highlighted by a recent 12-game winning streak. Victor Wembanyama is an MVP candidate once again, and the returning core of Dylan Harper, Stephon Castle and De'Aaron Fox has made the absence of free-agent acquisition Tobias Harris largely an afterthought. A recent hip injury to Harper does not appear to be serious, and San Antonio is deep enough to withstand his absence if he misses additional time. One month into the season, the Spurs look like the favorites to win the NBA title.

After emerging as one of the NBA's pleasant surprises last season, the Raptors have stumbled out of the gate this year. Kawhi Leonard has yet to play because of a knee injury, and Scottie Barnes has also missed time. The team's defense has gone from a strength to a weakness, particularly in close games, and Toronto ranks among the NBA's worst rebounding teams because of a wing-heavy roster that lacks a reliable center. The Raptors traded for Leonard with championship aspirations in mind, but the first month of that pursuit has been anything but encouraging.

The Jazz's hopes of emerging as a playoff contender after a lengthy rebuilding have been decimated by injuries to Lauri Markannen and Jaren Jackson Jr., as well as a disastrous start that featured a 12-game losing streak. Keyonte George played more like a rising star in two recent Utah wins and, along with rookie Darryn Peterson and 20-year-old Ace Bailey, gives the franchise a promising young core. But the Jazz expected to compete for a postseason berth and that might not be possible after an opening month that could hardly have gone worse.

The Wizards remain near the bottom of the Eastern Conference standings, and their decision to give Trae Young a $212 million contract extension is already drawing scrutiny. Young is shooting a career-worst 30.4% from 3-point range, and Washington ranks as the NBA's second-worst 3-point shooting team. Opponents have also targeted Young defensively in crunch time. The team's struggles are particularly concerning because Anthony Davis is healthy and playing well, while rookie AJ Dybantsa has shown encouraging flashes despite struggling to shoot from outside since returning from injury. Washington may still have enough talent to reach the playoffs in the East, but its biggest investment is underperforming.
