What's next for Arch Manning's future as Texas QB partakes in Senior Day?

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

"Closing Time" by Semisonic may soon be playing in the background for Arch Manning, as the possibility of his college football career coming to an end is looming, though that outcome is far from certain.

One milestone, however, is guaranteed for the Texas quarterback in Week 12: Senior Day.

Manning is set to take part in the senior day festivities ahead of Texas' Week 12 matchup against Arkansas at DKR-Texas Memorial Stadium on Saturday, joining the rest of the Longhorns' senior class as they are recognized for their contributions to the program during their time on the Forty Acres.

College football Fraud Alert: Texas wins, but Arch Manning is guilty

Manning has endured another up-and-down season for the Longhorns with the highs including a come-from-behind win against then-No. 1 Ohio State and wins against Tennessee and Ole Miss, while lows include turning the ball over multiple times in the fourth quarter in back-to-back losses to Oklahoma and Florida.

Despite all that, Manning still holds a strong 18-5 record while serving as Texas' full-time starter, which includes a 9-2 mark against top-ranked opponents.

That all said, there is a possibility of Manning prolonging his career. Once viewed as a near lock for the No. 1 overall pick in the NFL draft, Manning's draft stock is no longer as clear-cut as it once appeared, and he has another year of eligibility remaining, so a return to college football is far from an unrealistic scenario.

Here's what to know about his future plans:

Yes, Manning will partake in Texas' senior day festivities on Saturday ahead of its final home game of the season against Arkansas.

"I'm a senior, so I'm doing it," Manning said earlier this week.

Manning's decision to return to the Longhorns for the 2026 season followed in line with what each of his uncles, Eli and Peyton Manning, and his grandfather, Archie Manning, did by exhausting their eligibility before moving on to play in the NFL. Peyton and Eli Manning were both the No. 1 picks of their respective drafts in 1998 and 2004, while Archie Manning was the No. 2 overall pick in the 1971 NFL Draft.

While Manning is a senior and will be part of senior day on Saturday, it doesn't necessarily mean he'll be moving on from college or the Longhorns after this season. How come? Well, Manning has an extra year of eligibility — as he redshirted his true freshman season after appearing in just three games — so he could return to Texas for another season or transfer somewhere else. Or he could declare for the NFL draft.

Trick Play: Texas pulls off slick trick play against Lane Kiffin, using Arch Manning as decoy

Yes, Manning is eligible for the 2027 NFL Draft, which is the second straight draft he's eligible to declare.

It remains unclear whether Manning will declare after the Longhorns' season for the NFL draft or return for a fifth season. The Longhorns have KJ Lacey Jr. and Dia Bell already on their roster as Manning's backups, and have three-star quarterback Ty Knuster verbally committed to their 2027 recruiting class.

According to NFL Operations, to be eligible for the draft, a player must have been removed from high school for three years to be eligible.

"To be eligible for the draft, players must have been out of high school for at least three years and must have used up their college eligibility before the start of the next college football season. Underclassmen and players who have graduated before using all their college eligibility may request the league's approval to enter the draft early.

"Players are draft-eligible only in the year after the end of their college eligibility."

Manning is listed as the No. 22-ranked quarterback by ESPN's Mel Kiper Jr. in the 2026 NFL Draft class.

Polling NFL mock drafts from several major outlets — such as USA TODAY, CBS Sports and Sports Illustrated The 6-foot-4 quarterback is not in any first-round projections as of right now.

Back in late October, in the midst of a stretch of back-to-back losses to Oklahoma and Florida, an NFL scout told USA TODAY Sports' Matt Hayes that Manning's draft stock hasn't exactly been elevated by his then-19 games as a full-time starter at Texas despite having some notable top-ranked wins on his resume.

"Someone might take him in the third or fourth round right now. But a 1? No chance," the NFL scout told USA TODAY Sports. "There's any number of issues. Decision-making and arm strength at the top of the list."

Napoli & Juventus weigh up a January swoop for Joshua Zirkzee

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Both Napoli and Juventus are closely monitoring Manchester United’s Joshua Zirkzee ahead of the January transfer window, according to Italian transfer guru Fabrizio Romano.

While Zirkzee might have been eventually stayed put at Old Trafford over the summer following heavy speculation over a move away, the Dutch international failed to find any real regular opportunities under now-former United boss Michael Carrick.

Despite bagging a maiden Premier League strike of the season during his seven-minute cameo against Aston Villa prior to November’s international break, Zirkzee has yet to make a first start across any competition.

While Slovenian marksman Benjamin Sesko might have been ruled out for the remainder of 2026 due to an extended shin injury, the 25-year-old has failed to establish himself as a worthy deputy.

Nevertheless, despite registering just six goals in his 62 Premier League appearances since arriving at Old Trafford back in the summer of 2024, Romano is reporting that two Italian icons are planning on offering Zirkzee as Manchester United exit in January.

With the one-time Bayern Munich youngster reportedly making it clear that he is seeking regular first-team opportunities, both Napoli and Juventus are keeping a close eye on proceedings.

The Italian transfer expert suggested this week that alongside Napoli holding strong links with Zirkzee’s representatives, Juventus could be keen on a potential deal should it suitable for all parties.

Why Serie A players are wearing red paint on their faces in Week 12 fixtures

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Serie A players and coaches will be donning a splash red paint across their cheeks during the 12th round of league fixtures coming up this weekend to raise awareness for the ‘Un Rosso alla Violenza (‘a red card to violence’) campaign.

This will be the 10th time that Serie A has participated in the Rosso alla Violenza campaign, which is aimed to raise awareness around domestic violence towards women.

A number of gestures to raise awareness for the campaign are planned for the 12th round of Serie A fixtures: Players, coaches and officials will don the red face paint, and for the first time, supporters in the stands have been encouraged to participate as well.

Serie A captains will also wear a symbolic armband during Week 12. The hashtag #unrossoallaviolenza and several adverts and graphics will be shown in the stadiums and will be broadcast on TV ahead of kick-off.

“Serie A has been at the forefront of the fight against violence towards women for 10 years, and coincide with the international day for the elimination of violence against women during the 12th round of league fixtures, we will renew our determination and commitment to combating all forms of abuse and especially towards women,” said Lega Serie A President Ezio Simonelli.

Marco Chiesara, President of WeWorld, who organise the campaign, added: “Violence against women is not a private matter and does not only concern those who suffer from it. It is a collective responsibility. With this campaign, we want to reach a wide audience, particularly men, and invite them to question their own role in combating it.”

Peter Young is a Senior Reporter with Football Italia.

5 Years Later, 'Yellowjackets' Officially Solves One of Its Last Huge Mysteries

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Yellowjackets season 4 is already providing answers

Yellowjackets has plenty of mysteries that have yet to be answered as the twisty survival thriller reaches its final season. Is the show supernatural or just a psychological thriller? Are there more adult Yellowjackets still alive? What does the symbol the teen Yellowjackets keep finding in the wilderness mean?

Yellowjackets season 4 is series creators Ashley Lyle and Bart Nickerson’s last chance to answer those questions. However, the new season’s first episode, “Hivemind,” has already solved one big mystery.

The very first episode of Yellowjackets is memorable for raising several big questions. There was the tease of the Antler Queen and the mystery of which member of the stranded Yellowjacket girls soccer team had been skewered in a pit by her classmates. Both of those mysteries were solved in season 3, but another big one remains. Where exactly did the girls get stranded when their plane crashed in the remote wilderness 19 months ago?

Over the years, fans have speculated about the exact location the Yellowjackets could have crashed. Because the girls were based in New Jerseyand were heading to Seattle for a national championship tournament, the general consensus is that they landed somewhere in Canada. Canada has many beautiful cities and towns, but it also has plenty of wilderness they could have flown over if the plane had diverted due to poor weather.

Fan speculation seems to have paid off. In the opening sequence of “Hivemind” in 1998, we see a young Jeff Sadecki (Sam Braun) trying to sell furniture to a customer. However, he is interrupted by a news broadcast revealing that the survivors of the Wiskayok plane crash have been found.

Turning the volume up, the news reporter states, “It's believed they [the Yellowjackets] were located in a remote stretch of the Canadian Rockies."

Although no exact location is provided, confirmation that the girls were in a remote part of Canada supports the idea that it would have been utterly hopeless for all of them to set out in search of help.

For one thing, the Rocky Mountains stretch for over 3,000 miles, and the lack of food and water would makeit impossible for them to trek through the mountains without encountering danger, as happened when they tried and were attacked by wolves.​​​​​​​

Lastly, the plane’s transponder had already been sabotaged by Misty (Samantha Hanratty), meaning that even if people were looking for the girls at that location, they wouldn’t have been able to pick up the signal.

With so many factors working against the girls being found, it’s clearer than ever that they made it back to civilizationonly because some frog scientists found them in season 3 and had a satellite phone.

That’s one big Yellowjackets mystery finally solved, but will there be others? We can’t wait to find out.

Yellowjackets streams exclusively on Paramount Plus. Seasons 1-3 are available now, with season 4’s episodes airing through Jan. 22, 2027.

Italy U21 coach discusses Cisse’s rise, next steps for Camarda and why Bartesaghi is ‘a luxury’

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Italy’s U21 head coach, Silvio Baldini, has shared his thoughts on the Milan trio Alphadjo Cisse, Francesco Camarda and Davide Bartesaghi.

In recent years, Milan have shifted more towards giving some of their academy graduates more playing time with the first team. Bartesaghi was the first to properly get in, cementing his spot in the starting eleven last season, while Cisse has been the big breakthrough this season.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

Camarda has had a tougher time after a loan spell at Lecce, but Milan have made it very clear through renewals and words that they will focus more on the youngsters. It’s exciting times as well, given how they have all performed with the U21 national team too.

Speaking to Gazzetta dello Sport this morning, Italy’s U21 coach Silvio Baldini was asked about the breakthrough of Cisse, Camarda’s current state of mind and why Bartesaghi is a ‘luxury’ for his team. Safe to say that he likes the trio a lot.

Cisse has had a proper breakthrough. Did you see that coming?

“Compared to a year ago, I can see a different guy, who’s more mature and prepared, with higher self-esteem, psychologically changed, simply stronger.

“He has Milan to thank for signing him from Catanzaro in Serie B, believing in him and making room for him, then giving him consistent playing time. If he then makes the national team and does well, so much the better.”

Milan deserve credit, and you also deserve credit for discovering him as a centre-forward…

“Analysing training sessions, a series of situations emerged where he managed to score by attacking space and depth: if you can do it on the right and left, it means you can also do it in the centre. But even there, the credit goes entirely to the staff.

“They had the intuition, realising that Cisse could be useful to our game system in that way and at the same time, allowing Alpha to discover a new role successfully. I’m simply honest and meritocratic in my choices.

“Even though the goal against Armenia actually came from him as a second striker, at the end of that match we had two centre-forwards, him and Camarda.”

Camarda, by the way, has struggled at Milan but scored for the U21s. How is that possible?

“I can only speak about the work we do together. I spoke to him at length, I dedicated time to him, I asked him to explain his concerns, his fear of not being able to express himself as he would like. I told him stories of my own life, of how I came out of it.

“A dialogue not as a coach-player but as a son, or rather a grandson, as he could be, and a grandfather: from the heart. I’m not interested in performances as an end in themselves; they will come if he feels in the right place.

“I try to make him understand that I see him, I follow him in many small ways: he’s a conscientious boy who shouldn’t become obsessed with the goal, but rather experience football for what it is: fun.

“If, instead of fun, it becomes a test, then it worries you, and if you worry, you no longer enjoy it and it doesn’t help you perform at your best.”

To have more fun, do you recommend that he should leave Milan for another team?

“I’m not going to get involved in this. There are clubs, agents, and a family: they know what the best path will be for him. Let’s remember that he’s 18 years old, he plays for AC Milan and in the Under-21s.

“It’s clear that the more he plays, the better it will be for AC Milan and for everyone. We’re talking about a great player, a champion in my opinion, who needs to finish his development: he’s still growing, he already has a great eye for goal, and he’s sacrificing himself for the team.”

Camarda-Cisse: Can Milan provide the national team with one of the forward duos of the future?

“For me, yes. I see them as a permanent fixture in the national team, and of course they need to prove it and earn it on the pitch. But they have all the characteristics to do it. I found Alpha to be better prepared, even in his game decisions. Amorim did well to develop him.”

The third Rossoneri player is Bartesaghi: more of a full-back like with you or a winger like at Milan?

“Davide is one of the best players in his position I’ve coached in many years. He’s talented, very professional, mature, introverted yet open, and that’s not a contradiction: he has enormous potential, and he shows it every time he comes to us.

“If I were any Serie A coach, I’d take him with me. For the Under-21s, Bartesaghi is a luxury. He plays with heart, passion, and intelligence, as well as possessing high-level technical, physical, and running skills.

“He can play in any position on the flank. In my opinion, if he starts 50 meters from the goal, he can perform at his best.”

Andoni Iraola should snub Liverpool duo and start secret weapon vs Man United, the data is telling

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Correspondent AUTHORITY NCTJ-qualified football journalist with experience at VAVEL, Hayters TV, SPORTbible, and Centredevils. FOCUS Premier League analysis, tactical deep-dives, and original opinion content across Arsenal, Chelsea, Tottenham, Manchester City, and Celtic. THE INSIGHT Evan utilises a network of club and industry contacts to deliver verified, high-speed reporting. Cody Gakpo is Andoni Iraola's secret weapon ahead of Liverpool's clash against Manchester United on Sunday.

The 27-year-old has a superb record against the Red Devils, and will hope to add to it at Anfield.

But it is no guarantee that the Netherlands international is selected, with the competition on both wings intense this season.

Iraola will almost certainly deploy Bradley Barcola on the left of the attack, but it remains to be seen if Gakpo will be his pick for the right with strong competition from Rio Ngumoha and Victor Munoz.

Sunday's game will be significant for both bragging rights and for Liverpool to kick off their winter fixture list with victory, but for another reason, too.

Mauricio Pochettino will be taking charge of Man United for the first time at Anfield, and the Reds will hope to treat him to a baptism of fire.

The first time Gakpo ever came across Man United was during the 2022-23 season, just two months after he had arrived in the Premier League.

The winger opened the scoring that day, and would add the third goal in what turned into a 7-0 romping of Erik ten Hag's team at Anfield.

He has netted three goals since against the Red Devils, with all of them coming in the last three fixtures between the pair.

Surprisingly, Liverpool have not won any of those games despite the clear gap between the sides in the Premier League table, and they will hope to put that right on Sunday.

But it remains to be seen if Gakpo plays at all, with his position under threat from two of the next generation.

Both Munoz and Ngumoha have enjoyed strong seasons, and bring a youthful energy to the right side of the pitch.

Yet Gakpo's record and experience make him an equally strong option, and ultimately the way to go for Iraola.

It has been an interesting few months for Gakpo, who went close to joining Manchester City in the final days of the transfer window.

He ultimately stayed and started the season excellently, before tailing off in recent weeks following an injury.

Gakpo has retained Iraola's support, and Sunday's game could well be one to jumpstart him back into form.

"I would probably say Cody Gakpo," Iraola said last month when asked about Liverpool's most underrated player.

"He’s a player who has been putting up numbers, playing and performing for many years, and sometimes he isn’t valued because he’s just there and you take him for granted. His consistency, his personality. I would probably go with Cody."

Good morning, New York Giants fans! Here are the stories you need to as the Giants look to bounce back as they host the Jacksonville Jaguars tomorrow.

The upcoming stretch of games will reveal whether the Giants are for real or just the beneficiaries of the easiest schedule in the league in the first 10 weeks of the season. The Giants' first nine opponents have a combined .345 winning percentage. Their final eight opponents have a combined .566 winning percentage, which is the fourth highest in the league.

The Jaguars defense will represent the toughest challenge for McCarthy. His first three starts were against the Houston Texans (18th in points allowed per game), the Philadelphia Eagles (19th) and the Washington Commanders (20th). The Jaguars are allowing 16 points per game, second best in the NFL. The Jaguars have been particularly stout against the run, allowing the sixth-fewest rushing yards per game. They rank in the top 10 in yards per carry allowed, rush defense expected points added and success rate on run plays.

The Giants can't dwell on the opponent here, though. They need to stay focused on doing the basic elements correctly and consistently to be the best version of themselves. That's the recipe. "It always is. That's the formula," tight end Chris Manhertz said. "I think it's pretty simple from my vantage point: Take care of the ball, take care of your quarterback and execute at a high level.  Assignment, technique have to be on point. Every man is handling their 1/11th.

"Now it's easier said than done," he added. "There are people who are gonna win [their matchup] and people who are gonna lose. But I tend to believe if we're able to do all of those things on a high level in three phases, the outcome is usually good. So the goal is always to put all three of those together in concert. And if there's a void in one of those phases, the other two phases fill it and pick it up. But to me it's simple."

What we're hearing on the Giants: The Giants don't seem to like all the talk about facing the hard part of their schedule with Jacksonville, San Francisco, Seattle and Detroit looming over the next six weeks. The Jaguars are up first. The Giants aren't panicking after a tough loss to the Commanders. "We're so locked in right now on making sure that we worry about us," offensive coordinator Matt Nagy said. New York is also out to prove it's a good team.

Stat to know: The Giants are averaging 6.2 yards per play with quarterback J.J. McCarthy on the field this season, compared to 4.7 with other quarterbacks.

J.J. McCarthy was somewhat frazzled when he returned to the bench and sat down next to quarterbacks coach Brian Callahan seeking answers.

Giants traded for McCarthy to be a stabilizer at QB if season went sideways. His job now with NFC East title in reach: win. Nothing else matters. "I'm not sitting there like a scout saying he can work on his plant foot or his follow-through."

New York Giants: Is J.J. McCarthy a legit threat to Jaxson Dart? It's early in his tenure with the team, but a fresh setting has seemingly brought out the best in McCarthy following a brutal run in Minnesota. What if he keeps rolling like this and leads the Giants to the playoffs? And what if, by chance, Dart returns for those playoffs? You'd have to stick with McCarthy, right? It could get awkward well before the offseason arrives.

Likely no longer views Fields through a typical rookie lens. He described a player who understands the coverage picture and is prepared to win when the defense loads up against the primary options.

"I don't really look at Malachi as a rookie anymore. I look at him coming into his own player. So really just understand that if me and Leek are most likely getting most of the coverages, you're going to have to win your one-on-ones," Likely said. "And I feel like to this point, he's been doing a great job of winning whenever he's asked to win. And that's most of the skill guys that get their opportunity. But Malachi's definitely honed in on knowing that if I get one-on-one coverage, ball is most likely going to me. So I've just got to make my play."

New York Giants WR Malik Nabers vs. Jacksonville Jaguars CB Travis Hunter. The Jaguars head to East Rutherford to meet the Giants in a clash between division leaders. Amid fun storylines in this one will be Nabers and Hunter going head-to-head. Nabers has looked all the way back from a devastating knee injury. His 76.0 PFF receiving grade slots 19th among qualified receivers, averaging 2.23 yards per route run.

Hunter's first season operating primarily as a cornerback has yielded strong results. His 77.4 PFF coverage grade is the sixth-highest among qualified corners, with his 8.5 snaps per target tied for 12th-best. Nabers has endured two tougher outings in his last two appearances, sitting at 1.30 yards per route run or lower in each. Given Hunter's man-coverage proclivity, the receiver will need to be at his best against the athletic, physical second-year cover man.

Earlier this week, Jermaine Eluemunor was chatting with John Harbaugh. Eluemunor, the Giants' 10th-year right tackle, started thinking about how far this franchise had already come, halfway through Harbaugh's first season. "In past years, I was already booking vacations for the offseason at this point in the year," Eluemunor told Harbaugh.

"Yes, you had eight games left, but we weren't going anywhere," said Eluemunor, reflecting on the Brian Daboll-era misery. "It wasn't pointless and meaningless. But it just hurt inside knowing that we're playing in games that don't really matter. The type of team we had, everyone's mindset was: The coach is getting fired."

For more recommendations, check out our list of the best shows and movies on Prime Video.

After his acclaimed superhero series Spider-Noir was shockingly canceled by the platform, Nicolas Cage is back on Prime Video this week with the streamer's biggest new movie, which is also available in limited theaters. The sports biopic, Madden, stars Academy Award winner Cage as the titular icon as he grows from a Super Bowl winner into the iconic voice of football.

Dressed up in a full-body fat suit and prosthetic makeup, Cage is typically transformed for a brand-new role that sees him occupy the frame with several famous faces. Most notably, fellow Oscar winner Christian Bale portrays the late Oakland Raiders owner Al Davis, with the film also featuring John Mulaney, Kathryn Hahn, Sienna Miller, and Shane Gillis. It might have earned pretty poor reviews, but you should make up your own mind about Madden ​​​​​​this weekend.

Four franchises, four completely different ways of getting underneath your skin. This is the one built out of your particular fear, why it works so well on you, and what it would leave behind.

Your nightmare does not wait outside the door. It waits for you to close your eyes, which you are going to have to do eventually, because nobody has ever won that particular argument.

Your nightmare is not really the doll. It is the face of the person you tell, and the way they decide, very kindly, that you have imagined all of it.

Your nightmare has your number, knows your name, and has almost certainly been inside your house before. The worst part is that you already know them.

Your nightmare is not really about you. It is about the people asleep down the hall, and the feeling that whatever is here is not interested in leaving.

If you want something that's new to Prime Video, but Madden isn't to your taste, then how about one of the most underrated horror movies of the year? They Will Kill You, which somehow made just $10 million at the domestic box office, stars Zazie Beetz as a woman who takes a job as a housekeeper in what she soon learns is a demonic death trap, where she must fight to survive the night and save her life.

A fast-paced, adrenaline-fueled bloodbath, They Will Kill You lives up to its premise and then some. Not a beat is wasted in this gruesome slasher, which features Beetz in a note-perfect lead performance. The film is also darkly comedic, with its gothic setting, bloody premise, and electric performances combining for a film that will be pure, unadulterated fun for your weekend.

If you're trying to entertain the whole family this weekend, then perhaps They Will Kill You doesn't quite fit the bill. For something much more family-friendly yet just as fun, don't miss out on the brilliant animated sequel, Sing 2. Buster Moon (Matthew McConaughey) is back for the second installment, hoping to put on his most extravagant show yet, but he must first recruit the reclusive rock star Clay Calloway (Bono) to come along for the ride.

Ready to warm your hearts this cold November with a touching tale packed with funny moments and a toe-tapping soundtrack, Sing 2 is everything you could want. A whole host of beloved characters are back for the sequel alongside McConaughey's Buster Moon, including Tori Kelly’s Meena, Taron Egerton’s Johnny, Reese Witherspoon’s Rosita, Scarlett Johansson’s Ash, and Nick Kroll’s Gunter.

The NCAA women's soccer tournament is back, with a blend of familiar powers and emerging programs set to strive for a national championship.

While the regular season indicates there should be several legitimate contenders to lift the trophy in Cary, North Carolina, there is one safe bet entering the tournament: The ACC will play a prominent role.

At least one ACC team has appeared in the championship game each of the last eight years, with a conference team bringing home the national title each of the last three years. Florida State took the 2025 and 2023 crowns, but the Seminoles have looked mortal this year as the sport's most decorated program has reemerged. North Carolina, which won its 22nd national championship two years ago, looks like the team to beat once again after taking the ACC title.

There will be challengers, though. Vanderbilt suffered its first loss of the season in the SEC quarterfinals and is looking for its first NCAA title, along with a trio of Big Ten teams in Purdue, Michigan State and Ohio State.

Who will emerge from the 64-team field? Here's all you need to know to watch every game.

Veteran NFL safety Justin Reid announces his release from home-state New Orleans Saints

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

METAIRIE, La. (AP) — The New Orleans Saints released veteran NFL safety Justin Reid on Friday, ending the 29-year-old defensive captain's stint with the club after a season and a half.

"I genuinely enjoyed my time here and have nothing but love and respect for this city and the people who make it so special," Reid said in a social media post. "In life and in sports, things don't always go the way you planned or hoped they would. Unfortunately, this is one of those moments."

The move allows the 29-year-old Reid — a two-time Super Bowl winner with the Kansas City Chiefs — to try to latch on with another team during the latter half of this season.

Saints coach Kellen Moore portrayed the decision to release Reid as mutual between the team and player.

"Good conversations. Love Justin. Felt like this was obviously the best for everyone," Saints coach Kellen Moore said. "Justin's a good player, awesome person."

While Moore declined Friday to go into detail about the conversations Reid had with the coaching staff and front office, he indicated that the Saints had decided to replace Reid in the starting lineup with Jordan Howden, a 2023 fifth-round draft choice.

"Jordan's played a little bit more the last few weeks," Moore noted. "He's earned those opportunities."

Howden will now join Julian Blackmon, acquired as a free agent in 2025, as the Saints starting safeties.

"That combination will be good," Moore said. "This will be a great opportunity for Jordan."

Reid grew up near Baton Rouge, Louisiana, and is now in his ninth NFL season out of Stanford. He joined the Saints as a free agent in 2025 and has started all 24 games in which he has appeared. He had one interception last season but none this season.

The 29-year-old Reid began his NFL career with Houston and later played for Kansas City, where he was part of two Super Bowl-winning Chiefs teams.

He'd hoped to help the Saints rebuild under coach Kellen Moore, who was hired in 2025, shortly after serving as offensive coordinator for the 2024 Philadelphia Eagles. That Eagles squad defeated Reid's Chiefs in the Super Bowl the season in New Orleans.

The Saints went 6-11 in 2025 and entered this weekend at 3-6. They are scheduled to play at Chicago on Sunday.

"That doesn't change the love and respect I have for this organization or this city," Reid said. "I truly want to see this team and this city thrive. No one deserves it more than New Orleans and the Saints."

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Xabi Alonso has called on his Chelsea players to step up in front of goal in the absence of the injured Joao Pedro.

Joao Pedro has missed Chelsea's last four games across all competitions, including three in the Premier League, after suffering a thigh injury.

The Blues have failed to score in two of those three league games without the Brazilian, losing to Tottenham and Sunderland either side of a win over Manchester United.

Three of Chelsea’s four Premier League defeats this season have come to nil, and without Joao Pedro playing. The Blues average 1.8 goals per league game with him in the starting XI since the start of last season, compared to 0.9 goals without him starting.

Joao Pedro has scored five goals and provided three assists in six league games this season, more than any other Chelsea player.

Only Erling Haaland and Evanilson (both nine) have directly contributed to more goals than Joao Pedro in the English top-flight this season, but Alonso does not want to use his striker's injury as an excuse for his side.

"[We need] the whole team for that," Alonso said. "It's not just the strikers that can score. We can score from set-pieces, from defenders, from midfielders.

Things look bleak for Warriors, but they can survive this

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Warriors are in free fall, losing five games in a row, including their last four by 20 or more points.

Golden State Warriors guard Stephen Curry, right, confers with head coach Steve Kerr during an NBA game in Denver, Monday, Oct. 26, 2026. AP Photo/David Zalubowski

Of the 15 Western Conference teams, only 11 are going to have a real shot at securing the 10 playoff spots unless something drastically changes.

In other words, the West cellar is so weak that even if the Warriors can't arrest their precipitous slide without Steph Curry, they'll have more than enough time to recover when he returns.

So, while what's happening to the Warriors is unfortunate, it's far from catastrophic.

Curry is expected to be sidelined close to a month after suffering a right oblique abdominal tear on Nov. 11.

The 38-year-old had carried the Warriors to eight straight wins while playing some of the best basketball of his career, averaging 27.8 points (50% shooting from the field, 45.7% from beyond the arc), 3.7 rebounds and 3.9 assists per game.

Before Curry sustained the injury, he and Yaxel Lendeborg were looking like one of the NBA's best duos. Lendeborg, whom the Warriors drafted with the No. 11 overall pick in June, was having a Rookie of the Year campaign, averaging 21.6 points on 52.2% shooting.

But with defenses collapsing on him without Curry, the rookie has wilted, averaging 13.7 points on 26.8% shooting over the last four games.

Not only is Curry going to be back in the lineup soon, but Jimmy Butler (torn right ACL) is expected to return midseason.

The Warriors could still be really good. They could still have a deep playoff run. They could still be a contender.

This has been a roller coaster for Warriors fans.

Entering the season, it was questionable whether the Warriors would make the playoffs after they pretty much stood pat during the offseason. And after dropping their first three games, it seemed all but guaranteed that this was going to be a wasted season for Curry, a two-time MVP who carried the Warriors to four championships in eight years from 2015-2022.

Lendeborg emerged as his sidekick. He sprinted past his expectations. He became a force.

Yaxel Lendeborg, left, and Golden State Warriors head coach Steve Kerr smile during a game against the Houston Rockets on Wednesday, Nov. 11, 2026, in Houston. NBAE via Getty Images

All of a sudden, Curry and Lendeborg were stomping on their competition, beating contenders such as the Lakers, Timberwolves and Nuggets over a stunning stretch that had pundits questioning whether the Warriors were championship contenders.

But the short-handed Warriors were always one Curry injury away from disaster.

And when their nightmare was realized, the Warriors went from being one of the league's most fun teams to one of its biggest duds.

Golden State Warriors head coach Steve Kerr watches play during an NBA game against the San Antonio Spurs on Friday, Nov. 13, 2026, in San Antonio. AP Photo/Darren Abate

Curry and Butler will return, and the Warriors will have more than enough runway to correct course.

So the excitement the team was generating a little over a week ago?

Premier League news: Moyes demands more from Grealish, Iraola wary of United threat

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Everton’s playmaker returns after being stood down, while Liverpool’s head coach keeps focus for Anfield showdown

David Moyes has said he wants Jack Grealish to produce more for Everton as he prepares to reintegrate the playmaker into the squad.

Grealish has missed Everton’s past four games mainly for disciplinary reasons having failed to report for training before last month’s Carabao Cup win over Newcastle. His last appearance for the team was in the 2-0 loss to Chelsea on 17 October but he is fit and available for Monday’s visit to Brentford.

“He has served his punishment and we will treat him as we were treating him before,” the Everton manager said. “We will get on with things. The team is doing really well at the moment and what I don’t want to do is turn it into a Jack story because it shouldn’t be.”

The 31-year-old played in a behind-closed-doors fixture against Derby last week andalso apologised on social media for being unable to help the team, who won three of four matches in his absence. Grealish, however, had struggled to replicate last season’s form impact when joining on loan from Manchester City prior to his breach of discipline. And Moyes is demanding more.

He added: “Jack can offer us a lot. I want more from him, I want him to show more than he has done. I want him to give me more assists, more goals. There’s lots of things about him which we need and he’s good at retaining the ball, giving us good possession, carrying. So we hope that he can get back to those levels and give us what we need. I think this time last year he was probably sitting on five or six assists so we’re hoping to get that again.”

The Everton manager has backed Jordan Pickford to respond strongly to the threat from James Trafford to his England place, following after the Leeds goalkeeper’s inclusion and impressive display in the Nations League win over Spain.

“I don’t think it will have a great effect on him,” claimed Moyes. “If you’re playing for England you will always have other players chasing you. I still think Jordan Pickford is out there on his own at the moment. If anything, he’s getting better. It will be good for us now that he’s getting competition [with England].” Andy Hunter

Andoni Iraola has said Liverpool must disregard the latest turmoil at Manchester United and treat Sunday’s visitors like the team that thrived under Michael Carrick last season.

Sunday will be the first time since March 1937 that United have faced Liverpool in a league game at Anfield while languishing in the relegation zone. Mauricio Pochettino could start his United reign bottom of the table should results on Saturday go against his new club.

Iraola never lost in six league meetings with United as Bournemouth head coach. Before his first taste of the fixture with Liverpool, he insisted his north-west rivals are in a false position.

“United finished third in the table last season. You are looking at a squad with exactly the same players. They had very, very good results, especially in the second half of the season,” Iraola said. “Now we have just started, it is just 10 games. For sure, they will be a lot higher in the table [soon]. We have to rate them like this and the truth is the reference we have is that they finished third.

“We have to look at ourselves because we need the points. We need to improve our home form. It is also a big game in terms of relevance. It’s Liverpool v Manchester United, this is the biggest one in English football, so we want to play well and we want to win.”

Iraola was boosted this week by the returns of Conor Bradley and Giovanni Leoni to training after serious knee injuries, although the two defenders are not yet ready for consideration. “We think they will have two weeks on and off with the group,” he said. “Then it is going to be a matter of whether we organise something behind closed doors, 11 v 11, or they straight away play with the under-21s. Before they start playing with us they have to go through that.”

Liverpool’s other long-term absentee, Hugo Ekitiké, remains on course to return in January from an achilles injury. Andy Hunter

Xabi Alonso has admitted Chelsea’s injury list is a concern. The club have a lighter schedule after their failure to qualify for Europe but they are struggling to keep players fit and have a host of selection issues before Saturday’s home game against Leeds.

The Brazil striker João Pedro is yet to return from a thigh injury, Reece James is unlikely to recover from knee surgery until the new year and the France centre-back Maxence Lacroix is out with a long-term hamstring injury. Alonso is having to adjust. Jorrel Hato is unavailable in defence with a back injury and the England Under-21 winger Jamie Gittens, who has made five league starts for Chelsea since joining for £51.5m in the summer of 2025, has been out since September.

“It’s a concern,” Alonso said of the injuries. “Sometimes you can analyse them and find a reason but sometimes it is difficult to find a reason.”

Alonso has made 24 changes to his lineup in his first 10 league games. “It depends on the context and adaptability. I adapt to the circumstances. With the injuries you don’t have a choice. Once you have the whole menu, you can select what you like and I know what I like from the menu.”

Chelsea have failed to score in three of the four league games João Pedro has missed this season. They have also had to monitor Moisés Caicedo closely, with the midfielder minding a muscle issue. But Alonso wants his side to become less reliant on Caicedo and João Pedro.

“We want to have a complete squad,” he said. “That’s the goal of any manager. We have built a good squad. We are a better team with them. But it’s not about making a big drama when someone is out.” Jacob Steinberg

Enzo Maresca has said he may need to tweak the balance of Manchester City’s midfield amid their faltering Premier League form.

City went into the November international break on the back of three consecutive defeats in the league, having begun the campaign with seven wins on the bounce. Maresca’s side are third in the table, six points off leaders Arsenal, and face Fulham at the Etihad Stadium on Saturday.

Maresca defended Enzo Fernández, whose performances have prompted debate over his best position and whether he leaves City’s team unbalanced. Maresca has mostly opted to play the £125m signing from Chelsea deeper in midfield alongside fellow summer recruit Elliot Anderson, with the more defensive-minded Ayyoub Bouaddi, who also arrived in the summer, largely a substitute.

“For sure there are things we need to do better in terms of balance,” Maresca said. “But the same midfield that won seven games in a row is the same midfield that lost three games in a row.

“We won games with Enzo, we lost games with Enzo. I know that when there is a defeat, we are all looking for the reason why. Sometimes we probably need more balance, we need different kinds of players. [It] depends a little bit on the game. The idea is to continue in attack in the same way, creating chances, but finding a balance when we defend.”

Maresca also dismissed the notion that any uncertainty surrounding City’s financial case with the Premier League has negatively affected recent performances. It has been nearly two months since City were found guilty of more than 100 charges related to breaches of the Premier League’s financial rules, and they are waiting for the outcome of their appeal. All three of City’s league defeats have come since the ruling.

“I don’t think the players are thinking too much about the noise,” Maresca said. “I don’t think they are worried about it.” Dominic Booth

Nicolas Cage’s New Sports Movie Officially Torn Apart by Rotten Tomatoes Critics

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Nicolas Cage is a man with a wonderful reputation for excellent films. The most unique man in Hollywood won the Academy Award for Best Actor in 1996 for his role in Leaving Las Vegas, earned another nomination for 2002's Adaptation, and has appeared in several critically acclaimed masterpieces, such as 1987's Raising Arizona, 1997's Face/Off, and, more recently, 2022's The Unbearable Weight of Massive Talent, where he perfectly played an alternate version of himself.

But Cage is also no stranger to negative headlines among his many hits. Recently, he was caught up in controversy after speaking out against modern superhero projects, following the cancellation of his hit series Spider-Noir, an offshoot of the animated Spider-Verse films (Spider-Man: Into the Spider-Verse and Spider-Man: Across the Spider-Verse). "When I see these 50-plus and otherwise intelligent and remarkably gifted thespians putting on these ridiculous outfits and getting ready for the fight," he said, "it’s hard for me not to be reminded that the MCU is becoming a well-funded and glorified WWE."

Following these comments, Cage is back with his latest performance and is once again facing negative attention. The sports biopic, Madden, which stars Cage as the titular voice of football alongside fellow Oscar winner Christian Bale as Oakland Raiders owner Al Davis, is being torn apart by critics. The Prime Video release, which is also in limited theaters, has earned just 13% on review aggregator Rotten Tomatoes at the time of writing. This is Cage's second-lowest score for a film on the review site this decade. The film is directed by five-time Academy Award nominee David O. Russell, based on a script he co-wrote with Cambron Clark.

"Madden is so desperate to be loved that, unlike Madden himself, it can’t be respected," wrote one critic in a particularly scathing review, with others calling the film "vapid, unfunny, and clunky," "pure sawdust," and "led by the kind of stunt acting from both principals that never lets you forget they are giving a performance." For ScreenRant, Matthew Rudoy was more positive, awarding a 6/10 score and calling the film "a surprisingly heartfelt biopic."

Madden is available to stream on Prime Video. Stay tuned to Collider for all the latest movie news.

Chelsea must toughen up against Leeds – or risk being ‘bullied’ again

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Chelsea were exposed by a traditional No9 in their last outing at Sunderland and Dominic Calvert-Lewin could do the same

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Chelsea have a No 9 problem at both ends of the pitch. Up top, Xabi Alonso is struggling to find a solution in the absence of Joao Pedro, who has still not returned to training after weeks out with a thigh problem. And at the back, Alonso’s porous defence appears fallible in the face of the old-school striker’s revival.

Defeat at Sunderland brought accusations that Chelsea’s defence was susceptible to being “bullied”. The primary bully, in this case, was frontman Brian Brobbey, one of the Premier League’s most imposing units. His overpowering of Levi Colwill led to Sunderland’s controversial penalty that proved the difference two weeks ago.

Alonso didn’t agree with such a critique at the time. "Not bullied, I would say that it was a situation that we could have defended better,” he said after the match at the Stadium of Light. But amid Chelsea’s frantic form, questions are being asked whether Alonso’s back line can handle the Premier League at its most physical.

In this regard, Saturday’s test of Leeds provides the ultimate test. There are few teams in the top-flight as physical as Leeds, even if Daniel Farke may sometimes opt to play down his players as “butterflies”, like he did for Jayden Bogle after the right-back avoided a red card for a challenge on Tottenham’s Mathys Tel.

Leeds have won the most duels in the Premier League this season; Farke’s players are not scared of going in for a challenge. That has made them a particularly difficult team to score against, conceding a mere eight goals so far. Chelsea have let in double that tally, with only Arsenal and Brighton conceding fewer than the Whites.

Alonso isn’t only learning of English football’s physical requirement now; his five seasons at Liverpool would have taught him as such. And he knows that if his team continue to be beaten in this department, it will repeatedly cost them in this league regardless of the opposition. “It’s the Premier League. It’s not new,” Alonso said. “It’s not just against Leeds. It will happen next week against Forest, and the next one against Palace. This is the level of the competition.”

Ever the tactician, Alonso does not put full emphasis on brute physicality – even if it is a necessary step to unlocking quality. He continued: “It’s not just the duels – it’s about a football thing or tactical thing. When you have good control, normally you are in better positions to press and duel. When you don’t have that much control, you aren’t in that good position. It’s not just ‘one v one, let’s see what happens’. No, you can help situations to be stronger in duels. After, it’s the individual qualities that help.”

Leeds nevertheless appeared to lean into the criticism in the build-up to their trip down south. They posted a compilation of their best moments against the Blues from over the years, which included its fair share of crunching tackles and Chelsea mistakes. Whether a statement of intent or merely mind games, Alonso said his side was only focusing on themselves. “We focus on not being soft tomorrow and being competitive,” he said. “That’s our goal.”

Like Sunderland, Leeds’ main threat comes in the form of a traditional No 9. Dominic Calvert-Lewin may have been studying tape of Chelsea’s last outing and licking his lips this week, knowing that he has the capacity to cause the same sort of problems as Brobbey.

“We prepare knowing that we have prepared because it’s a player with different qualities but the same kind of profile,” Alonso said. “It’s nothing new. It’s quite common in the Premier League that they have a good No 9.

“Calvert-Lewin is doing well. He played with England. But it is up to us, how we defend, how we compete in those situations, how we attack the last line, how we attack their defenders. The preparation has, as always, we think been good. We are ready for tomorrow.”

Back in fashion, the target man profile is giving Chelsea’s young defence trouble. While Alonso’s arrival at Chelsea coincided with the move away from their chaos of youth, his back line remains fresh-faced. His five first-team centre-backs have an average age of 23, with Maxence Lacroix the oldest at 26. They all came into senior football with the old-school No 9 being just that, and the leap back in time has forced them to adapt.

So far, the learning curve has been apparent. But Alonso doesn’t believe his players lack the tools to deal with what used to be the norm up top.

Asked if centre-backs have forgotten how to defend the traditional No 9, Alonso replied: “No, I don’t think forgotten. But it’s demanding to play against No 9s. They have good movement in the box, out the box. But this is the demand. You can ask the full-backs, they have to play against great wingers in one v one situations. But I don’t think it’s been forgotten.”

Alonso is personally happy to see the revival of the No 9. “It’s good they are back, these type of players,” he said. Whether he still feels the same after the visit of Calvert-Lewin and the rest of Leeds’ hardmen may depend on his players toughening up to the task.

Don’t want to pay $80 for Grand Theft Auto 6? Don’t have a console to play it on? There are alternatives. Here are three games you can play for free on PC this weekned.

Let’s start with Crusader Kings 3. It’s a grand strategy game in which you leverage diplomacy, marriage, personal development, and economic expansion to become a dominant imperial power in the Middle Ages. Whether through statecraft or scheming, you’re basically doing fantasy football for geopolitics while navigating your character’s personal legacy and lore like you might in a visual novel. It’s pretty good! And easy to lose your weekend to. It’s free to play on Steam from now until November 23.

Then there’s Mistfall Hunter. This one is also medieval but decidedly less well-received. It’s a dark fantasy extraction ARPG with both co-op and PvP multiplayer. Unlike most of the extraction games out there, which are shooters, this one is melee-focused. It launched to a 55-percent positive Steam review rating but has been slowly improving. Players have praised the combat and upgrade system despite lots of complaints about balance and server issues. “It be good,” one Steam player wrote today in the review section. “imo its not fun,” wrote another. It’s normally $25 but currently free to play until November 23.

Finally, we have Mark of the Deep, aâwait for itâpirate-themed, isometric soulslike metroidvania. You’re shipwrecked on a magical island searching for your crew while fighting skeletons, monsters, and other dark creatures. It’s gesturing in the direction of games like Death’s Door and Tunic, but sounds like it doesn’t reach the same highs and has been criticized for its arbitrary difficulty and occasional movement bugs. The good news is there’s no stamina bar, so you can dodge-roll to you heart’s content. It’s free to claim from the Epic Games Store until November 26.

PSG: Ferran Torres, a shortage which is seriously starting to worry

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

There are evenings which only accentuate doubts. The one experienced by Ferran Torres against Troyes on Saturday evening is undoubtedly one of them. In a meeting where Paris Saint-Germain ended up finding the fault to win (1-0), the Spanish striker, aligned in a right winger role, once again went through the debates without managing to be sufficiently decisive. However, there was no shortage of opportunities and opportunities to find the net presented themselves to him. But as too often in recent weeks, the last gesture was lacking. A new frustrating performance which extends a worrying period of scarcity for a player whose offensive efficiency is nevertheless one of the main missions.

The Trojan evening did not allow Ferran Torres to smile again. From the 38th minute of play, the Spaniard came up against Hillel Konaté in a tight angle, before missing two new opportunities just before the break, in the 45th and 45th+2. In the second half, the scenario repeated itself: served by Fabián Ruiz in the 67th minute, he again saw the Trojan goalkeeper reject his attempt. Four clear chances, four failures and an exit in the 70th minute, replaced by Quentin Ndjantou. The contrast is striking with his Parisian debut: after scoring seven goals in his first six appearances, Torres is now going through a series of eight games without scoring.

As a reminder, his last goal dates back to September 20, during the victory against Olympique de Marseille in Ligue 1. In the Champions League, he has not found the net since his hat-trick against Slovan Bratislava. Faced with growing questions, Luis Enrique however chose to defend his attacker. Asked at a press conference about his difficulties, the Parisian coach, in fact, wanted to put this less good period into perspective: “everyone was concerned about Mika Godts, now it’s Ferran. These are attackers who miss and score goals.. A protective speech which reflects the technician’s desire not to publicly overwhelm his player.

In the mixed zone, Lucas Beraldo also recalled the importance of solidarity within the Parisian locker room, without wanting to dwell on the individual case of his teammate. “Torres? As I said, every match is different, tonight we played against a very good team, we managed to score and win and that’s the most important thing. At PSG, we are a family, in good times and in bad times.. Valuable support for the Spaniard, who will now have to quickly regain his effectiveness. Because if Luis Enrique continues to place his trust in him, the missed opportunities begin to accumulate and the shortage becomes difficult to ignore.

Where the 49ers need to improve defensively vs. Vikings

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Last weekend was not a good day for the San Francisco 49ers defense, as they gave up 40 points on the Dallas Cowboys, yet still managed to come away with the 46-40 victory.

San Francisco's defense struggled on all fronts. The secondary especially had its issues, as Dallas's top three receivers all had quality games. George Pickens had 11 catches for 145 yards and three touchdowns, and that was with him dropping three passes. CeeDee Lamb had seven catches for 72 yards and a touchdown, while Ryan Flournoy had six catches for 71 yards and a touchdown.

Cornerbacks Deommodore Lenoir, Renardo Green, and Upton Stout entered the game banged up, and it showed, as both had their fair share of struggles against Dallas's elite offense. Of course, the matchup wasn't easy facing a high-caliber quarterback with two Pro Bowl receivers, but it was a rough day for a defense that had been quite good over the first half of the season.

That wasn't the only issue. The 49ers pass rush was also ineffective, failing to sack Dak Prescott, and Nick Bosa came out this week and took accountability for his play this season. Bosa has yet to record a sack, although his other numbers still indicate a productive season, especially with the double teams and chips he's facing.

Now, this week, the health is looking better. Stout remained limited in practice with his ongoing shoulder injury and is questionable, but Lenoir wasn't on the injury report and Renardo Green practiced in full on Thursday and is off the injury report for Sunday's game.

But the 49ers still have a tough challenge against the Minnesota Vikings, who put up 30 points on the Green Bay Packers in Kirk Cousins's return. Minnesota's offense suddenly looks formidable, and this battle should be a tough one on both sides.

After their struggles against Dallas, where do the 49ers need to improve defensively against Minnesota? Ahead of Sunday night, defensive coordinator Raheem Morris broke down the main issues he saw from the Cowboys game and what to take from it.

"Extending drives early with some of the penalties to get those guys to go down there, particularly on the first drive [was a problem]. We gave up three, I believe, on that first one," Morris said. "We got the big penalty to get those guys down there. They got a big-time field goal kicker. A couple of big plays in the first half. The two big plays were the ones that killed us there early. Got a pretty good grip on it after that and kind of held it down. It was at some point it was like 38-19. Then that finishing moments, we didn't do a very good job of getting the finishes off. Usually, the finishes happen when you're able to generate pressure with sacks or you're able to get interceptions.

"The Cowboys did a nice job, to give them credit, of getting the ball out of their hand. Did a nice job of getting the ball into their good players' hand, keeping drives going, extending drives. They had about three or four drives there where it was almost like overtime-like play for them, where it was going to be fourth down territory regardless and they were able to capitalize and go down and score on us. You would like to be able to stop some of those plays and be able to get those guys going and get off the grass at those moments and then that score wouldn't have got to where it was, but those things didn't happen.

"We didn't execute that way. And it's a collective group. I know [Nick] Bosa stood up in front of you guys and took all the credit and took all the accountability, but I really think it's just all of us. Some of it could be better play calls for them. Some of it could be better for us, what we can do for those guys. Some of it can be better plays made by us. Some of it, we just ran out a little bit of gas, got a little bit tired. Let's not kid ourselves. Human nature as well. So, fortunate to get out of there with a win. Definitely want to learn those lessons when you get a chance to do those things in those moments, when you get wins. Something that we can learn from and grow from and something that we definitely will."

One thing Morris did see from his team was accountability after the game, with the leaders on the team stepping up.

"They're such an accountable group," Morris added. "Most guys, you come in and you're just upset after a game. These guys, they hold themselves to such a high standard. They come in, they're able to speak very clearly right off the bat. So it's not going to be more vocal the next week because they were vocal the first week. Not necessarily Bosa. Bosa's kind of a to-himself guy, but for a guy like Fred [Warner], he's always going to speak up and do the things that happened. It wasn't like there was mistakes. They did some better job executing. There was a few mistakes. There's always going to be mistakes and there's always correcting mistakes. I'm not saying there's not mistakes, but there's always going to be correcting mistakes, but you correct those mistakes, you move on.

"There's some other things that, just you talking to as an individual player. The best thing about our guys, they always point at themselves first. Whether it's Fred, whether it's Bosa, whether it's DMo (Deommodore Lenoir), Renardo [Green], anybody on our football team. That's the cool part about being here with these guys is they point at themselves first. Then a lot of the times I'm able to say, 'Hey, I can help you here' or 'we can do these things better. We can make these calls. We can help you out, do a little bit better in these situations.' They are all so upfront and forthgoing with those things. That makes it really cool to talk to those guys that way."

Health seemed to be a factor last weekend, but the 49ers do need to be more disciplined and avoid the killer penalties (although some looked to be questionable calls).

But there is a lot at stake this weekend for the 49ers, as their No. 1 seed odds shift dramatically with a win or a loss. San Francisco's offense has always been up to the task, but they'll get their toughest matchup of the year with Brian Flores's defense. So, the 49ers defense will need to show up as well against this new-look Vikings offense with Kirk Cousins and execute on all three levels.

Virgil van Dijk delivers future update amid Liverpool contract uncertainty

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Virgil van Dijk is unsure where he will be playing his football next season, with his Liverpool contract due to expire in the summer.

The Dutchman, 35, has plied his trade at Anfield since 2018 and is now a two-time Premier League champion.

He will be out of contract in June, though, and has revealed in a new interview with Sky Sports that it remains unclear whether he will extend his stay. He is, however, clear that he expects to continue his playing career for several more years.

Per reports from BBC Sport, talks regarding a new deal are underway but an agreement is not close, and no resolution is expected before the new year. From January, Van Dijk will be free to sign pre-contract agreements with clubs outside England.

“The main thing for me is that I feel good and I'm looking after myself and I still have many more years in my opinion,” said Van Dijk.

“Whatever's going to happen after this season, we'll see.

“At this moment of time, if anyone says that or put out that they know what I'm going to do next season, it's untrue because at this moment of time, speaking to you, I've got no idea either.”

He continued, explaining that he will remain fully focused on on-pitch matters as he awaits clarity on his contract situation.

“But I'm relaxed with it because if you know me, I only focus on what I can control and that's at the moment the Manchester United game. Then we'll focus to the Champions League and whatever happens in the new year coming, we'll see then.

"But it's been an incredible journey, almost 10 years this summer and it's been a big part of my life and I'll forever be grateful for anything that happened over those years.”

He added, matter-of-factly: “If it's getting extended, we'll see then. If not, then I'm going to say goodbyes in the summer.”

YouTube bows out of World Cup rights race; Netflix might step up

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

YouTube was an official “Preferred Platform” for the 2026 FIFA World Cup, and it worked to make the partnership a high scorer. It tapped creators to film promo content, then brought them pitchside to film dozens of videos at the tournament’s top matches around the world.

It also exercised the broadcast rights that came along with being a Preferred Platform: By the time Spain took it all home, YouTube’s World Cup content had drawn nearly 2 billion unique viewers, 550 million of whom tuned in from their TV sets and bumped YouTube’s Nielsen numbers.

But that success apparently isn’t enough of a carrot for YouTube to go bigger and bid for TV rights to future World Cups.

Daily industry updates, straight to your inbox.

According to Atlantic sports columnist Andrew Marchand, YouTube declined to submit a bid for American broadcast rights to the 2030 and 2034 World Cups.

There were rumors that YouTube planned to go out for rights, but a source familiar with the matter confirmed to Marchand that YouTube is not tossing its hat in the ring.

It’s not alone: Apple, ESPN, NBC, and the newly formed Skydance have also declined to bid. Between their various subsidiaries, that knocks out chances of the World Cups airing on CBS, TNT Sports, Paramount+, HBO Max, and Telemundo.

So who does want the rights? Marchand reports Fox–which had the rights for 2026–is going out for them. But, perhaps surprisingly, the other top frontrunner seems to be Netflix.

Or maybe it’s not so surprising–Netflix already has rights to the 2027 Women’s World Cup, and we know it’s willing to pay big for exclusive sporting events, so maybe the men’s Cup is exactly the complement it’s looking for.

We don’t know what the winner will end up paying here, but Fox paid $485 million to air the 2026 tourney in the U.S. Netflix did say it expects to close out the year spending money on developing original content and investing in sports (and now it has freer cash considering its failed bid to buy Warner Bros. Discovery). The World Cup just might be one of those investments.

Brandon Ingram expected to make season debut for Clippers on Monday vs. Rockets

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Brandon Ingram is expected to make his 2026-27 season debut for the Los Angeles Clippers on Monday against the Houston Rockets, according to ESPN's Shams Charania.

Before opening training camp, the Clippers announced that Ingram had been diagnosed with a partially torn Achilles tendon in his right leg. The partial tear was discovered while the two-time All-Star underwent surgery to remove a bone spur from his right heel in May.

That heel injury caused Ingram to miss Games 6 and 7 of the Toronto Raptors' first-round playoff defeat to the Cleveland Cavaliers.

Ingram was traded by Toronto to the Clippers as part of a package including Gradey Dick, unprotected 2031 and 2033 first-round selections, a 2027 first-round swap and second-round picks in 2030 and 2033 in exchange for Kawhi Leonard.

The deal was originally agreed upon in late June and expected to become official on July 6 when the NBA's moratorium on transactions ended. However, the trade was put on hold amid the NBA's investigation into the Clippers allegedly evading the league's salary cap while signing Leonard to a three-year, $103 million free-agent contract in July 2019.

Both the Clippers and Raptors agreed that the trade wouldn't be completed until the league's probe was completed. The deal finally went through in mid-September, approximately two weeks before NBA teams opened their training camps.

The Clippers knew about Ingram's Achilles tear — repaired in the same procedure that removed his bone spur — before agreeing to the trade, according to interim president of basketball operations Trent Redden. Redden told reporters that Ingram would require about six fewer months of rehab with a partial tear and expected he would play for the Clippers this season.

Ingram averaged 21.5 points, 5.6 rebounds and 3.7 assists for the Raptors last season after being acquired from the New Orleans Pelicans in early 2025. Shortly after joining Toronto, he signed a three-year, $120 million contract extension.

During his 10 NBA seasons, Ingram has averaged 19.8 points, 5.2 rebounds and 4.2 assists while shooting 37% on 3-pointers. He was the Los Angeles Lakers' 2016 first-round pick (No. 2 overall) out of Duke. Ingram played three seasons for the Lakers before being traded to New Orleans as part of a three-team deal that sent Anthony Davis to L.A.

Without Ingram, the Clippers are off to a 4-10 start that currently has them as the third-worst team in the Western Conference. The team has also been without Bradley Beal (knee) and Jordan Miller (shoulder) during the opening weeks of the season.

Newcastle United are reportedly facing questions over their recruitment strategy after Matthias Jaissle recommended Pierre-Emile Hojbjerg, only for the club to pursue and sign Ederson Gonzalez instead.

The Magpies have been looking to strengthen their midfield options, with greater physicality, experience and tactical discipline becoming key considerations for their recruitment team.

According to a Daily Mail report, Jaissle identified Hojbjerg as a suitable addition and recommended the experienced Danish international to Newcastle. However, the club ultimately decided to move in a different direction, opting to bring Gonzalez into their squad instead.

The decision raises questions about whether Newcastle overlooked an opportunity to secure a proven midfielder who could have made an immediate difference.

Hojbjerg has considerable experience at the highest level, having previously represented clubs such as Tottenham and Marseille. His ability to dictate the tempo of matches, protect the defensive line, and contribute to build-up play makes him an attractive option for clubs seeking greater midfield stability.

The situation is particularly concerning given that Newcastle have lost influential midfielders Sandro Tonali and Bruno Guimaraes, leaving a significant gap in terms of experience and leadership.

Gonzalez arrived from Manchester City on a £60 million deal, but his performances have reportedly fallen short of expectations. Newcastle had hoped that the Spaniard would provide the quality needed to compensate for their departures, but his adaptation has not gone according to plan.

Want more CaughtOffside coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust.

Jaissle is now keen to have greater influence over recruitment decisions ahead of the January transfer window. The German manager reportedly wants to bring in a creative attacking midfielder and another striker to improve his team’s options.

However, Newcastle must remain mindful of their financial restrictions, meaning the recruitment department will need to identify affordable targets who can deliver an immediate impact.

The Magpies cannot afford another expensive recruitment gamble, particularly with their summer investments yet to produce the desired results.

Shevchenko lying about drugs test claims - Mudryk

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Mykhailo Mudryk has won 28 caps for Ukraine

Mykhailo Mudryk has accused Ukraine legend Andriy Shevchenko of lying about the circumstances surrounding the winger's positive drugs test as his dispute with the country's football federation continues.

Tottenham Hotspur winger Mudryk, who is on a season-long loan from Chelsea, was suspended from November 2024 to July 2026 after testing positive for the banned substance meldonium.

In an interview on Wednesday, Mudryk said he blames the Ukrainian Association of Football (UAF) for his failed drugs test and subsequent ban, believing it entered his system while on international duty.

In response, Shevchenko, the UAF president, accused Mudryk's representatives of repeatedly asking for a UAF employee to "falsely" take the blame for his failed test.

Former Chelsea and AC Milan striker Shevchenko also said Mudryk's comments amounted to a "baseless slander against our people, who do their work honestly and professionally", and called on the player to retract his words.

In a statement on Friday, Mudryk refuted Shevchenko's accusation, saying it was a "lie" and that "neither I nor my representatives made any such request".

Mudryk has also accused the UAF of attempting to mislead his team's investigation into the reasons behind his failed test, and claims to have evidence proving it.

"My team and I repeatedly asked the UAF to help establish how I might have been exposed to meldonium and who, if anyone, might have been responsible," said Mudryk.

"In several letters to the UAF at the time, my lawyers made clear that we were seeking truthful information that could be independently verified. One of those letters was leaked to the media by the UAF earlier today and clearly shows that my lawyers wanted 'true, verifiable and reliable information'.

"Further, the UAF were repeatedly unhelpful and even attempted to mislead my team in order to distance themselves from the case. For example, UAF staff testified at my hearing that I was not using the UAF's supplements and Mr Shevchenko has repeated that assertion in his statement - but I have WhatsApp messages that prove that I was using their products during their training camps. That is, I have evidence to prove that their repeated assertions are wrong."

Mudryk also said the UAF was "repeatedly slow" to respond to requests from his team and "withheld important information and documents" from his lawyers.

BBC Sport has contacted the UAF for comment.
