Mauricio Pochettino must fix a club stuck in a constant cycle of failure. The new Man United boss has the personality but the squad is a mish-mash and where he sits as a coach is a great unknown, writes IAN LADYMAN

📅 2026-11-20  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Published: 16:51 EST, 20 November 2026 | Updated: 17:07 EST, 20 November 2026

In a soft-focus Sky TV interview that followed Mauricio Pochettino’s opening press conference on Friday, the Manchester United chief executive Omar Berrada revealed there had been a ‘buzz’ about the club since the arrival of their new manager.

Whether he was speaking metaphorically or referring to the sound of drones stealing Pochettino’s training secrets from above only he will know.

As is usually the case these days, much about the modern United remains unclear.

But what we do know after welcoming the eighth full-time manager since the days of Sir Alex Ferguson is that once again England’s biggest football club is relying on a personality lift to drag them back to their feet.

This is what happens when you get things wrong for so long, when you buy the wrong players and install executives who appear to be learning on the job. You end up hiring and firing managers, waiting for the one who finally arrives with the magic dust, the capacity to somehow create something greater than the sum of the rather wonky parts.

Listening to Pochettino here – a talented coach and a good person – was to occasionally think back to words spoken in the weeks of their arrivals on this territory by predecessors such as Ruben Amorim and Erik ten Hag.

For they too were asked about such intangibles as culture and mood and philosophy. They too were asked about more prosaic football matters such as match fitness.

It’s a conversational cycle that tells a story of recurring and systematic football failure.

Mauricio Pochettino faced reporters in his first press conference as Manchester United boss on Friday

A drone captured footage of Pochettino's first training session on Thursday

Amorim – back on a grim, foggy day exactly two years ago – was asked about Marcus Rashford and whether he had been right to spend time off in New York days before the new United manager walked through the door.

Here, Pochettino could justifiably have been asked about another forward Bryan Mbeumo spending the wee hours of Thursday morning playing the newly released Grand Theft Auto video game when he probably should have been asleep.

This is the United that Pochettino is now being asked to fix, a club where names and faces change but problems remain the same.

The Berrada interview was actually more enlightening than Pochettino’s carefully managed introduction to the wider media.

Certainly it was interesting to listen to his assertion that nine defensive players in one squad was enough. In football, as United are discovering yet again, it’s not necessarily the quantity that matters but the quality.

One point did resonate rather more deeply, though, and that was the one Berrada – once of Manchester City – made about United’s players needing a manager capable of dealing with the scrutiny of the club and who could shield some of his players from all that comes with it.

The recently-departed Michael Carrick did look rather lost on the touchline in the final tumultuous weeks of his time as manager. He didn’t look an awful lot more comfortable in front of the TV cameras either.

Bryan Mbeumo posted this picture after the midnight release of Grand Theft Auto VI

In the dressing room? Well, he lost the loyalty of his captain Bruno Fernandes towards the end and that is never a good sign.

Pochettino will do rather better at all that. The former Chelsea, PSG and Tottenham manager has a personality and a charisma that could fill an aircraft hangar. His players will arrive at Anfield for his opening game on Sunday feeling better about themselves than they have for some time.

United have hired some confidence and some authority and some experience at a time when they desperately need it, even if Berrada’s claim that they had to call the USA Federation last week to check on his release clause suggested this is an appointment made rather on the hoof.

It is, however, the impact that Pochettino can bring over time on a jumbled squad put together for six different managers over an incredible 12 years that will define him. Here, the 54-year-old undoubtedly faces challenges.

Talking this week to someone associated with Tottenham about Pochettino’s progressive seasons there between 2014 and 2019, I was reminded of the quality that the Argentinian was given to work with.

Pochettino's Spurs side harnessed the attacking power of stars including Harry Kane and Heung-min Son

The French international goalkeeper, Hugo Lloris, the Belgian central defensive partnership of Toby Alderweireld and Jan Vertonghen, England international defenders Kyle Walker, Danny Rose and Kieran Trippier and, further forward, the burgeoning attacking talents of Dele Alli, Christian Eriksen, Heung-min Son and Harry Kane.

It was a prototype Premier League football team, both in talent, age, fitness and physicality. That was why, in 2016, it almost won it.

Pochettino does not have this at Old Trafford. He doesn’t have anything like it. He has some talented players. The fact he inherits a team in the bottom three is down to Carrick’s inadequacies.

But this United squad is a mish-mash and it serves a purpose to ask how many would get in that old Spurs team we talk about.

Fernandes? Maybe. Mbeumo? Probably. That is about it and that, frankly, is where United are right now.

Pochettino has long wanted this job. It’s not a posting of convenience for him. Like others before him – men like Jose Mourinho and Louis van Gaal – he has long since coveted the cachet, the stature and the power of this remarkable football institution.

Mourinho and Van Gaal had better players than Pochettino is being handed and they both still failed, largely because they were shown to be coaches already over the top of that hill that both had worked so desperately hard to climb.

Mbeumo is one of the few United players who might have got into Pochettino's Spurs side

Where does Pochettino sit in terms of his own personal and professional growth? That is such a hard question to answer and will be utterly fundamental to how he travels in the weeks and months to come.

Sitting here fresh from the training ground and strangely seeming to question whether the glass of water in front of him was fit to drink, Pochettino declared himself to be in his coaching prime.

The proof or otherwise of that will only be revealed out on the grass. For all that he can plead valid mitigation, Pochettino was not hugely impressive in Paris or at Stamford Bridge. Before he starts to talk about dysfunctional football clubs, he may wish to take a look at his new surroundings.

Liverpool away feels like something of a free hit. Sporting in Lisbon on Wednesday less so and Brentford at home next Saturday definitely not. And so it begins. A run of 12 games in six and a half weeks that may well see Pochettino crawling into January begging for fresh legs and new players.

The new manager said here that he wishes to be across transfers and little wonder. When he looks at the mistakes made in that regard by a succession of United regimes and executives, he may indeed question what it is they put in the water in the north-west.

United fans will perhaps appreciate that one of the ‘higher ups’ at Old Trafford has finally put his head above the parapet, even if they question some of what Berrada said. That’s been overdue.

In terms of their new manager, they can be forgiven for keeping a lid on any excitement. Many of them have simply been wearied by the cycle.

Inside the rise of 'humble' Nyoni and why Iraola trusts him

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Trey Nyoni scored his first Liverpool goal earlier this month

Trey Nyoni knew this was the season when opportunity beckoned for him at Liverpool.

With Curtis Jones leaving for Inter Milan and the club deciding not to buy another midfielder, Nyoni saw the prospect of regular first-team action if he impressed new head coach Andoni Iraola in pre-season.

First impressions matter. So on the day he returned from a holiday in Morocco in June, Nyoni went from the airport to a personal training session in Leicester - where the 19-year-old grew up and where his family are still based.

Nyoni sought the help of a private football coaching operation as he targeted off-season gains before joining up again with Liverpool.

Coach Steve Stavrou and his son Emillio have also worked with players including Harvey Barnes and Haji Wright. Nyoni asked to work on line-breaking passes, getting away from pressure and improving his finishing.

"We merely supplemented the ability he's already got. He's a quiet and humble lad but knows his ability on the pitch and plays with such a confidence," Emillio Stavrou tells BBC Sport.

Under Iraola, Nyoni has thrived after a stellar pre-season, making 13 appearances already, just one shy of his total appearances last season.

Earlier this month, he scored his first goal for the club in Istanbul.

Liverpool ultimately lost to Fenerbahce in the Champions League that night and Nyoni's belief that "the night has been ruined" showed his inner competitiveness.

His first Premier League start came against last season's champions Arsenal on 1 November.

Nyoni fouled Arsenal's captain early on, leading one source to remark: "He's a nice kid but you saw that steel too with the way he went into Martin Odegaard."

Perhaps the most significant praise came from Liverpool legend Steven Gerrard, who said he couldn't see why Iraola was taking Nyoni off just before the hour mark in that game.

Iraola was managing Nyoni's workload, with the midfielder playing 85 minutes three days later in Turkey.

By now, it is clear that Iraola is unafraid to use him.

"He has to continue in the same place he has been since pre-season: being ready for every game. He is playing all the games lately - so we trust him," said Iraola.

That speaks volumes of Nyoni's rise since joining from Leicester City in 2023 and making his Liverpool debut under Jurgen Klopp as a 16-year-old.

Nyoni, whose parents have Zimbabwean heritage and support Liverpool, is in advanced talks over a new deal.

Having made his England Under-21 debut last week, this is turning into a breakout season with club and country.

Under Iroala, Nyoni has made six starts for Liverpool

In the summer window, the average spend on a player signed from another Premier League club was Â£39.4m.

Judging by Liverpool's recent signings - the likes of Alexander Isak (Â£125m), Bradley Barcola (Â£123m) and Florian Wirtz (Â£116m) - there is a sense of satisfaction at the club when the likes of Rio Ngumoha and Nyoni, who cost a fraction of those sums, emerge as first-team options for years to come.

A tribunal decided that Liverpool had to pay Chelsea up to Â£6.8m in compensation for Ngumoha. In Nyoni's case, Liverpool paid just under Â£1m in compensation to Leicester.

Nottingham Forest were linked with a Â£30m move for Nyoni in the summer. Forest denied making the bid, while Liverpool were not interested in a sale.

Iraola saw enough over pre-season to know the teenager would play a prominent part in the new campaign. He only got 237 minutes last season under Arne Slot but has already played 619 minutes this time around.

Slot saw the potential of Nyoni, who trained regularly with the first team. But, given Liverpool's struggles in 2025-26, the Dutchman was reluctant to experiment.

Under Iraola, Nyoni is finding his feet in a deeper midfield role than he had been used to, seeing more of the ball.

"I've been playing like a number six. I played there when I was young, but it's a new position for me. I've had to learn a lot in a short space of time," Nyoni told The Inside Scoop., external

The time he spent before pre-season impressed on the coaches there what a talent Nyoni possessed.

"In the drills, he'd make everything game realistic. Everything was with intent and you see a lot of what he does is with disguise, particularly with his passes," Emillio Stavrou recalls.

With two games a week now the norm for Liverpool, Iraola will continue to rotate his team.

But seeing who he names in midfield against Manchester United on Sunday will be interesting because Nyoni is showing that he can fight for a position alongside the likes of Dominik Szoboszlai, Ryan Gravenberch and Alexis Mac Allister.

Inevitably there is work to be done, particularly in terms of endurance and physicality, in order to cope with the rigours of playing consistently at the top level, but it is so far, so good for Nyoni.

Liverpool and Man Utd fans give predictions ahead of Premier League match

In a similar manner to Ngumoha's emergence last season, Liverpool were keen not to block Nyoni's pathway, and under Iraola he has been given the platform to thrive.

Liverpool are still only an injury away from looking short of options, so getting another midfielder remains a priority for next summer.

Monaco's Lamine Camara and Bournemouth's Alex Scott are two players of interest.

In the here and now, Nyoni needed this opening. He has come out of his shell and feels a lot more like a part of the squad. That quiet nature is slowly changing and that was visible in an interview he did while on England duty.

Asked how Liverpool are doing by one of the hosts, who supports Manchester United, Nyoni simply responded: "Better than United."

Deep down, there is a young man that backs himself and by all accounts, loves football. Neymar is his favourite player.

"He's obsessed with training and obsessed with getting better. The first session we had with him after he'd just got off the plane tells you about him," adds Emillio Stavrou.

Nyoni told Stavrou's little brother in the summer that he backed himself to play regularly for Liverpool.

'Future is really bright' for 'revelation' Nyoni

Where Burnley v Blackburn ranks in the EFL's top 8 fiercest rivalries

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The passion and intensity of supporters is what makes British football such a good watch, and derby games are particularly special.

There are some fantastic rivalries up and down the divisions, and whilst those in the EFL may not be able to match the media hype that the likes of Arsenal vs Tottenham or Man Utd vs Man City generate, there’s no denying that some of the tastiest derbies in Britain are outside the top flight.

FLW ranks the eight most famous footballers to have played in the Championship at some point in their career.

One of those is Blackburn vs Burnley, with the East Lancashire rivals set to meet at Turf Moor on Sunday in what will be a ferocious contest.

With that in mind, FLW has ranked the top EIGHT fiercest rivalries in the Football League…

With Bristol Rovers in League Two, and Bristol City in the Championship, this may be something of a forgotten rivalry, as the two clubs haven’t actually met since 2013.

That was a Johnstone’s Paint Trophy clash, with the Robins coming out on top at Ashton Gate, in what was a brilliant atmosphere. Things did spill over on the night, with home fans invading the pitch, but it also demonstrated how much the game meant.

Unfortunately, we could have to wait some time for the next Bristol derby, but the two clubs not playing for so long will only add to the animosity when they do meet again.

It’s a similar story between Stoke and Port Vale, although you have to go back to 2002 for the last time the two clubs met.

But, the Potters’ U21 side did face Vale in the Checkatrade Trophy back in 2018, with police criticising the ‘despicable’ behaviour of the supporters on the night.

Again, the absence of this fixture on a regular basis will only add to the hatred that develops between the two clubs, and whilst it’s another fixture that may not be played for a long time, it’s still one of the fiercest in the EFL.

The Steel City derby is another fixture that we won’t see this season, but it is a game that has been played regularly over the years, and it’s always a great occasion, whether it’s at Hillsborough or Bramall Lane.

Owls supporters won’t have fond memories of their last encounter, as their relegation was confirmed with a defeat to the Blades, in what was a very tough time for the club.

But, they’re under new ownership now, and they will be determined to get revenge for that when they do meet again, which could be as soon as next season.

Even though Wrexham have forced their way into the conversation about who is the best club in Wales, there’s no denying that the biggest rivalry is between Swansea and Cardiff.

The two clubs have a real hatred for one another, and it’s the first fixture that both sets of supporters look for, and the rivalry resumes at the end of November.

Despite relegation in the 2024/25 campaign, Cardiff hammered Swansea 3-0 in the last meeting, and with both teams doing well in the Championship, it should be a tense occasion.

Next up, we’ve gone for the East Lancashire derby, and neutrals who don’t know what this fixture is all about will get to see it on Sunday.

It’s a fixture that Burnley have ruled in the last decade or so, with Rovers having failed to beat their rivals in 11 attempts, which stretches all the way back to 2010.

But, with the Clarets having not won at home in over a year, and Blackburn in good form, they will believe that Sunday is the perfect opportunity to inflict more pain on Burnley.

The south coast derby is another fierce rivalry in the Championship, and it has already produced drama this season, with Saints beating Pompey on their home patch.

After years apart, the two sides met last season, and whilst the games weren’t the best for neutrals in terms of action, the atmosphere made them a gripping watch.

Portsmouth are having a good season so far, and they will be desperate to get one over their bitter rivals in 2027 when they meet at Fratton Park.

This is another brilliant derby in the Championship right now, with Wolves and West Brom meeting for the first time in front of fans since 2012 earlier this season.

A Fer Lopez goal was enough to give Wolves the bragging rights, with the game played out in front of an electric atmosphere at Molineux.

With Albion and Wolves in the mix for promotion, this rivalry could go up a level throughout the season, and the clash at The Hawthorns later in the season has the potential to be epic.

Will West Brom finish above Wolves this season? Let us know in the comments below.

Finally, the number one rivalry in the EFL has to be West Ham vs Millwall, and the meeting back in September was proof why this rivalry is seen as one of the best in the country.

An exciting game helped, and The Den was rocking when the Lions went two goals up, before West Ham pulled it back to nick a point.

This is another fixture that had been absent for so long, so hatred had been brewing, and most would agree that it is a great addition to the Championship.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Prediction: Aston Villa vs Sunderland

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Aston Villa welcome Sunderland to Villa Park on Saturday afternoon with both sides seeking to build momentum after the international break.

While Villa are aiming to rediscover their winning touch after four matches without a victory, Sunderland arrive buoyed by an important success over Chelsea that moved them off the foot of the Premier League table.

Aston Villa’s campaign has stalled somewhat in recent weeks after a strong spell earlier in the season.

Unai Emery’s side enjoyed a run of six wins and one draw from seven matches in all competitions, but they have since failed to win any of their last four games. Their most recent outing was a dramatic 4-4 draw against Manchester United, a result that left Emery furious after watching his team squander two separate two-goal leads and concede twice in stoppage time.

Villa’s Premier League record currently stands at three wins, four draws and three defeats from 10 matches, leaving them 10th in the table and five points adrift of the top four.

Defensive issues have become increasingly apparent. Since the beginning of October, no side has dropped more points from winning positions, while Villa have conceded in 13 of their 16 matches across all competitions this season.

There have been positives at home, however. Villa are unbeaten in their last five matches at Villa Park, winning three and drawing two, while scoring 14 goals during that period. They also boast a strong recent record against Sunderland, having lost only one of their last 12 league meetings with the Black Cats.

Amadou Onana and Matteo Ruggeri remain unavailable through injury.

Aaron Wan-Bissaka and Johan Manzambi will both need late fitness assessments, while Matty Cash is expected to miss out after suffering concussion while on international duty with Poland.

Taylor Harwood-Bellis could therefore continue at right-back after making his full Premier League debut against Manchester United.

Nicolas Jackson, John McGinn and Alysson all scored in the draw at Old Trafford and are expected to retain their places. Leon Goretzka is also competing with Joao Gomes for a midfield role alongside Boubacar Kamara.

Sunderland head to the Midlands in far better spirits after ending a difficult run with a crucial win before the international break.

Regis Le Bris’s side defeated Chelsea 1-0 at the Stadium of Light thanks to a first-half penalty from Enzo Le Fee, snapping a seven-match winless run in the Premier League.

The victory lifted Sunderland off the bottom of the table and moved them within three points of 13th place, highlighting how quickly the situation could improve with another positive result.

Despite their recent success, away form remains a major concern. The Black Cats are bottom of the away standings, having collected just one point from five road fixtures while conceding 13 goals, a total matched by no other side in the division.

Sunderland are also attempting to end a long run without success at Villa Park. Their last victory there came in January 2011, and they have failed to win any of their last seven away matches against Villa in all competitions.

Sunderland have several injury concerns heading into the match.

Captain Granit Xhaka has been ruled out with a calf injury, while striker Brian Brobbey is unavailable due to a thigh problem. Romaine Mundle, Daniel Ballard and Habib Diarra also remain sidelined.

There is better news defensively, with Omar Alderete returning from suspension and potentially replacing Luke O’Nien in central defence.

Chemsdine Talbi will hope to keep his place after making his first Premier League start of the season against Chelsea, while Wilson Isidor is expected to lead the attack. Isidor scored both home and away against Villa last season and will be looking to repeat that feat.

Aston Villa’s inability to protect leads has become a recurring problem, and Sunderland should believe they can create opportunities given the hosts’ recent defensive record.

However, the visitors face this contest with significant absences and continue to struggle away from home. While Sunderland will be encouraged by their victory over Chelsea, Villa’s strong home form and greater attacking depth should give them the upper hand.

Wayne Rooney made Wrexham & Preston North End claim - It's unlikely anytime soon

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

For the next month or so, a fair portion of the population with a love of football will be intrigued to see how Wayne Rooney fares on ITV hit show, I'm A Celebrity, Get Me Out of Here.

The 41-year-old is one of the most iconic strikers ever produced in English football, having skyrocketed to fame with his iconic goal for Everton against Arsenal in 2002.

In his playing days, Rooney became Manchester United's all-time leading scorer whilst winning several trophies and individual accolades, and after a brief return to Merseyside, found himself donning the colours of Derby County in the Championship.

The former forward then earned praise for his managerial spell at Pride Park as a youthful Rams side competed valiantly in the face of adversity, and would have survived were it not for a 21-point deduction.

The less said about Rooney's dugout spells at Birmingham City and Plymouth Argyle, the better, but during the three spells, he would have learned plenty about what it takes to succeed in one of European football's toughest divisions.

However, a claim made earlier in 2026 by the former England great is certainly looking pear-shaped at this moment in time.

Following his dismissal at Home Park in December 2024, Rooney is yet to return to management but has become a regular fixture on our TV screens with Amazon Prime, as well as his analysis on Match of the Day.

He is also a regular pundit on The Overlap with Sky Sports duo, Gary Neville and Jamie Carragher, where major talking points are discussed and analysed at length, as well as being in conversation with supporters.

Earlier this year, it was put to Rooney by Carragher as to what his 'ideal' Premier League would look like, and in doing so, the former pinpointed three clubs he would like to see make the 'big time in the coming years'.

Two of those were Wrexham and Preston North End, who both reside in the Championship right now, with the Red Dragons certainly one of the EFL's most ambitious clubs under Ryan Reynolds and Rob Mac's ownership, while North End are one of the country's most historic clubs, but have not played top-level football for 65 years.

The North Wales outfit's story has gained plenty of traction, as their Hollywood owners have invested heavily in various aspects of the club, particularly on the pitch.

With Phil Parkinson at the helm, Wrexham achieved an unprecedented three successive promotions from the National League to the Championship, before adding further depth and quality last season with the likes of Callum Doyle, Issa Kabore, Lewis O'Brien, Nathan Broadhead, Kieffer Moore and Josh Windass.

Although the club have certainly spent more than the sums of cash coming into the Racecourse Ground in player sales, the strategy very nearly worked for a fourth-straight season, but a slow start saw Parkinson's side narrowly miss out on a play-off spot.

However, there haven't been many indications that promotion will be achieved this season due to an inconsistent start, in which experienced players such as Broadhead and Windass have suffered injury misfortune, as they sit 15th in the table despite a return of four points from six against high-flying Middlesbrough and Wolves before the international break.

Harry Kane is the current greatest player in the world, but who follows him as the best English frontmen right now

Circumstances may not be overly rosey at Wrexham, but at Deepdale, they are at their lowest ebb for many years, as North End already seem doomed for League One football next season.

The Lilywhites have, in recent times, developed a knack for finishing mid-table in the Championship whilst being criticised over a lack of ambition, but that didn't appear to be the case this summer.

Although a Middle-East takeover is yet to be approved, such feats, as well as the club-record signing of Alfie Devine from Tottenham were two key reasons for optimism in Central Lancashire in pre-season.

However, a malaise which saw Preston win just six of their final 23 matches last season following a positive start continued into this campaign, with Paul Heckingbottom replaced by Deepdale cult hero, Graham Alexander, last month.

That said, the Scot is yet to turn the tide and has won just one game out of seven thus far, albeit those three points came before the international break against Southampton.

As such, Rooney's hopes for Wrexham and Preston to reach the Premier League anytime soon are in serious jeopardy, with North End appearing more likely to be overtaken by the third team he wishes extreme luck towards - Stockport County.

The Hatters have, like the Red Dragons, risen from the depths of non-league in recent seasons to have such lofty ambitions under owner, Mark Stott.

Even the exit of current Sheffield Wednesday head coach, Dave Challinor, hasn't derailed County's progress despite suffering play-off agony in the past two seasons, with Jimmy McNulty's side currently second in League One, as only a two-goal swing separates themselves and Huddersfield Town right now.

Wrexham and Preston fans, what do you make of how Wayne Rooney's prediction currently looks? Comment below!

As such, a return to the Championship for the first time in well over 20 years could restoke a minor rivalry between Stockport and Wrexham from their rise up the pyramid, but it still doesn't change how unlikely Rooney's prediction is to become anytime soon.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

New Phil Neumann update has Birmingham City fan 'massively relieved'

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Birmingham City's league-leading start to the Championship season has been the culmination of many key factors, none more so than the consistency displayed by Chris Davies' side.

A 10th-placed finish last term gave the Blues boss plenty of foundations to build from, even if the season as a whole was one of mixed fortunes in terms of both results and performance levels.

Birmingham have scored three or more goals in four of their last six matches whilst also conceding the fewest number of goals in the Championship thus far - 14.

Those within Davies' rearguard have earned plenty of praise for their contributions towards such exploits, but in the case of German centre-back Phil Neumann, it has also led to exit talk.

Neumann, 29, has been an ever-present alongside Birmingham captain, Christoph Klarer, so far this season, following on from an intriguing first campaign in English football after leaving Hannover 96.

The German youth international had previously been the subject of interest from clubs such as Sassuolo, Hull City and Borussia Mönchengladbach, but recent reports have suggested that Nottingham Forest, Fiorentina, Coventry City, Ipswich Town and the Tigers are plotting a potential January move for the towering figure.

Given the nature of modern football, Neumann has since revealed that he is aware of such speculation, but, to the delight of Blues fans, stated that his main aim is to return his current employers to the Premier League.

Liam Millar's immediate future has been debated with his Hull City contract expiring next June

"Of course I hear about those kinds of reports, but I don't pay much attention to them. I have my advisors for that, people I trust who handle those matters," he told Transfermarkt.

"My full focus is on Birmingham City and what we've set out to achieve as a team. There's nothing more important to me than doing everything I can to achieve our goals with the club.

"We have a big task ahead of us, and that's where I'm concentrating all my energy. Everything else is secondary at the moment."

Neumann's total of 12.1 defensive contributions per 90 is currently the seventh-highest figure of any Championship player and the largest amongst Birmingham's squad at present, which comes on top of his brace against Wrexham in August that sealed the first of eight victories so far for Davies' side.

Such metrics only back up the defender's commitment to the promotion cause at St. Andrew's @ Knighthead Park, and Football League World's Birmingham City fan pundit, Jason Moore, is one of many supporters who have been reassured by Neumann's words.

"I sort of expected him to have that mindset because his family's settled here, he's settled here, he's performing really well.

"I get the interest because he's performing well. But, I expected that he'd just knuckle down and focus on the club. It's a massive relief.

Blues fans, what did you make of Phil Neumann's comments? Tell us below!

"He's been an absolute rock. I honestly believe we've got the best centre-back partnership in the league. I don't think there's many that come close to potentially touching either of them," he added.

"To have him focused and just wanting to keep performing for the club and wanting to go for promotion is just a massive relief. Touchwood, he stays focused, and we can achieve great stuff this season."

Neumann may have never played top-flight football in his career so far, but with the defender in his prime years, he is very much on the cusp of earning those stripes in one form or another.

Given Birmingham's lofty short and long-term ambitions, it is no surprise that the defender wants to aid those plans whilst continuing to be valued as a first-team regular, which wouldn't necessarily be guaranteed if a January move was sanctioned.

Moore recently told FLW that the Second City outfit should only consider offers if they were between the £15m and £20m ballpark, but if Neumann continues his remarkable partnership with Klarer over the next 31 matches, there is every chance Tom Wagner could land over 10 times' as much cash.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

The EFL League One club that will wince at Bradford City, Sheffield Wednesday takeover developments

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Bradford City, Sheffield Wednesday, and Barnsley are among the Yorkshire clubs and rivals looking to make their mark in the EFL, with plenty of regional interest in League One during the 2026/27 campaign.

Of course, Rotherham United have dropped out of the division following relegation, joining York City in League Two, but Huddersfield Town and Doncaster Rovers are among the five Yorkshire representatives remaining at this level.

With those two joining Bradford City, Sheffield Wednesday, and Barnsley, that means plenty of local derbies and familiar rivalries, although the fortunes of some clubs in the region are beginning to look very different as we approach the midpoint of the campaign.

It is an exciting time for two of Yorkshire's biggest clubs, with Bradford potentially on the verge of fresh investment from a Thai investor and Sheffield Wednesday having already been rescued by David Storch and Arise Capital a few months ago.

Wednesday's takeover has brought renewed hope following an incredibly difficult period under Dejphon Chansiri, while Bradford could now be about to embark on a similarly exciting new chapter to kick on to the next level after promotion from League Two less than two years ago.

The Bantams have been linked with a potential investment that could provide the financial backing needed to take the club forward, having already established themselves as a competitive League One outfit immediately.

Both clubs have sizeable fanbases, strong stadiums and plenty of potential to grow with the right ownership in place. That is precisely what makes the situation so frustrating for Barnsley supporters.

These are two clubs right on their doorstep who could soon be moving forward, while the Tykes appear to be heading in the opposite direction and potentially down to English football's fourth tier for the first time since 1979.

Barnsley have not played in the Premier League since the 1997/98 campaign, although they have come close to returning to the second tier's top flight on several occasions, most notably reaching the semi-finals of the play-offs in 2021.

However, their decline since finishing fifth that year has been alarming. They have since finished 24th in the Championship the year after, to fourth, sixth, 12th, and 15th in League One before finding themselves second-bottom of their current division in 2026/27.

Frustration towards the club's ownership has been building for some time, with many supporters increasingly desperate for change at the top. There is only so much blame that can be laid at the doorstep of Daniel Stendel and his youthful playing squad.

Seeing Bradford potentially secure fresh investment while Wednesday have already found new owners will only intensify that feeling. Both could be given the platform to progress, while Barnsley are struggling to keep pace with even clubs of much smaller stature than them at the fourth tier currently.

The 25 top scorers in the Championship since the second tier was rebranded back in 2004/05, including Billy Sharp and Aleksandar Mitrovic.

Despite obvious envy towards Sheffield Wednesday and Bradford's situations, there are still reasons for Barnsley supporters to be optimistic, particularly with Stendel possessing a track record of getting the best out of teams and a number of promising young players to build around.

Patrick Kelly, Reyes Cleary, Luca Connell, and Vimal Yoganathan all offer potential, while Daniel Johnson and Cameron McGeehan provide some much-needed experience in the centre of the Barnsley midfield.

Will Barnsley survive this season and kick on in 2027/28? Comment below!

However, the lack of older heads elsewhere is a concern, with those two midfielders the only members of the squad aged over 26. That is not an ideal situation for a club trying to navigate a difficult campaign, particularly when leadership and experience are desperately needed.

There is certainly a young core that potential investors could be attracted to building on, but Barnsley need greater stability, stronger recruitment, and proper backing to turn that promising core into something meaningful. The foundations are not necessarily as poor as their league position suggests.

When is the Nations League draw and who could England play in quarter-finals?

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

England will be at home for the second leg of their Nations League quarter-final, which will take place in March

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

England will play either Belgium, Netherlands or Norway in the Nations League quarter-finals as Thomas Tuchel’s side look to build on their impressive victory over world champions Spain in the group stage.

The 3-2 win in Madrid, which featured a dominant performance before hanging on with 10 men, saw England top their group and they will enter Friday’s draw as one of the top seeds, along with France, Germany and Portugal.

England will play a two-legged quarter-final during the next international break in March, and will be at home for the second leg as they look to advance to the Nations League Finals, which will be staged in June and at the end of the season.

There have been reports of the Football Association expressing an interest in hosting the Nations League Finals, which feature the two semi-finals and the final. Portugal, Italy, the Netherlands and Germany have previously hosted the Finals.

The host country of the Finals is typically involved in the final four, although the Nations League could also allow the FA a test event before hosting Euro 2028 the following summer, with Scotland, Wales and the Republic of Ireland also co-hosts.

Here’s everything you need to know ahead of today’s draw

The draw will take place at 11am GMT on Friday 20 November. It will be conducted from Nyon, Uefa’s headquarters, in Switzerland.

England cannot draw Spain as they played them in Group A3

Moore eyes Kane shirt as Spurs' past and future stars meet

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Moore is Tottenham's youngest-ever Premier League player, having made his debut for the club's senior side at just 16 years old.

Cologne loanee Mikey Moore says he hopes to exchange shirts with former Tottenham team-mate Harry Kane when they face Bayern Munich in the Bundesliga on Friday.

The 19-year-old England Under-21 international is on a season-long loan from Spurs, while Three Lions captain and Ballon d'Or winner Kane left north London to join Bundesliga giants Bayern in 2023.

"I haven't actually spoken to him. When he was leaving Tottenham I was just coming into the first team so we don't really know each other too well," said Moore.

"But we've got Bayern on Friday so I'm looking forward to hopefully getting a chat with him and maybe his top."

The 19:30 GMT kick-off will be shown live on BBC Sport and iPlayer.

Kane was still at Tottenham when Moore was first invited to train with the senior squad during the 2021-22 season.

"He's been a massive idol of mine since I was really young," Moore told the Euro Leagues podcast last month.

"When I first went up to train with the first team under Antonio Conte, Harry Kane was over there, and [I saw] some finishing that [I have] never seen anything like again.

"It will be a great experience to play against him and share a pitch with him."

The pair may not have crossed paths in Germany yet but Moore did bump into the England skipper while on international duty last month.

"I'll try my best to get my hands on that shirt," Moore added. "I saw him on the last international break, actually, and went and said hello.

"Hopefully, I'll be coming back to Koln with that in my hand."

How much German has Mikey Moore learned so far?

Moore says the success of Jadon Sancho and Jude Bellingham in the Bundesliga influenced his decision to join Cologne on loan.

Moore's move came almost a decade after a 17-year-old Sancho left Manchester City to join Borussia Dortmund.

During his time in Germany, Sancho established himself as one of Europe's most exciting wingers, making more than 150 appearances for Dortmund and helping the club win the DFB-Pokal in 2021. He subsequently secured a Â£73m move to Manchester United.

Bellingham followed a similar path in 2020, leaving boyhood club Birmingham City to join Dortmund. The midfielder quickly developed into one of the most sought-after players in world football before joining Real Madrid for an initial Â£88.5m in 2023.

"Over the years you've seen so many young players come out here, and there are good examples of world-class players like Jude Bellingham and Jadon Sancho doing really well," Moore said.

"It's a really attractive place to play football, especially for a young attacker. It's a great platform to play, and that was sold to me by the people at the club and the manager.

"They've put real trust in me, and the amount of game time I've played so quickly in my Koln [Cologne] career has been nice. It's been good to have that trust from all the staff and people at the club.

"I'm already happy with my decision. Now I can really kick on, make my own story and build my own path in German football."

Although Moore only turned 19 in August, he is no stranger to stepping outside his comfort zone.

Last season, he made 47 appearances in all competitions, including the Champions League qualifying rounds, during a loan spell at Scottish Premiership side Rangers.

It was the first time Moore had left his boyhood club Tottenham, and he rose to the challenge, scoring six goals and providing two assists in 28 Scottish Premiership appearances.

The experience offered a different test to the one he now faces in Germany, but it provided valuable exposure to the demands of first-team football.

"I had options to stay in England and maybe stay somewhere a little more comfortable," Moore said.

"But the conversations came down to me picking a team, and I wanted to take myself a little bit out of my comfort zone to test myself in one of the top five leagues.

"When we were speaking, everyone said they thought it would be a really good experience for me to experience this type of football.

"It's really intense, and there are some great teams in the league.

"For me, it was a no-brainer. I made the decision early that I wanted to come out here, and now I just need to push on."

Having now found an apartment close to fellow England youth internationals Reigan Heskey and Jahmai Simpson-Pusey, who also joined the club this summer, the teenager has turned his attention to learning German.

Moore had his first lesson last month and, at his mother's request, is hoping to expand his vocabulary in the language over the coming weeks.

"I wasn't the brightest at school, so I might take a while to learn the language," Moore joked.

"At least I'll be able to learn a few words so I can order my food in German."

‘Fun to see’ former Timberwolf Karl-Anthony Towns in playoffs with Knicks: ex-coach

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

MINNEAPOLIS — Karl-Anthony Towns' old coach has seen the full journey.

Chris Finch heard all the criticism Towns constantly received during his Timberwolves tenure.

"Particularly when he started his playoff journey," Finch said before the Knicks' 102-99 win over the Timberwolves on Thursday night, "how much flak he had gotten."

Towns returned to Minnesota for the third time since the blockbuster trade with the Knicks two years ago, recording 22 points, 12 rebounds and four assists in the win. It was his first time returning as an NBA champion.

Timberwolves head coach Chris Finch speaks on the sideline as guard Bones Hyland looks on during a Nov. 16, 2026, in Minneapolis. AP Photo/Ellen Schmidt

He is no longer the oft-criticized star many saw as an underperformer and contract albatross. Towns now is playing some of the best basketball of his career after playing a central role in the Knicks' championship. He has shed a lot of the narratives that plagued him as a pro.

"It was fun to see him at the heart of it, too," Finch said. "Especially as they were kind of tweaking their offense going along and all of his skills came to the surface through that."

Towns called it "bittersweet" returning as a champion, having wanted to deliver a title to the organization with which he spent his first nine years.

Karl-Anthony Towns shoots over center Rudy Gobert during the Knicks' 102-99 win over the Timberwolves on Nov. 19, 2026, in Minneapolis. AP Photo/Ellen Schmidt

"I still have so much love for this place," Towns said. "It doesn't go away. I think it's actually grown as I've been away. I think that even for me and my dad, we were just driving around the city and just going to places. We went to eat at a spot we love to go here. It always hits home. This is the last place that I got to play basketball with my mom. I got to see the kids be young before now. Jolani, my niece, who was in my Rookie of the Year photo actually in here, she was a baby, and now she's in high school. It's a different part of life, but it's a part of life that I really enjoy."

Head coach Mike Brown said his team's hustle allowed it to escape with a win.

"The thing that saved us was winning the 50-50 ballgame," Brown said. "We were 13-for-14 in that area, and that was huge for us because we had trouble scoring in the fourth."

Forget Bahoya: Daniel James Set to Push for January Leeds Exit

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Leeds United have had a mixed start to their season in the Premier League, and Daniel Farke has made some strange decisions in the eyes of the fans.

The Whites claimed a 1-0 win over Tottenham Hotspur just before the international break to move the team up to ninth in the table, as they snapped a run of just one win in their previous nine fixtures against the north London club.

Despite their healthy position in the Premier League table, Leeds have won just three of their ten games so far this season in the top-flight, and have scored just 10 goals. But the manager has continued to persist with his safety-first style of football, leaving a number of attacking talent out of the side to ensure there is more defensive stability.

Dominic Calvert-Lewin continues to be the focal point of the attack, and has rewarded the manager with a healthy return of four goals in those 10 games, while his strike partner has often been Noah Okafor, who has scored twice himself.

But aside from those two players, everyone else is more defensive minded. A midfield three usually consisting of Ethan Ampadu, Ao Tanaka, and Anton Stach has produced just one goal and one assist combined this season, while the back five is made up of traditional defenders.

There has also been frustration at a lack of opportunities for summer signing Jean-Matteo Bahoya, who has featured for just 11 minutes this season, despite arriving as a highly-rated and skilful attacker. But while there has been speculation that he could find his spell at Elland Road being cut short, despite an option to make the move permanent next summer, he isn't alone in being ostracised from the side.

Fellow winger Daniel James had been a regular under Farke for much of his Leeds career, but this season he has been very much a bit-part player.

The Wales international has featured for just 117 minutes across four appearances this season, making just one start in the Premier League. As expected, that means he is yet to contribute to a goal or assist, and there is believed to be growing frustration at his situation, despite having signed a new contract last year.

Speaking to Football Insider, expert Mick Brown revealed that the former Man Utd winger could push for a move away in January as a result of a lack of opportunities.

"Daniel James is in a bit of a strange position at Leeds, it's hard to know where he stands," he said.

"He is a valuable asset to have coming off the bench, because he's quick, he's direct, he can put defenders under pressure, and he's good to have when you're chasing a game.

"But despite all of those things I've said, notice there's nothing there about his quality as a player, and that's where the doubts are.

"Physically, he's great, he offers a lot in that department, but his actual skill level has always been in doubt and I'm still not convinced he's a Premier League player in that respect.

"He's done well at Leeds, he's played an important role for them, but now he's not starting games and it looks like they're moving to a level where he won't be needed.

"From his point of view, he is not happy with his role, he wants to be starting games at this stage of his career, and he doesn't just want to be sitting on the bench and coming on every now and then.

"So there is every chance that, if things don't change soon, he could push for a move away because that's what it might take for him to move on."

While James was a key figure in the team's promotion to the top flight, his performances in the Premier League have been far from the level needed or expected.

His pace is a clear weapon to have in the squad, but he isn't a wing-back and he doesn't have the technical quality or physical attributes to be able to play centrally. He currently finds himself behind Harry Wilson, Brenden Aaronson, Lukas Nmecha, Okafor and Calvert-Lewin in those roles too, which makes keeping him around a bit pointless.

The player is clearly unhappy with his situation and wants to go, and while Farke doesn't want his squad to become weaker, the club should be looking to cash in, make some money from him, and reinvest that into the squad for a player who can actually help them.

A new striker is likely to be a priority, but if the club want to bring in a more attack-minded wing-back option that could help too. Either way, it's obvious that James hasn't got a long-term future at Elland Road, and January seems to be a perfect chance for both parties to go their separate ways.

His career has flourished since he completed a transfer away from Elland Road

6 Funniest Far Side Comics Starring the Devil

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Gary Larson’s loved sending people to Hell

In the 1980s and 1990s, nothing in the funny pages was quite as dark as The Far Side. Lucy van Pelt pulling the football away from Charlie Brown as he was about to kick it is about as dark as things got in Peanuts. And in Garfield, the lazy cat’s darkest running gag also revolved around a kick — when he’d kick Odie the dog off a table.

Meanwhile, The Far Side featured jokes about hangings and murder — death was a regular punchline. Larson also liked to make light of whatever came after death. Occasionally he’d make a joke about Heaven, but he clearly found Hell far more amusing. Many Far Side comics take place in the underworld — or some specific version of it — with Satan a recurring figure in his comic strips. Few characters in The Far Side appeared more than once, but the Devil himself made many appearances.

In this comic from June 11, 1988, a pizza gets delivered to Hell and Satan is clearly unhappy about it. “First of all, this is going straight back — and I’ll just have a little chat with whoever placed the order,” says Satan. The best little detail in the comic strip is the slogan on the pizza box. It says “Jerry’s Pizza: We Deliver Anywhere” and Jerry clearly lives up to her word.

I can understand the Devil’s annoyance. Pizza is one of the greatest pleasures on Earth, which means that pizza is absolutely disqualified from being served in Hell, even though there’s a perfect-looking pizza oven literally everywhere you look.

A recurring source of humor in Gary Larson’s comic strip was to take a common phrase or idiom and interpret it as literally as possible. In one comic from 1992, he picked apart the common phrase “ride off into the sunset” by depicting a burnt-up cowboy that had literally ridden into the sun. In another comic from 1985, he played on the phrase “dressed to kill” by showing two sharks in fancy evening attire.

In this comic from 1982, Larson dissected the phrase “To Hell and back” by turning Hell into a mere tourist destination. In it, a man is showing off slides from his vacation, which include a picture of his wife standing with the Devil. Especially fun here is how happy the Devil is, while the man’s wife looks bored by the encounter.

While Hell showed up in several Far Side comics, it wasn’t always the same Hell, or at least it didn’t appear to be. Several comic strips depicted very specific, different forms of Hell, each of which was specifically designed to torture a particular resident of the underworld. For example, this comic from 1990 has the caption “Bowler’s Hell.” It shows the bowler having made an impossible-to-hit split, with the Devil saying “Whoa! Another split?... What a bummer!” Clearly, endless splits like this would be the worst possible punishment for a bowler.

If a bowler’s version of Hell is endless splits, a classical conductor's version of Hell would have to be an orchestra filled only with banjos. Larson seemingly found banjos to be obnoxious instruments as a number of his comic strips feature them in a less-than-flattering light. In another, more Earthly Far Side comic, a different conductor is horrified by the sound of a banjo in his orchestra as he shouts “Stop! Stop! What’s that sound? What’s that sound?”

Still, it seems that, for Gary Larson’s musical tastes, banjos rank higher than accordions, which received even harsher treatment.

This Far Side comic contrasts Heaven and Hell. In Heaven, you receive a harp when you die, but in Hell, the instrument given out to all new inhabitants is an accordion. I seriously wonder if any accordion or banjo players were ever offended by Gary Larson’s repeated attacks on their instruments.

In yet another specific version of Hell, dogs who have been sentenced to eternal damnation are forced to work as their arch-nemesis: mailmen. Originally, I’d assumed that this comic strip was a parody of the movie All Dogs Go to Heaven, a truly beautiful animated film directed by Don Bluth. But the years don’t add up as this comic strip comes from 1986 while All Dogs Go to Heaven is from 1989.

And while I’m not the kind of person to get into a theological debate between any two parties, I think Don Bluth is right about where dogs go in the afterlife, whereas Larson is totally wrong about any dog going to hell.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Mikey Moore sent to the 'wrong club' by Tottenham - ex-Spurs employee

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Mikey Moore would be enjoying a more beneficial season if Tottenham Hotspur had sent him on loan to Millwall.

That is according to former senior Tottenham scout, Bryan King, speaking exclusively to Football Insider.

The 19-year-old was loaned out to FC Koln instead, and despite enjoying regular minutes, is struggling to make an impact.

Moore has failed to score or assist in his opening nine games of the season, despite having found a goal and an assist against Championship side Charlton Athletic in the Carabao Cup before he left.

Tottenham hopes remain high for the winger, who will likely remain in Germany all the while he is a consistent starter for Koln this season.

The teenager scored his first goal for England's Under-21s on Monday and will hope to carry the confidence back to his club form.

A fine #YoungLions move finished off by Mikey Moore 🎯Head to the England app to watch the match live! 📲 pic.twitter.com/heT9679XJb

Harry Kane went on loan to Milwall for the second half of the 2011-12 season, featuring under the late Kenny Jackett.

The young striker scored nine goals in 27 games for the Lions, who were desperate to get him back in the following season.

Though the loan did not directly precede his breakthrough at Tottenham, it was the first taste of what he could become.

"For me, it was the wrong club. Okay, he might not have been playing in the first team [if he had stayed], but then you don't know," King told Football Insider.

"But I would have been happier for him to be working and training with the first team at Tottenham, and maybe looking for a Championship club.

"I think I've said before, his family are Millwall supporters. Millwall would have been a good little club for him on loan.

"Millwall didn't do Harry Kane any harm. It's that kind of football he needs."

FIFA rules prevent a player from appearing for more than three clubs in a single season, with Moore already having played for both Koln and Tottenham.

The Lilywhites likely have the option to terminate the deal in January, but it remains to be seen if they will.

Despite his struggles, the winger is playing regularly and gaining good experience in another league.

Given the competition at Tottenham, it is unlikely that Moore would see his return as a positive move.

West Ham United have set their sights on Sheffield United star Sydie Peck as they aim to bolster Scott Parker's squad in January.

The 22-year-old is firmly on their radar, alongside a number of other clubs who have been keeping tabs on his performances at Bramall Lane over the last 12 months.

After a strong start to the season with Sheffield United, despite the Blades' struggles, the midfielder has been tipped as one of the best players in the second tier.

Former West Ham scout Mick Brown told Football Insider that the Hammers have plans to sign top Championship players in January to aid their chances of promotion.

Peck is one of a number of players who have impressed West Ham scouts, while they have already identified a number of key areas to improve when the transfer window opens.

Sheffield United's slow start to the season has led to suggestions that their top players, including Peck, could be tempted away with West Ham poised to take advantage.

Football Insider revealed on 17 November that Sheffield United are extremely reluctant to lose Peck mid-way through the season as he will play a crucial part in their plans.

The Blades are aware of the growing interest in the 22-year-old, with Premier League clubs also keeping a close eye on his situation.

However, West Ham could make a move to test the Blades' resolve, with Parker aiming to strengthen his squad with proven Championship quality in January.

"West Ham have got to be looking to strengthen their squad in January," Brown told Football Insider.

"There are areas where they need to improve if they want to get promoted, and one good way to do that is by looking at which players are impressing at that level.

"They will be looking at the best talent in the Championship, players who are thriving in the second tier and will be capable of making that step up to the Premier League.

"Sydie Peck is the latest player who falls into that bracket, he's been impressing for Sheffield United, who haven't had the best season by any means.

"So if there's a chance he might be available because his side has struggled, I expect West Ham will be all over it, because he would tick a few boxes for them.

"He's still only young, he's got experience in the Championship, but he's got plenty of room to develop and step up to a higher level.

"They'll be keeping a close eye on his progress and his performances, and if Scott Parker feels he needs to strengthen in midfield, he's certainly one they could look at."

As previously mentioned, West Ham are far from the only club keeping tabs on Peck's progress, with a number of clubs across England tracking him closely.

The midfielder's performances have placed him firmly on the radar of Premier League clubs, who have been impressed by his displays, and could be tempted to make a move.

Football Insider revealed on 18 November that Newcastle and Sunderland are among the Premier League clubs looking to lure Peck away from Sheffield United.

Both clubs view Peck as a player with a big ceiling and feel that he could become a top-level talent in future, particularly if he makes the jump up to the Premier League.

Peck has already chipped in with four goals and two assists this season, as his reputation continues to grow in the second tier, and there is no shortage of interest.

Texans vs. Colts prediction: Houston looks to bounce back on 'TNF' vs. lowly Indy after rough Week 10 loss

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

In the Week 11 edition of "Thursday Night Football," the Houston Texans play host to the division rival Indianapolis Colts.

Houston enters this game coming off a dispiriting loss to the Cleveland Browns that dropped the Texans to just 3-6 on the season and dealt a significant blow to their playoff hopes. The Texans need to start stacking wins, and they need to do it right now if they want to make a run at a back-end postseason spot.

The Colts, meanwhile, snapped a five-game losing streak with a close win over the Miami Dolphins last week. They didn't exactly light the world on fire, though, and have the look of a team that is just playing out the string after their series of trades ahead of the deadline.

Will the Texans bounce back, or will the Colts make it two in a row? We'll find out soon enough. Before we break down the matchup, here's a look at how you can watch the game.

The Texans defense has been one of the most disappointing units in the league this season. Houston ranks 20th in yards allowed, 18th in points allowed and 22nd in EPA per play, according to Tru Media. The Texans checked in first in yards, second in scoring and third in EPA per play a year ago, and were expected to again field one of the top defenses in the league this season after returning almost all of their key pieces.

Instead, Houston has struggled to contain opposing passing attacks, repeatedly leaking on the back end despite putting pressure on quarterbacks at an above-average rate. Players like Kamari Lassiter have regressed significantly, while Reed Blankenship has been a bust as a free-agent signing.

Luckily for the Texans, they are about to face one of the most inept passing offenses in the NFL. Daniel Jones has been entirely unable to drive the ball in intermediate and deep areas of the field since returning from his Achilles tear, and it has neutered the entire aerial attack. Jones has completed a career-low 59.6% of his passes at a career-low 5.6 yards per attempt, with just seven touchdowns against a league-high 12 interceptions.

The Colts also traded away both Josh Downs and Keenan Allen last week, leaving them with Laquon Treadwell, Ashton Dulin, Anthony Gould and Deion Burks as their main wide receivers. There is not an imposing player in the bunch. The Texans should be able to shut down this passing offense with relative ease -- especially if they can get pressure on Jones, who can no longer evade the rush like he did last season. He's already been sacked more times than he was a year ago despite playing three fewer games.

The Colts have turned the dial down on Jonathan Taylor's workload in recent weeks, having him split snaps and carries with rookie Seth McGowan, including in the red zone. Taylor played 81.2% of the offensive snaps through the Colts' first six games, according to Pro-Football Reference, but is at just 66.5% of the snaps since then, with McGowan's usage ticking up.

Taylor has also not been as effective this season (career-low 4.2 yards per carry) as in recent years, which isn't surprising given the total lack of a passing game and the Colts' offensive line issues. Those issues should persist on Thursday, with both of Indianapolis' starting tackles out for this game. It's fairly difficult to run on the Texans to begin with (4.0 yards per carry allowed), and it should be even more so with the Colts in their current state.

C.J. Stroud is playing the best ball of his career, including his rookie year. Stroud checks in sixth in the NFL in EPA per dropback, according to Tru Media, despite the fact that he's been working with an injured and rotating pass-catcher corps for much of the season.

The Colts are an easy bunch to throw on, checking in 27th in the league in net yards per attempt allowed (which accounts for sack yardage), and they won't have Charvarius Ward in this game and may not have Sauce Gardner, either. Nico Collins should see favorable matchups on the outside and be able to feast all night long. The likes of Tank Dell and Xavier Hutchinson could get in the mix as well, though they tend to rotate more heavily in and out of the lineup and therefore see fewer targets than Collins.

It's also quite easy to run on Indy, which has leaked 4.8 yards per carry on the season, 29th in the NFL. The Texans, though, have been entirely unable to run the ball, with neither David Montgomery (who is set to return from injury on Thursday night) nor Woody Marks (who is out with a concussion) able to establish anything resembling success on the ground. Montgomery was averaging a mere 2.7 yards per carry before his injury, and Marks has been at only 3.7 per carry in Monty's absence.

This is likely to be a game where the Texans lean heavily on Stroud to establish a lead, then turn to the ground game to run the clock out, in what should be a relatively easy matchup given the state of the Colts' roster. So long as the Texans keep Stroud well protected early on, that strategy should be plenty successful, even if it doesn't necessarily result in a blowout win because the Texans themselves aren't that explosive of an offense.

I just don't think the Texans should be favored by 12.5 points over anybody.

'She's a Lot of Fun': GTA 6's Mandy Has PS5 Players Smitten

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

I knew when I was reviewing GTA 6 that bunny boiler Mandy was going to take social media by storm, and it was quite fun watching the web react to Chapter 1’s ‘Pipeworks’ mission as the game launched overnight.

Now it’s being played by millions, actress Lexie Shoaibi has revealed she stars as the character, who not-so subtly throws herself at Jason during a very early story mission.

I actually think this is one example of the game’s slightly more mature writing. Wait, don’t roll your eyes – just hear me out, okay?

While this whole quest is played more for laughs, it’s there to establish the strength of Jason and Lucia’s relationship, as Jason shows no signs of being tempted by Mandy’s advances.

“She’s a lot of fun,” Shoaibi said, after confirming she did voice acting and motion capture for the character. At the time of writing, her post on Instagram has received over 90k likes.

But the response on social media would suggest gamers think she’s more than a “lot of fun”.

One post by Complex, featuring a few images and the caption ‘Mandy from GTA 6’, attracted 37 million views in just a few hours. Pertinently, it’s been bookmarked 54k times.

I don’t want to analyse this character too deeply, but I think it’s the moment I realised Rockstar has really pushed the boat out here.

The sheer variety of body types, and the detail invested into them, is unprecedented really. Mandy is just one of many similarly realistic NPCs in the game.

And while the character is almost certainly a psychopath, it’s been amusing to see her go viral overnight.

Arsenal 'have ingredients' but Europe's elite on another level

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Arsenal goalkeeper Misa Rodriguez made six saves but was beaten twice in defeat by Lyon

It is only 18 months since Arsenal tasted European glory, but they are not a team on many people's list of favourites for this season's Women's Champions League.

That is because not only have the Gunners disappointed across their opening 15 matches of the season, but also the elite teams in Europe seem to have gone up another level.

In the space of seven days, Arsenal have witnessed first hand what it takes to reach those heights - and the gap they must bridge.

Last week, they were resilient at Barcelona to earn a very respectable 1-1 draw, but were fortunate the Spanish side were not clinical with their chances at Camp Nou.

On Thursday night, they were outplayed at home in losing 2-0 to an excellent Lyon side, who look on course to match or better their runner-up finish from last season.

One point from such games could be pleasing - but that only shows the work Arsenal need to do to be considered one of the top teams in Europe.

French giants Lyon outclass Arsenal in Women's Champions League

Arsenal came into Thursday's defeat by Lyon off the back of a fifth successive Women's Super League draw at Emirates Stadium against London City Lionesses.

In contrast, Lyon have been flying domestically, winning all nine of their matches, scoring 49 goals, and then not conceding any in their unbeaten Champions League run.

Such form sets the pace and it was clear at Meadow Park that they were superior to Arsenal in every department.

Vibrant Germany international Jule Brand gave them the lead midway through the first half and Melchie Dumornay sealed the win with a powerful strike in the 57th minute.

Arsenal defender Leah Williamson said the "better team won the game" and manager Renee Slegers highlighted the difference in physicality between the teams.

"Compared to last year when we played Lyon [in the semi-finals], they are even better now. They have standout individual quality," Slegers said.

"They dominated in the first half. We had solutions for the second half and had more presence. We wore them down better but it still didn't lead to open chances."

Despite playing at right-back, Jule Brand had the third most touches in the Arsenal penalty area for Lyon (five) after striker Marie-Antoinette Katoto and fellow goalscorer Melchie Dumornay

Lyon had a 3.33 expected goals ratio on Thursday, serving up 23 shots to Arsenal's seven. They created six big chances and 14 of their shots were inside the box.

Dumornay oozed quality, midfielder Lily Yohannes showed composure and Brand was very impressive both defensively and going forward for Lyon, deployed in a new position.

Those individuals have benefitted from the expertise of former Barcelona manager Jonatan Giraldez, who joined in June 2025 - the month after Arsenal won the Champions League - and has now had the time he needed to impose his style.

As a result, Lyon have gone up another level and the gap between these two teams has widened by Arsenal's drop-off this season.

Slegers also said Arsenal had "prepared all we could" for the intensity that Lyon brought in London - but it still did not have an impact.

Asked how far off Lyon they currently are, Slegers said: "It's 2-0 today and they are better than us but it doesn't mean if we were to play them again soon, this season, that it would be the same game again.

"We saw moments where we did well and we know from the last couple of years we can hurt Lyon.

"But for today, their pace and physicality helped them get too much dominance in the game and that is one of their strengths. Then it became hard for us."

Arsenal have not yet qualified for the knockout stages of the Champions League

Williamson said Arsenal "know what they need to do to be better" and that they are aware of "where the gaps are" to Europe's elite.

They are already 12 points off the pace in the Women's Super League title race and are unlikely to qualify automatically for the Champions League quarter-finals.

"The level rises year on year. Everybody cops goals - we have to try not to and we have to create," said Williamson following the defeat by Lyon.

"We're all focused and we're all together. That's the most comforting thing for me - but this has been a tough period."

It has not all been bad despite disappointing results - and context is important.

Over a gruelling seven days, they have faced European holders Barcelona, WSL high-flyers London City Lionesses and record Champions League winners Lyon.

They showed grit and resilience out in Barcelona last week - traits that have not been on show enough for Arsenal this season, but are clearly there.

Players like Williamson, striker Alessia Russo and summer signings Georgia Stanway and Ona Batlle know what it takes to compete at the highest level.

Arsenal invested in the summer transfer window and brought in proven, experienced players, but that has not translated into results - yet.

Giraldez said time with his players has helped improve Lyon and perhaps that is what Arsenal need - patience.

"I think they are changing now. They made a good game against Barcelona and it's very difficult to go there and get a point," said Giraldez.

"The game against London City was much better. It's not just about them, but also about us. Now we have improved and play better football than last year.

"I think it is a matter of time [for Arsenal]. They have a good coach and good players. In general, they have all the ingredients to have a good season."
