Huddersfield Town vs Leicester City - prediction, team news, lineups

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By Sam Varley | 19 Nov 2026 23:57

Aiming to build a lead at the top of the League One table on the return from the international break, Huddersfield Town will welcome Leicester City to the Accu Stadium on Saturday.

The hosts currently lead second-placed Stockport County on goal difference while their visitors sit 11th and four points off the top six.

Huddersfield Town return from the November international break on Saturday aiming to pick up where they left off and pull clear at the top of the League One table.

Aiming to kick on from two disappointing years in England's third tier and fight for promotion back to the Championship, following the permanent appointment of Martin Drury over the summer, they have enjoyed an impressive campaign thus far, currently sitting top having earned 29 points from their first 14 games, winning eight and only losing one of those.

That solitary defeat came in mid-September, and the Terriers have won five and drawn two of seven league outings since, having taken on Luton Town in their final game before the break on the back of four straight victories over Plymouth Argyle, Peterborough United, Wigan Athletic and Bradford City, scoring 11 goals and conceding just two across those.

Drury's men had to settle for a point, though, levelling early in the second half through Joe Taylor in a 1-1 draw before heading into a pair of cup games, firstly bowing out of the FA Cup to fourth-tier Exeter City.

They would then beat Liverpool Under-21s 4-0 in the EFL Trophy last weekend, and now with their full focus back on League One, currently ahead of Stockport County on goal difference and third-placed Oxford United by three points, Huddersfield Town will hope to continue their hot streak and put another victory on the board.

Their visitors, meanwhile, return from the break with their sights set on the playoff places and the hopes of continuing an improved run of their own.

After suffering back-to-back defeats from the Premier League to League One, and turning to Russell Martin in the summer, Leicester City endured another poor start, managing just two wins and nine in their first eight games.

The Foxes appear to have turned a corner since, though, putting three more wins and a draw on the board in their last five league outings to climb into the top half, having beaten Doncaster Rovers, Bromley and Wigan Athletic alongside an away loss to Luton Town.

Martin's men then hosted Stevenage in their final league game before the break and had to settle for a point, having twice led through Sammy Braybrooke and Kamari Doyle and been pegged back, before they recorded consecutive cup victories, getting the better of Oxford City in the FA Cup and Walsall in the EFL Trophy.

Now also focused back on their League One bid, having moved within four points of the playoff spots and five of fourth place, Leicester City will hope to get back to winning ways with the scalp of the league leaders to move closer to the frontrunners on Saturday.

Huddersfield Town form (all competitions):

Huddersfield Town will remain without injured defenders Jack Whatmough and Murray Wallace and midfielders Marcus McGuane and Herbie Kane.

Drury fielded a strong team for their EFL Trophy game last week, with star forward Joe Taylor scoring another two goals to go alongside his 10 for the season in League One, and he will continue to spearhead the attack.

Derensili Sanches Fernandes will return to join him after international duty with Cape Verde, having also shone this season with seven goals and six assists in 14 League One appearances.

Leicester City have a relatively clean bill of health, other than long-term injuries for goalkeeper Jakub Stolarczyk and defender Ben Nelson.

Martin found a somewhat settle starting XI for the most part before the break, and Warren O'Hora, Harry Souttar and Caleb Okoli are bound to continue in a back three with Will Alves and Luke Thomas operating as full-backs.

Will Smallbone, John Lundstram and 18-year-old prospect Louis Page are likely left to compete for two midfield spots, while Sammy Braybrooke and Kamari Doyle may again support front man Emil Riis, who has scored six goals in nine league games so far this term, despite competition from Liam Cullen, Wes Burns and Conor Chaplin.

Huddersfield Town possible starting lineup:

Young; Balker, Bowat, Roughan; Bronkhorst, Humphreys, Collins, Mumba; Sanches Fernandes, Taylor, Fletcher

McCarthy; O'Hora, Souttar, Okoli; Alves, Lundstram, Page, Thomas; Braybrooke, Riis, Doyle

Leicester City certainly appear to be improving and showing their class more after a tough start, but they face a tough test away at a formidable Huddersfield Town team who also boast an abundance of quality and have settled into life under Drury perfectly.

With Taylor and Sanches Fernandes both in hot streaks, we fancy the Terriers to come out on top at home in a high-quality encounter.

He's the son of former MLB player and manager Miguel Cairo, who played from 1996 to 2012 in the big leagues and got his own start with th Blue Jays.

Leyton Orient vs AFC Wimbledon - prediction, team news, lineups

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By Ben Sully | 19 Nov 2026 23:48

AFC Wimbledon will be looking to claim a second straight win when they make the short trip to Brisbane Road for Saturday's League One clash against Leyton Orient.

The O's, meanwhile, are bidding to record a fourth consecutive home victory in all competitions.

Leyton Orient head into the weekend in 14th spot after recording five wins, two draws and six defeats in their 13 league games this term.

Orient cruised to a 3-0 victory in their most recent league outing against Cambridge United at the end of October, a result that has formed part of a three-game winning run on home turf.

Their last victory at Brisbane Road took place in the FA Cup first round, which saw Idris El Mizouni net a brace in an emphatic 5-1 win over sixth-tier Harborough Town to set up a second-round tie against Port Vale.

However, Richie Wellens's side were unable to repeat that sort of success in their away clash against Barnet in the EFL Trophy, falling to a 4-2 defeat as they finished at the bottom of Group F and behind third-place Wimbledon on goal difference.

Orient will now return to the familiar surroundings of Brisbane Road, where they have won four of their last five outings, including three victories without conceding in their previous four home league games.

That represents a significant improvement from the start of the season when they lost each of their opening three home matches in the third tier.

Wimbledon have played a game more than Orient, but they are a point worse off after winning four, drawing four and losing six of their 14 League One matches.

In fact, the Dons have lost four of their previous six games in the third tier, although they did manage to end an 11-game winless run in all competitions in their most recent outing against Blackpool.

Alistair Smith's first-half proved enough to fire Wimbledon to a 1-0 victory, representing their first three-point haul since recording back-to-back wins against Bromley and Doncaster Rovers in September.

After enjoying success at Plough Lane, Johnnie Jackson's side will now make the trip to Brisbane Road for the second time this season after playing out a 1-1 draw in August's EFL Trophy encounter.

They missed out on a bonus point with a defeat in the subsequent penalty shootout, but they enjoyed greater success in January's league visit, which saw them fight back from a goal down to claim a 3-1 victory.

However, their hopes of repeating that success may be tempered by the fact they have lost six of their last eight competitive away games, including each of their last four.

Orient midfielder Ollie O’Neill will be out for around four months after undergoing an operation not long after returning from a long injury absence.

The hosts are also expected to be without the services of Nicholas Oyekunle, Demetri Mitchell and Sean Clare.

Rarmani Edmonds-Green will feature alongside Alfie Gilchrist in central defence after returning from injury to start the wins over Cambridge and Harborough.

As for the visitors, Wimbledon are unable to call upon Josef Bursik, Dan Sweeney, Dujuan Richards, Ryan Johnson and Donnell McNeilly due to injury.

Jayden Stockley will serve a one-match ban after being sent off for two bookable offences in the narrow win over Blackpool.

Aaron Sasu could move forward to replace Stockley in the frontline, which would open the door for Steven Sessegnon to feature at left wing-back.

Burton Albion vs Luton Town - prediction, team news, lineups

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By Sam Varley | 19 Nov 2026 23:48

On their return to league action after a weekend off for the international break, Luton Town will head to the Pirelli Stadium to take on Burton Albion on Saturday.

The visitors sit five points outside of the top two in the League One table in the midst of a seven-game unbeaten streak in all competitions while their hosts are 15th after consecutive league defeats.

Burton Albion head into the weekend aiming to bounce back from a pair of defeats and move back towards the top half of League One.

Aiming to build on a 17th-placed finish last time around, the Brewers enjoyed a positive start to the new term, earning 17 points from their first 13 outings courtesy of four wins, five draws and four defeats.

That was largely thanks to a solid home record, having only lost once in seven league outings at the Pirelli Stadium, most recently beating Leyton Orient 2-1, only to suffer consecutive away losses in their last two third-tier games.

A trip to Wycombe Wanderers ended in a 2-0 loss, before they followed up consecutive cup matches with a visit to MK Dons and suffered a 6-2 beating, having fallen 3-0 and 6-1 behind with goals from Emeka Adiele and Kain Adom nothing more than consolations.

Now sat 15th, but just two points off 11th-placed Leicester City, Burton Albion will bid to put those league defeats behind them and bounce back to winning ways at the Pirelli Stadium.

They face another tough test, though, as the visitors arrive aiming to continue an unbeaten run and narrow their gap to the league leaders.

In their first full season under Jack Wilshere, after falling agonisingly short in their playoff pursuit under him late last season, Luton Town currently sit fourth with 24 points on the board from 14 games.

The Hatters lost four of their first nine league games but have since bounced back in style, rebounding from the last of those with four straight wins over Barnsley, Leicester City, Doncaster Rovers and Peterborough United.

A trip to league leaders Huddersfield Town then followed, and Wilshere's men left with a creditable point from a 1-1 draw having led through Jordan Clark and been pegged back by Joe Taylor, before they hosted Forest Green Rovers in the FA Cup first round before the international break and won 2-0 with Emilio Lawrence and Clark on the scoresheet.

Now on the back of a free weekend last time out due to international call-ups, and a 3-0 EFL Trophy win over Peterborough United on Tuesday as Clark again scored alongside Nahki Wells and Devante Cole, Luton Town turn their focus back to League One and will hope to narrow their five-point gap to the top two with a fifth win in six league matches on Saturday.

Burton Albion are set to remain without Finn Delap, George Evans, Kyran Lofthouse and Caylan Vickers on Saturday due to injuries.

Matthew Dennis also missed their last game, and in his and Vickers's expected absence, Mark O'Mahony should again lead the line for the Brewers.

He should again have support from star man Charlie Webster and Kain Adom who scored against MK Dons last time out, while Kegs Chauke is a mainstay in midifeld.

Luton Town are still unable to call on the injured trio of Hakeem Odoffin, Gideon Kodua and Shayden Morris, who is yet to feature this season.

Wilshere fielded a strong starting XI for their midweek EFL Trophy tie with Peterborough and the Hatters will look similar on Saturday, with Wells continuing to lead the line having netted his ninth goal of the season in that win.

He will have support from Kasey Palmer and Jordan Clark, who also scored last time out to make it a goal in each of his last four games, while Dan Gore will hope to continue alongside midfield mainstay Liam Walsh despite Wilshere often fielding another attacking midfielder in League One action.

Addai; Araujo, Tilt, Sibbick; Borges Rodrigues, Chauke, Krubally, Adiele; Webster, O'Mahony, Adom

Shea; Jones, Andersen, Johnston, Ashby; Walsh; Richards, Clark, Donley, Palmer; Wells

Burton Albion have seen their resilient start to the season halted in recent weeks, and we see them falling short against a strong Luton team who have won six of their last seven matches in all competitions.

LSU president orders RJ Luis benched amid SEC expulsion threat

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The RJ Luis Jr. drama came to an inevitable and ignominious end — or, at least, pause — Thursday, Nov. 19, as the LSU guard is to be benched by Will Wade at the behest of LSU President Wade Rousseau after playing in one game on Wednesday, Nov. 18.

Luis, who played under the protection of a court order, scored 11 points for the Tigers in his season debut against Louisiana-Monroe, which ended in a 79-67 LSU win. Luis was added to the roster Tuesday, Nov. 17, with Rousseau saying in a statement:

"LSU is a member institution that will act in accordance with that order. The conference has confirmed it will do the same and has stated in writing that it 'will abide by the terms of the preliminary injunction while it remains in place and will not impose penalties or fines on LSU if (it) places Mr. Luis on its men's basketball roster."

'Really gross': Dan Hurley rips NCAA basketball eligibility crisis, blames coaches

Toppmeyer: Greg Sankey once ruled college football. Now, he's losing grip on SEC

In a course reversal Thursday, Rousseau said: "In my role as President of the LSU System, after further review, I have instructed LSU Men's Basketball Coach Will Wade to not play RJ Luis."

In a statement, Luis' lawyer Elliot Abrams said there were threats of expelling LSU from the SEC if Luis continued to play.

"The SEC's decision to threaten a member with expulsion if it plays an eligible student-athlete is reprehensible and seriously harms a young person who was caught in the middle of a power struggle having nothing to do with him," Abrams wrote in a statement to multiple media outlets.

Luis declared for the 2025 NBA Draft and was in the Jazz and Celtics organizations, but joined a lawsuit to get another year of eligibility after being waived before the start of the 2025 season.

Wade didn't elaborate on his decision to play Luis after Wednesday's game.

"I'm going to refer to our university's statement from yesterday," he said. "I'll be happy to answer any questions about how he played, but going to refer in terms of anything about his eligibility and rostering him to the university's statement yesterday.

"Everybody who played tonight is eligible."

LSU went through this same situation with football, when Dae'Quan Wright and Zxavian Harris were cleared to play by the courts, added to the roster, and ultimately later removed. Expulsion talks arose during that time as well.

How the Lakers' Quentin Grimes wants to maintain his hot shooting, even off the bench

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

When Lakers public address announcer Derrin Horton introduced Quentin Grimes in the starting lineup Wednesday, the easy-going Lakers guard was met with an unusually loud ovation. Dropping a career-high 47 points with 10 made three-pointers less than 24 hours before warranted such a reception.

Grimes has used a recent promotion to the starting lineup to fuel a career scoring binge. But his official place in the starting lineup could be short-lived when Austin Reaves returns from a left heel bruise that's kept him sidelined for three games. No matter if Grimes gets his name announced at the beginning of the game or comes off the bench, he wants to maintain his rediscovered rhythm.

"I feel like we all respect each other's games, we know what everybody can do," Grimes said Wednesday after he scored 20 points in the Lakers' loss to the Utah Jazz. "JJ [Redick] gives everybody credit to go out there, no matter who it is, to be themselves, so I feel like whenever we get back whole I'll still be able to keep playing how I've been playing."

Reaves, who was injured during the third quarter against Milwaukee on Nov. 11, did on-court work Wednesday morning before the Lakers' game against the Jazz, and Redick said it went "well" but didn't have any further idea about Reaves' availability for Friday's NBA Cup game against the Sacramento Kings at 7 p.m. at Crypto.com Arena.

In the three games without Reaves, Grimes has flourished, averaging 29.7 points, 6.3 rebounds and 2.7 assists while shooting 57.4% from the field and 63.3% from three-point range.

It's not just the "statement" night in which he joined Kobe Bryant as the only Lakers players to make 10 or more three-pointers in a single game. In five starts this season, Grimes is averaging 24.8 points and shooting 55.3% from three compared with 5.9 points and 24% three-point shooting off the bench. The Lakers are 4-1 in games Grimes has started.

Lakers guard Quentin Grimes, shooting over Jazz center Jaxson Hayes, scored 20 points on Wednesday. (Eric Thayer / Los Angeles Times)

Grimes credited his recent success to finding a rhythm after a slow start to the season in which he was eight-for-33 shooting from three in the first 11 games before missing two games because of a sprained right pinky. During his career, Grimes has averaged 14.3 points per start compared with 8.5 points coming off the bench with 59.6% and 57.8% true shooting percentage, respectively.

Grimes has made a career out of shape-shifting. The 25th pick in the 2021 draft once was just trying to crack the New York Knicks rotation as a three-and-D player. With Dallas, where he played briefly with Luka Doncic, and Philadelphia, he had the ball in his hands more often, jumping between the starting lineup and the bench.

Now with two dynamic scorers in Doncic and Reaves, the Lakers are searching for new ways to let Grimes shine.

"A big part is like for him if he's gonna play off-ball, he needs to feel like he's touched the ball and he feels like he's in a rhythm," Redick said. "So, again, this is a new team, and we're feeling out a bunch of stuff for each individual guy. Our job as coaches is try to maximize each player and maximize the group, and there's a learning curve to that."

Grimes, whose relationship with Redick started in 2023 when Grimes visited Redick to work out one summer but accidentally wandered into his future coach's neighbor's house in the Hamptons, has cherished the open line of communication with the coaching staff.

"It's a good problem to have: a coach that wants to get you the ball, wants to get you into a rhythm, stay in that rhythm that I've been in," Grimes said. "So I feel like it's a good thing. He's really good at what he does. We're rolling right now. So I have no doubt he'll be able to keep me in this rhythm once we all get healthy."

Read more: Swanson: One frigid night against the Jazz doesn't change the fact the Lakers are legit

Grimes was thought to be a strong contender for an opening day starting role next to Reaves and Doncic. But the Lakers started wings Matisse Thybulle and Ziaire Williams first along with center Walker Kessler, and then inserted 6-foot-8 forward Jake LaRavia in Williams' place when the Lancaster native missed three games because of a left foot injury.

Redick has said previously he liked the balance of those lineups: Reaves and Doncic for offensive punch, two perimeter, defensive-minded wings to back them up and hit open three-pointers and Kessler to anchor the middle.

The Lakers' lineup with Doncic, Reaves, Thybulle, LaRavia and Kessler still ranks second in net rating among five-man groups that have played more than 50 minutes together.

Instead of focusing on the starting unit, Redick wants to keep tinkering with lineups to find more minutes with neither Doncic nor Reaves on the court. Coaches think those moments could help other players get into the rhythm on their own so that during the second half when the staff is deciding which lineup will finish the game, everyone will be in a good flow.

"I think with this group and the way this group [is], it's just an egoless group," Redick said. "We're going to be able to be flexible with the starting lineup and flexible with matchups."

This story originally appeared in Los Angeles Times.

​Women’s Super League predictions: Arsenal, Chelsea and Man City backed as big favourites on MD11

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

The 2026-27 Women's Super League season is already 10 games in, and both ends of the table are taking shape.

Charlton Athletic remain without a point to their name at the foot of the standings, but above them, Birmingham City, Crystal Palace, Brighton and Liverpool are all clustered within five points of each other.

At the summit, Chelsea sit one point above reigning champions Manchester City, while London City Lionesses are flying high in third place.

Arsenal have a lot of ground to make up, but they are made the biggest favourites for MD11 when they host West Ham this weekend.

Chelsea face Liverpool, and Man City take on Tottenham in some intriguing matchups. Here, we use Opta insights and the supercomputer to look ahead to the WSL action.

It's heating up at the top of the #BarclaysWSL table https://t.co/weclTxN0Gy

— Barclays Women's Super League (@BarclaysWSL) November 16, 2026

Chelsea have won 17 games against Liverpool in the WSL (D4 L5), their joint-most victories over any one side in the competition (also 17 wins v Everton).

And Opta's model is forecasting another Chelsea victory at St Helens Stadium, with the Blues handed a whopping 70.4% chance of victory.

That makes them the most favoured away team on MD11 by some distance, with Liverpool's 13.1% win probability coming in higher than only two other sides.

Liverpool have won only five of their 26 WSL meetings with Chelsea (D4 L17), though each of the most recent four of those have come at home, most recently in May 2024 (4-3).

Chelsea have won each of their last six WSL away games, only once before having a longer winning run on the road in the competition, eight straight wins between August 2015 and September 2016.

The Blues' expected goal difference of +25.3 is more than twice that of any other side in the WSL this term, with London City next on +11.7. Sonia Bompastor's team have both attempted the most shots (228) and faced the fewest (72), taking more than three for every shot allowed.

It feels unfair to label London City as a surprise package this season, given the investment into their squad this past summer. Can a team with a Ballon d'Or holder in their ranks really be considered a surprise package?

Putellas has created 18 chances, provided one assist and scored three goals already in the WSL, and her form has been integral to London City sitting in third place ahead of hosting Crystal Palace.

London City are the clear favourites, with a win likelihood of 67.2% to Palace's 14.8%. There is an 18% chance of a draw.

Palace have, however, won their last two away league games against London City without conceding (1-0 in 2022-23, 2-0 in 2023-24), though they have already lost away to the Lionesses in this season's Players Cup (2-4).

But Palace are winless in their last 14 away games in the WSL (D5 L9), since a 2-0 victory at Leicester City in October 2024. Having drawn each of their first three away matches this season, the Eagles have lost both of their last two.

London City have won six of their last seven WSL home games (L1), scoring at least twice in every victory.

Mary Earps at full stretch #BarclaysWSL | @LC_Lionesses https://t.co/sFSOanUDhk

— Barclays Women's Super League (@BarclaysWSL) November 15, 2026

Manchester United are fourth, eight points behind leaders Chelsea, and they are the favourites to take three points against next-to-bottom Birmingham.

The Red Devils have a 60.1% win probability, while Birmingham have a 19.2% chance.

After a difficult start under new boss Eva Olid, United are unbeaten in their last eight WSL games (W5 D3), having lost their opening two this season.

The Red Devils have conceded just four goals during this run, compared with seven in those first two matches, and have kept a clean sheet in each of their last three.

Ella Toone has scored (three goals) or assisted (two assists) in all four of United's WSL games against Birmingham. No fixture has been played more often in the competition with the same player registering a goal involvement in every meeting.

Spurs are unbeaten in three league games, and that form has taken them to fifth in the standings.

However, they are not given much of a chance against Manchester City, who have a 74% win probability. Spurs come out on top in just 10.8% of the supercomputer's simulations.

City have won 12 of their 13 WSL games against Tottenham, with the exception being a 2-1 home defeat in September 2021.

And Spurs have lost six of their last seven away matches against City in all competitions (W1), averaging over four goals conceded per defeat (26 in six).

City have won each of their last 17 WSL home games, including all 10 in 2026; only Chelsea have ever won more home matches in a calendar year, winning 11 in both 2022 and 2023.

Double digits in Europe Bunny Shaw makes more history after her goal against BK Häcken! https://t.co/2nKTU104bK

— Manchester City Women (@ManCityWomen) November 19, 2026

Charlton Athletic are only the third side to lose their opening 10 games of a WSL season, after Doncaster Rovers Belles in 2016 and Yeovil Town in 2017-18. Those teams did not collect their first points until their 16th and 13th matches, respectively.

And while the supercomputer expects it to be close between Charlton and Aston Villa, the Villans are anticipated to take the points (42.6% to Charlton's 32%). There is a 25.4% probability of a draw.

Villa have taken four points from their last two league away games (W1 D1), twice as many as across their previous five (D2 L3); the Villans have lost just one of their five matches on the road this season (W1 D3).

Charlton have conceded nine set-piece goals in the WSL this season, at least three more than any other side and accounting for 30% of their goals against (9/30). Six have come from corners, also the most in the division, while the Addicks are yet to score via one themselves.

Everton sit eighth on the back of three straight draws, but those all came against teams above them in the standings (Spurs, Chelsea and Man Utd).

They now host Brighton. Everton have never drawn four consecutive games in the competition, with their only other run of three coming in their first three WSL fixtures in April 2011.

The Toffees are the favourites, with a 43.5% win probability, but at 26.8% this is the game that is most likely to be a draw, with Brighton's chances ranked at 29.7%.

Everton have kept a clean sheet in three of their last five WSL home games, as often as across their 23 home matches prior; the Toffees themselves could go goalless in back-to-back home matches in the league for the first time since November 2023, however.

Between the 2018-19 and 2023-24 seasons, the head-to-head record between Everton and Brighton in the WSL was perfectly balanced with four wins each (D4) – since then, however, the Seagulls have won all four of their top-flight meetings with the Toffees.

Everton surpassed 250 @BarclaysWSL matches on Saturday – the Toffees being only the third team to reach the milestone. If you know your history. https://t.co/i5VfwXUf6n

— Everton Women (@EvertonWomen) November 16, 2026

The most likely winners on MD11 are Arsenal (76.1%), as they host West Ham, who are handed just a 9.7% win probability.

With just three wins from their opening 10 matches (D5 L2), Arsenal have recorded their fewest victories at this stage of a season. Their 14 points are also their joint-lowest tally after 10 games, alongside 2014.

West Ham have won three of their last five WSL games (L2), having won just one of their opening five this season (L4); the Hammers' four victories are already three more than they had recorded at the same stage last term (one).

Arsenal are the first team to draw each of their opening five home games of a WSL season, with the last four all finishing 1-1. The Gunners are nevertheless unbeaten in their last 24 home matches in the competition (W16 D8), since a defeat to Chelsea in October 2024.

But they will now be looking to bounce back from a disappointing 2-0 home loss to Lyon in the Women's Champions League on Thursday.

The Gunners have won all eight of their WSL home games against West Ham by an aggregate score of 27-8, always scoring at least twice in those matches.

Manchester United is heading towards Mauricio Pochettino!

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Manchester United are expected to part ways with Michael Carrick in the coming days. According to the British press, Mancunian leaders are considering sacking their coach during the international break, after having been in danger since the start of the season. Despite a spectacular draw against Aston Villa (4-4), the Red Devils remain in the relegation zone and have only two victories in ten Premier League days. A situation which considerably weakens Carrick, while the club would like to quickly find a new technician to turn things around.

Appointed in January to replace Ruben Amorim before receiving a two-year contract in May, he could therefore already be living his final hours on the Mancunian bench. After leading his team to third place last season and qualification for the Champions League, the former midfielder is going through a much more complicated period. Asked about a possible resignation after the draw against Aston Villa, he was categorical: “it won’t happen”. But according to the Daily MailManchester United would like to install their new coach before the trip to Anfield on November 22.

In this perspective, Mauricio Pochettino now stands out as the big favorite to succeed him. Currently coach of the United States, the Argentinian could be released thanks to a clause set at more than 8 million euros. His experience in the Premier League, with Tottenham, Chelsea and Southampton, constitutes a strong argument for the Mancunian leaders. According to the BBCMauricio Pochettino would be open to a transfer to Manchester United, even if he has just extended his contract with the United States because the clause would allow him to leave his role as coach if a prestigious offer arises.

Other profiles nevertheless remain under study, notably Eddie Howe and Julian Nagelsmann. The former Newcastle coach had been considered during Manchester United’s previous discussions, but he would have already refused the idea of ​​taking charge of the Red Devils. For his part, Nagelsmann is available after his departure from the German selection, while the name of Antonio Conte is still circulating. Pochettino, however, remains one step ahead in Mancunian thoughts, even if his arrival would require resolving the question of his contract with the United States.

NFL Slams Mike Florio & Chris Simms' Hitler Comments About Mike Vrabel

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The NFL admonished football commentators Mike Florio and Chris Simms after the podcast hosts described New England Patriots head coach Mike Vrabel's facial hair as a "German mustache."

The NFL is addressing new inflammatory comments.

After sports commentators Mike Florio and Chris Simms mocked coach Mike Vrabel's appearance on their Pro Football Talk Live podcast, making allusions to Nazi Germany and Adolf Hitler's mustache, the league condemned the comments.

The NFL's executive vice president Jeff Miller told Front Office Sports in a Nov. 18 statement, "We found the remarks inappropriate and offensive."

While discussing the New England Patriots' Nov. 15 loss to the Detroit Lions in Munich, Germany, Simms and Florio—who did not explicitly name Hitler—made remarks about the Patriots head coach's looks.

"He got the German mustache to go along with that right now, too," Simms—a former Denver Broncos quarterback—said during their Nov. 17 episode. "So, he was hitting all themes there over in Germany."

Meanwhile, Florio, 61, joined in, adding, "If you're in Germany, you gotta be careful trimming the sides of that thing."

As he laughed, Simms, 46, noted that Vrabel's appearance in Germany could make some pedestrians do a double-take. He continued, "Who's that on the street over there? He put on a few pounds. Hold on, he's big, holy crap."

But others on the PFL team weren't impressed with the jokes, with Florio noting that their show producer Gary Carter was heard groaning during the segment.

"It's funny, Gary," he said. "It was 80 years ago. Let us have some fun, for crying out loud."

Florio—who began the radio show in 2015—and Simms addressed the interaction following the incident.

"On yesterday's (Nov. 17) PFT Live show," they said in a joint statement on NBC Sports' website, "we made comments that some found offensive and we apologize."

NBC Sports—which distributes the podcast, though Florio retains 100 percent equity—also admonished the hosts for the comments.

"On yesterday's PFT Live show, Mike and Chris made statements that were inappropriate," a spokesperson for the sports news company told The Athletic. "They have apologized."

E! News has reached out to Vrabel's reps for comment and has not yet heard back.

MLS Cup bracket predictions: Lionel Messi's Inter Miami set for repeat?

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

It's time for some brackets as the MLS Cup playoffs are upon us. With the Wild Card round kicking off on Thursday night, 18 teams (still too many) will enter the gauntlet that is the playoffs. All eyes will be on Nashville SC, who have a first-round bye after winning the Supporters' Shield and setting a new MLS regular-season points record with 77 points. However, when it comes down to it, anyone can fall in a one-off match, as being successful in the regular season is a different proposition than doing so in the postseason.

While the Wild Card round is a one-off, win-or-go-home match, the first round is a best-of-three series with games going to a penalty shootout if tied. That can change the calculus a bit, especially for teams getting an early goal in the first game, since there are still two more chances to advance. But since there are playoffs, that also means that we have to predict the bracket, so let's get to it.

The Seattle Sounders come into this postseason in a tough spot. While they've become a team that can't be counted out of MLS Cup contention, they enter the playoffs without one of their best defenders. Center back Jackson Ragen was suspended for two games following a disciplinary review for his challenge on Los Angeles FC's Jacob Shaffelburg. While the Sounders have returned to health, this opens the door for Real Salt Lake to get out of the Wild Card round.

In Orlando, this is one of the matchups of the entire playoffs as Evander and Antoine Griezmann will battle in a match where defense is optional. Both teams had higher ambitions heading into the season, so for the loser, it could have major implications. But after winning Newcomer of the Year, Griezmann, alongside Orlando's strong attack, will prove to be too much to handle for Cincinnati as we head to the first round of the playoffs proper.

The parity in MLS this season, especially in the Western Conference, is real. You could tell me that anyone would come out of the West, and it's believable. Vancouver may be the team to beat, but needing to replace Sebastian Berhalter and Andres Cubas midseason did hurt the squad. Jesper Sorensen has done plenty to keep Vancouver afloat, but they're still vulnerable, although that'll be more of an issue in later rounds depending on how the bracket shakes out. St. Louis City SC have already exceeded expectations by winning the U.S. Open Cup, and if Alexandru Matan is on his game, they may be unbeatable, but they can run hot and cold. Add in Bruce Arena still evolving with the league and the cardiac kids of Dallas (and of course the brilliance of Petar Musa), and that brings us to the advancing sides. I would apologize to Los Angeles FC, but post-Steve Cherundolo, this is a side that has faltered, and a long look needs to be taken at the squad following the season to move forward.

In the East, we're in B.J. Callaghan's world. Los Angeles FC are the last team to do the double of winning the Supporters' Shield and MLS Cup, and it's a tough thing for Nashville to do, but this team has only allowed 25 goals in a full season. They can score, they can grind a game to a halt, and they have so many ways to give the opposition fits. In a best-of-three, good luck getting past them. Speaking of slowing a game down, the New England Revolution are a worst-case matchup for the fighting Cavan Sullivans of Philadelphia. Joshua Wynder has stabilized the defense in front of Matt Turner, and while the Revolution may not have a high ceiling as constructed, the floor is so high in this unique setup. Charlotte facing Chicago might as well be a coin flip. Then there's Miami facing Columbus. Like the Union, the Crew can win because their attacking upside is so high, and Miami are a flawed team, but Lionel Messi is still doing Lionel Messi things. As long as that's the case, I'm rarely going to bet against the Herons.

Now the playoffs really are getting underway. These are one-off games where the best of the best are going up against each other. San Jose's run comes to an end here, while St. Louis and Dallas fight in an evenly matched clash to determine who else comes out of the Western Conference. Nashville's defense again puts up a suffocating performance to get past Charlotte, and Messi drives Inter Miami past the Revolution, setting up a fascinating set of conference finals.

Okay, I swear that this wasn't supposed to go chalk with three of the top four finishers in the regular season making it to the conference finals, but getting to the end of the season and saying that the good teams are actually good shouldn't be revolutionary. Thomas Muller and Brian White are enough to push the Whitecaps to the MLS Cup final, where the Eastern Conference yet again comes down to Messi versus Hany Mukhtar. Nashville may be the better-coached team, but Miami have found a way with their backs against the wall to produce a rematch of last season's MLS Cup final.

Participants: Vancouver Whitecaps, Inter MiamiWinners: Inter Miami

Hey, it's Messi and Muller, just like we're having a top-tier Champions League matchup. It has been a playoff gauntlet, but the more things change, the more they stay the same. Muller and Messi have faced each other 11 times, with Muller claiming seven wins to Messi's four. However, after getting one over on Muller last season, Messi isn't going to let a chance at a second consecutive MLS Cup pass by, even if this one is in Vancouver amid the uncertain future of BC Place as the home of the Whitecaps.

Andriy Shevchenko hits back at explosive Mykhailo Mudryk claims in blame game over doping case: Ukraine chief accuses £89m star's camp of asking official to falsely take blame for positive drugs test

📅 2026-11-19  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By LUKE AUGUSTUS, ASSISTANT SPORTS EDITOR

Published: 16:00 EST, 19 November 2026 | Updated: 18:30 EST, 19 November 2026

Andriy Shevchenko has accused Mykhailo Mudryk's representatives of trying to persuade the Ukrainian FA to find someone to falsely take the blame for the winger's failed drugs test.

Mudryk, now on loan at Tottenham from Chelsea, was suspended for 20 months between November 2024 and July 2026 after testing positive for banned substance meldonium in October 2024.

In an interview, Mudryk blamed the Ukrainian FA (UAF), claiming the substance entered his body while on international duty.

'This drug could only have got into my body where I was, and that's Ukraine,' he said.

But Shevchenko, president of the UAF, described the accusations as 'baseless slander'. Shevchenko added: 'His representatives repeatedly approached the UAF with a proposal to find an employee who would falsely take responsibility for the meldonium found in his doping sample,' he told Tribuna. 'Such a proposal contradicts the principles of our work.'

Shevchenko said investigations had failed to establish how the substance entered Mudryk's system. He added that national team staff complied with Chelsea's request not to give Mudryk medication, and that two of his personal specialists were allowed at training camps.

Mykhailo Mudryk (left) has been accused of 'baseless slander' by Ukraine FA (UAF) president Andriy Shevchenko after he blamed his failed drugs test on the national team's setup

'If Mykhailo has evidence to back up his words, let him provide it,' Shevchenko said, demanding a public retraction and apology otherwise.

Mudryk's representatives have been approached for comment.

The 25-year-old was initially handed a four-year ban after being charged by the Football Association for failing a doping test. He was able to return when a seismic resolution was struck after changes to the 'scientific reporting standards'.

In a bombshell two-hour interview with Ukrainian journalist Dmitry Gordon via his YouTube channel, Mudryk laid the blame for his situation with the Ukraine national team - something Shevchenko vehemently denies.

'Loud statements have been made about the UAF and the Ukrainian national team. This is baseless slander against our people, who do their work honestly and professionally,' the UAF president, who also played for Chelsea during his time, said in a statement to The Athletic.

'After Mykhailo became a Chelsea player, the club sent a letter to the UAF requesting that he not be given any medication during national team training camps,' he continued.

'The national team staff complied with this request. Head coach Serhiy Rebrov also approved for Mykhailo to have two of his personal specialists work with him at the training camps. These were the exact people who monitored his regimen and recovery, and helped him with his daily routine.

Mudryk has won 28 caps for Ukraine but is yet to represent them since his ban was overturned

'The UAF completed all necessary checks in this case. We provided Mudryk's representatives with the information they requested and remained in contact with those working on the case.

'I know the people who have worked with the national team for many years. I trust them, because throughout all this time there have been no incidents in the team that could make anyone doubt their professionalism. If Mykhailo has evidence to back up his words, let him provide it. If no such evidence exists, I expect him to publicly retract his accusations and apologise to the people whose honesty and professionalism he has called into question.'

In his interview, Mudryk - who is yet to add to his 28 caps for his country since returning from his ban - laid the blame at Ukraine's door.

'I had never heard of a drug like meldonium, never seen it, never come across it in my life. I only learned about it when they told me the news. I definitely have ideas. It's a drug you can only get in Eastern Europe,' the winger said.

'This drug could only have got into my body where I was, and that's Ukraine. There are no such drugs in England. It supposedly stimulates your heart and helps you, as far as I understand, handle physical load better.

'I was at home and Chelsea's management came to see me and they said we needed to talk. Then they say you've returned a positive doping test and now you're facing a ban.

'You can't believe it's true. You can't believe this is happening to you. If I'd ever taken anything anywhere, I'd have understood that this conversation might happen one day.

He is currently on loan at Tottenham from Chelsea but has only played once so far this season

'At least I'd have understood it was possible. But when you spend a huge pile of money on watching your diet, on a nutritionist, on a chef. When you spend that much money every year on all these things, that's the last thing you expect. Because it speaks for itself: that you take things very seriously, like nutrition, everything that goes into your body, you watch it very closely.

'Otherwise there'd be no need to spend money on it, because you could manage without it. They tell you this news, and you sit there, and you kind of listen to them but don't hear them.

'At some point you just go into a trance. It feels like you're falling in a dream off a skyscraper. You're flying down, you feel yourself flying down, and only in a dream do you wake up when you hit the ground, but in real life you don't wake up, you just keep falling. It hits you very hard mentally. At that moment the tears came later.'

Mudryk, who joined Chelsea for £89million from Shakhtar Donetsk, has only made one substitute appearance for Tottenham so far this season due to an ankle injury.

Mauricio Pochettino's first Man United training session is LEAKED ahead of Liverpool clash: Drone over Carrington captures 12 MINUTES of footage in major security breach

📅 2026-11-19  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Published: 14:07 EST, 19 November 2026 | Updated: 18:48 EST, 19 November 2026

Manchester United have fallen victim to a security breach after footage of Mauricio Pochettino’s first training session as head coach was leaked online.

The 12-minute video taken by a drone hovering over the club’s Carrington training complex on Thursday clearly shows Pochettino and his coaching staff working with the players.

The incident is a major embarrassment for United on Pochettino’s first full day in charge after the club paid £10million to buy the Argentine and his staff out of their contracts with the US Soccer Federation.

His first game is against Liverpool at Anfield on Sunday, although it’s unclear how much United’s bitter rivals would be able to benefit from the video.

United regularly use their own drones to film training sessions, but put up signs six years ago banning the use of them near the training ground in case they could reveal top-secret information to their opponents.

Mauricio Pochettino's first Man United training session has been leaked on YouTube

Footage of the session was captured from a drone close to Carrington and uploaded online

Man United players were captured during a drill under the gaze of Pochettino's coaching staff

The footage was posted on YouTube by an account called CC Overview which insists that the airspace over Carrington is unrestricted and the drone was operating legally within Civil Aviation Authority rules.

It said that the video was taken half an hour into Pochettino’s training session and clearly shows the 54-year-old instructing the United squad along with his coaching team of No.2 Jesus Perez, first-team coach Miguel D’Agostino, goalkeeper coach Toni Jimenez, his son and fitness coach Sebastiano Pochettino and conditioning coach Silvia Tuya.

Pochettino only flew into Manchester on Wednesday with D’Agostino, Tuya and analyst Alec Scott after taking charge of his last game as US coach against Haiti in Cincinnati.

They caught a private jet provided by United after the game and he went straight to Carrington after landing in the UK shortly before noon.

Perez, Jimenez and Sebastiano Pochettino have been at the training ground since the weekend after being given permission by the US Soccer Federation to leave after the first-leg against Haiti in Miami.

Pochettino addressed the Man United's internal media team for the first time on Thursday, outlining how he plans to drag this underperforming squad up the table.

'The culture is the most important,' Pochettino explained.

'Create the habits, the principles, and I think that is what we want to create.

The breach comes as Man United prepare for a daunting trip to Anfield on Sunday afternoon

The 12 minutes of drone footage captured Pochettino and his staff coaching across two training pitches at Carrington

Man United installed signs in 2018 warning that the use of drones over Carrington was 'strictly prohibited' and would be reported for prosecution

'We need to [be] looking forward and to find a way to create the best habits and the culture that makes us to be close to winning every single game.'

'The players need to express their talent. We need to create the environment and the platform for the players to perform. That is the most important thing and that is our priority now – it's to work with them and try to take the pressure.'

Pochettino has stressed he is focused on the potential of his squad, rather than their present position in the Premier League.

'It's a mistake to see the [current] situation. We need to see the potential of this club,' a defiant Pochettino added.

'Of course, the circumstances are the circumstances, and we need to be responsible for the circumstances. But at the same time, we need to understand that it's a game and, to connect with the game, we need to feel free.

'The players need to express their talent. We need to create the environment and the platform for the players to perform. That is the most important thing and that is our priority now – it's to work with them and try to take the pressure [off].

'Pressure needs to bring energy, responsibility needs to bring energy. Because if not, it's too heavy to put all that pressure and responsibility on your back.

'When you connect with the game, you connect through your talent and when you feel freedom, when you feel happy, when you want to play for your team-mates, when you want to play for your family, when you want to play for your club, your fans.

'That is what we need to create all together and I think we have an amazing staff - we were talking a lot today. Then [we need to] settle all the principles and the ideas and to create our own philosophy and start from zero.

'I think with the power of this club and the fans supporting us, I think we will get in the place that we deserve to be.'

Have your say: Endrick or Tresoldi – who should Milan sign in January?

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Milan are preparing to spend big on a striker in January, but conflicting reports mean it’s unclear whether Real Madrid’s Endrick or Club Brugge’s Nicolò Tresoldi is their priority.

According to La Gazzetta dello Sport, the Rossoneri have a budget of around €70m for the winter window, and they plan to sign only one forward. Gerry Cardinale is studying deals for both.

Brazilian outlets TNT Brasil and ESPN Brasil report that Real are now open to selling Endrick permanently for €60-70m, possibly with a buy-back clause. Neither the club nor the player wants another loan. Gazzetta has described a deal worth around €60m plus bonuses, with an €80m buy-back for Real. Milan’s pitch is a starting role, and Ricardo Kaká, who recently met Cardinale, could help.

However, other reports say Cardinale has settled on Tresoldi. Brugge would prefer to sell him in the summer, and their asking price has jumped from €40m to at least €60m. Roma are also chasing Endrick, and Corriere dello Sport reported last week that the Brazilian wasn’t keen on Milan.

Endrick is the bigger name and has the higher ceiling. At 20, he’s already a Brazil international. He’s explosive, strong for his size and can play through the middle or out wide, which would give Ruben Amorim options alongside Gonçalo Ramos. The concerns are his lack of football and the price. He’s played only 141 minutes for Real this season, and a big fee with a buy-back clause attached could mean Milan pay full price for a player they might not keep. José Mourinho also wants him to stay.

Tresoldi, meanwhile, scored a Champions League hat-trick against Inter at San Siro last month, and he’s a lifelong Milan fan who calls joining the club his “dream”. He’s eligible for Italy, which matters too: Fabio Capello pointed out that Milan started only one Italian at Genoa. However, his top-level experience has come only in the Belgian league, and €60m is a steep price. Gazzetta has warned that Brugge is “an expensive shop”, which Milan already know after signing Charles De Ketelaere and Ardon Jashari from them.

So which one should Milan go for? Vote below.

How I Had to Learn to Stop Saying “Yes” When I Meant “No”

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

After years or decades, problems and patterns can become deeply entrenched. Be a fly on the wall during a sample couples therapy session and witness the slow, deliberate work of changing those patterns for a more enduring love.

Self Tests are all about you. Are you outgoing or introverted? Are you a narcissist? Does perfectionism hold you back? Find out the answers to these questions and more with Psychology Today.

Posted November 19, 2026 | Reviewed by Tyler Woods

For years, I was a girl who would say “yes” when I meant “no.” It was only after I decided to stop drinking that I realized how much my “yes-girl” tendency had fueled my drinking cycle over the years.

Over the years, both through my own recovery and my work in helping others break their drinking patterns, I have come to see that most people who eventually come to question their relationship with alcohol don’t have a “drinking problem;” instead, they have a “drinking solution.” In my case, my inability and unwillingness to say “no” had created a lot of problems that I ended up turning to alcohol to solve.

I would say yes to a football weekend marathon when I didn’t even know what a quarterback was, and then turn to a 6-pack to make time pass faster. I would say “yes” to hosting the relative whom I couldn't stand, then try to push away my resentment with a bottle of wine. I would say “yes” to helping out at a neighbor’s garage sale after a long week and end up trying to recover from exhaustion with a few cocktails.

As I started to help others break their old drinking patterns, I realized I was far from the only person who got caught in this pattern. In fact, I came to nickname this particular drinking pattern “the Yes-Effect Drinking Loop.” It’s one of the 10 most common invisible drinking loops that keep an unwanted drinking pattern alive.

In most unhealthy drinking patterns, drinking is only what we see on the surface. The Invisible Drinking Loop is a term I created to help people understand the hidden forces that keep their drinking patterns alive. An invisible drinking loop is often driven by the need to solve a problem, fueled by the belief that alcohol can help, and maintained by the habit loop in which drinking has become the default routine to cope with or deal with certain situations or emotions.

In the “Yes-Effect Drinking Loop,” drinking has become the solution to deal with the side effects that arise when a person struggles to say no.

As is true with most drinking loops, simply stopping drinking doesn’t break the loop.

With alcohol out of my life, I found myself sitting through events that I had no interest in, battling the desire to reach for a drink to make time pass faster, hanging out with people whose company I didn’t enjoy, missing the quick mental escape alcohol could offer, and dealing with overwhelming cravings by the end of the day after I once again stretched myself too thin in the effort to please others instead of honoring my own limitations.

It took me a long time to realize that simply stopping drinking doesn’t solve the problems; instead, it exposes them. I needed to learn to find another way to address the problem that I once attempted to solve through drinking, and that meant learning how to say no.

Learning to say no after years of saying yes is not easy. When faced with a choice between disappointing others and disappointing myself, my default response was to choose the latter. Somewhere along the line, I had been conditioned to believe that fulfilling others’ expectations, even those of people whom I wasn't even that close to, was a way to keep myself safe, liked, and in good standing.

Yet, I failed to see the hidden cost of all the efforts to keep others happy; I kept myself unhappy. So much so that alcohol became the only way I knew how to push down my disappointment, exhaustion, and resentment.

Learning to say “no” started with a radical decision to honor my own preferences rather than automatically prioritize others’ expectations. Don’t get me wrong, it doesn’t mean becoming self-centered or never making compromises in life. But it does mean taking an intentional pause before giving in to my compulsive need to keep others happy. And when faced with the choice between disappointing others and disappointing myself, I wanted to make that choice consciously, rather than by default.

Of course, giving myself permission to say no was one thing. Actually knowing how to say it was another. After years of automatically saying yes, saying no felt like a skill I had not yet learned.

That's why Skill Expansion is one of the core pillars of my approach to breaking an old drinking pattern. Truly growing beyond alcohol often requires us to develop new skills and tools to navigate the challenges we once relied on alcohol to handle. For the Yes-Effect Drinking Loop, learning to say no is one of those skills.

Fortunately, like most skills, saying no is something we can learn through practice. Personally, I've found that having a few ready-to-use ways to say no makes a world of difference. That's why I've put together 7 of my favorite scripts for saying no with grace.

Bella Hadid Cozies Up to Boyfriend Nathan Lacy During Aspen Trip

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Bella Hadid enjoyed a shopping trip with her boyfriend Nathan Lacy in Aspen, Colo., on Nov. 17, with the couple showing off their romance with some sweet PDA.

Bella Hadid is getting cozy for cuffing season.

A week after hard launching her new romance with Nathan Lacy, the supermodel stepped out with her boyfriend for some vintage shopping in Aspen, Colo., on Nov. 17.

And the couple didn’t hold back on showing their love. Hadid and Lacy shared loving smooches and hugs, cuddling up together as they walked down the streets browsing the shops. In one sweet moment, the supermodel leaned her head on the construction worker's shoulders while he carried their shopping bags.

And though their romance is heating up, Hadid embodied chill vibes for the outing, bundling up in a white sweater with a denim jacket and matching denim jeans. Hadid—who styled her blonde locks in two pigtail braids—accessorized her winter look with a white beanie, black boots, cream gloves and a pair of black sunglasses.

As for Lacy, he sported a cream jacket over a blue plaid shirt, which he paired with dark jeans and sneakers.

But it wasn’t just the couple who enjoyed the shopping outing as they brought along their adorable golden retriever, which trotted along as Lacy walked it on a leash.

Though Hadid and Lacy first sparked romance rumors in September while on a grocery run together, it wasn’t until last week that the Victoria’s Secret Angel appeared to confirm the romance.

In a Nov. 9 Instagram post, the 30-year-old shed light on her and Lacy’s date night at a hockey game to watch the Colorado Avalanche beat the Detroit Red Wings on Nov. 7.

“Let’s go Avs!” Hadid wrote alongside the carousel of photos, which included PDA-filled shots with Lacy. “Saturday night was especially meaningful.”

And the Orabella founder emphasized that she loved cheering on the NHL team.

“I’ve always loved hockey living in NYC,” Hadid shared. “And being a Colorado girl at heart, growing up here since I was young, I guess it was only a matter of time before I found my way to the @coloradoavalanche.”

She added, “Regardless of where you are in the world—the heart, the grit, the camaraderie between teammates, the passion of the players, and the absolute die hard (in the best way) loyalty of the fans is the most epic thing to watch in real life when it comes to hockey!!!!”

CFP committee needs to show its work after Alabama over UCLA ranking

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Alabama is 8-2. UCLA is 10-0. And in the latest College Football Playoff (CFP) rankings that were released on Tuesday night, the Crimson Tide came in one spot (No. 3) ahead of the Bruins (No. 4) for a second straight week.

That is just one of the many things that has fueled debate the last couple weeks about whether the CFP committee favors the SEC over the Big Ten, Big 12 and ACC. I don't believe the answer is as simple as them being biased, but I also understand why people could think they are.

Because much like when Nate Bargatze was asked how many liters are in a gallon, or how many yards are in a mile in the "Washington's Dream" sketch on Saturday Night Live, "nobody knows" what the CFP committee values when it comes to their rankings.

The fix for this should be simple: tell everyone how you go about determining the rankings. Or, more bluntly, which advanced metrics are used to help rank the top-25 teams.

When asked about that very thing earlier this week by The Athletic, CFP executive director Rich Clark danced around the question a bit and ultimately said this:

"Just to let you know, we have not provided those publicly. Basically, there's so many metrics to really give that out publicly with an explanation of them isn't really feasible right now. It is something that we're considering. We're looking at this in the offseason to see what metrics and how we would present those potentially in the coming seasons."

The committee is not hiding the fact it uses metrics to help set their rankings, but why not tell fans how many, and which, metrics are used? And which ones are the most important? And how were those metrics determined to be more important than the others?

And how is it not "feasible" to simply say which metrics you use? Perhaps Clark means it isn't feasible to show every metric in a clean, TV-friendly graphic. Maybe not. But with ESPN holding the CFP's media rights through the 2031 season, it's fair to wonder how much input the network has in what the public sees.

One metric we know the CFP values is strength of schedule. Committee Chair Hunter Yurachek — the athletic director for Arkansas — has defended Alabama's ranking due to the Crimson Tide's schedule. They rank sixth in strength of record and sixth in strength of schedule on ESPN's Football Power Index (FPI).

That may be a fine and dandy explanation to a casual college football fan, but ESPN owns the media rights to the CFP, so using ESPN's own metrics is really suspect.

Other than that, what else does the committee value? Head-to-head results don't really appear to be one of them. CBS Sports noted the committee has valued head-to-head results in some cases, like Alabama over Kentucky, but not in others.

One prime example is…you guessed it…Alabama being six spots ahead of Tennessee despite the Volunteers beating the Tide by 16 points this season. Yurachek explained that decision by citing Alabama's three wins over teams in the committee's top-25.

Now, to be fair, the metrics do back up the strength of schedule debate in Alabama's favor. UCLA is first in strength of record, but just 67th in strength of schedule. With a non-conference schedule that featured teams like San Diego State (96th in FPI) Cal (97th) and Nevada (98th), that isn't incredibly surprising.

Alabama's non-conference schedule wasn't much better. Florida State (No. 40) is a shell of what it once was and is in the midst of a coaching search, and East Carolina (No. 89) and FCS Chattanooga don't move the needle either. At least not to the point where they should definitively be ahead of the Bruins with two losses.

If the strength of schedule and strength of record metrics are what the committee favors more than others, just say it. A published methodology protects the committee more than this current approach does. Instead, every close call we get currently in the rankings can look like a favor to ESPN and the SEC.

But perhaps it isn't the strength of schedule that matters; perhaps it's strength of schedule within a team's conference. ESPN's Heather Dinich said on Tuesday night's show that there is a "new metric" the committee is using this year to help them rank each team. And the metric certainly seems to favor schools where football "just means more."

So the reason No. 21 South Carolina is still ranked — even after losing last week, 34-6, to an Arkansas team (Yurachek's Arkansas team, just to remind you) that was 3-6 and had lost four of its last five games before that game — is because…the SEC's schedule is tougher? According to who, exactly? Who determines how difficult a conference's schedule really is?

Nobody knows, but it must also be the reason three other four-loss teams — No. 22 Oklahoma, No. 24 Florida, No. 25 Ole Miss — are also ranked.

Ultimately, the CFP Committee needs to be transparent before the college football season even begins. Members of the committee change every year, so the way they go about developing the rankings should be made available every offseason so we all know exactly what to expect. Then, if the committee changes their criteria midseason, those changes should be made public as well, along with legitimate reasons for why those changes were made.

Show your work, CFP Committee. Fans can handle it.

→ Baca artikel asli

Elezioni FIFA, solo tre candidati: perché Ramon Vega non c'è nell'elenco ufficiale?

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Ramon Vega non sarà in corsa per la presidenza della FIFA. Contrariamente a quanto dichiarato nei giorni scorsi a Politico, l’ex difensore della nazionale svizzera non ha presentato una candidatura valida per le elezioni del prossimo marzo. Per partecipare alla sfida servivano le firme di sostegno di almeno cinque federazioni nazionali, ma gli appoggi annunciati da Vega non sono mai arrivati in forma ufficiale.

Il 55enne aveva già tentato di entrare nella corsa alla guida del calcio mondiale in tre occasioni, nel 2016, 2019 e 2022, senza mai riuscire a superare lo scoglio delle sponsorizzazioni richieste. La nuova candidatura sembrava destinata a rappresentare una voce alternativa rispetto all’attuale gestione della FIFA, ma si è fermata prima della fase elettorale. Con l’esclusione dell’ex giocatore del Cagliari, restano dunque tre candidati ufficialmente in gioco: il presidente uscente Gianni Infantino, il giapponese Kozo Tashima e il francese Philippe Diallo.

Infantino punta a ottenere un quarto mandato consecutivo, mentre gli altri candidati proveranno a proporre un’alternativa alla linea portata avanti dalla FIFA negli ultimi anni. La corsa alla presidenza entrerà nella fase decisiva in vista del voto previsto a marzo, con un quadro ora più definito dopo il passo indietro forzato di Vega.

Julius Randle on Nets' 'embarrassing' blowout loss to Bulls: 'One of the worst games I've ever been a part of'

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Brooklyn Nets wore red, white and blue alternate uniforms on Wednesday night, throwing it back to the franchise's ABA heyday when they welcomed the Chicago Bulls to Barclays Center. The evening featured an appearance by 10 members of the teams that won ABA championships in 1974 and 1976 — including the legendary "Dr. J," Julius Erving.

"I fully anticipated the Nets to be so fired up having us here, they would blow Chicago out," the Hall of Famer said during an appearance on the YES Network broadcast midway through the second quarter. "But I'm looking at 62-39, so … I was wrong."

The Nets scored Wednesday's first basket, with young guard Nolan Traoré getting a runout dunk after Egor Dëmin picked off a Josh Giddey pass. A minute later, Bulls guard Norman Powell knocked down a 3-pointer to put Chicago on top … and, well, that was all she wrote for Brooklyn.

The Nets never again led, or even tied, the visitors, trailing by as many as 37 points in a truly dismal performance that settled as a 137-107 blowout loss. At home. At the hands of what had been a three-win team, with the NBA's fifth-worst offense and absolute worst defense … and without exciting lottery pick Caleb Wilson.

It was a wretched outing — one that saw the Nets commit 20 turnovers leading to 27 Chicago points, and allow 15 first-half 3-pointers and 76 first-half points, the third straight game in which they conceded 70-plus before intermission. Brooklyn gave up 62 total points in the paint, and sat idly by as eight different Bulls scored in double figures.

In the midst of the depressing showing, courtside cameras caught Brooklyn forward Julius Randle lighting into his teammates on the Nets bench:

After finishing the night with 17 points, four assists and no rebounds in 27 minutes of work in the lopsided loss, Randle — who's soldiered through a 17-win season with the Lakers, the post-Anthony Davis doldrums in New Orleans, and the 21-win end of the David Fizdale era in New York — sounded a similarly dour note about the state of affairs for the 3-11 Nets in his postgame press conference.

"That was embarrassing," Randle said, according to Brian Lewis of the New York Post. "Been in the league for 13 years, and that's one of the worst games I've ever been a part of. And not because of the score … but it's how we did it. That wasn't cool. Nothing about it was cool. We've got to find a way to figure out the root of it or whatever, but that's just unacceptable. It's just a pride, an effort, the competitive spirit — all of that, it was nonexistent."

The Nets entered Wednesday intending to celebrate the high-water mark in franchise history, but wound up closer to a historic nadir. According to Erik Slater of Clutch Points, the last two losses "mark the first time they've given up 135 points in back-to-back games since April 1 [and] 2, 1969."

That's a far cry from where the Nets hoped to be after trading this summer for Randle, a three-time All-Star and two-time All-NBA selection who'd starred for the cross-town New York Knicks before helping the Minnesota Timberwolves make a pair of playoff runs, to pair with incumbent sweet-shooting forward Michael Porter Jr. in the frontcourt, in hopes of restoring a franchise that had the third-worst record in the NBA over the preceding three seasons to respectability.

But despite the presence of the 20-point-scoring veteran forwards and some exciting flashes from young guards Traoré, Dëmin and rookie Mikel Brown Jr., Brooklyn has remained mired in the muck, ranking among the league's worst teams on both ends of the court and looking like a team without much of a sense of who it wants to be, how it wants to play, and what its path to success might look like.

"Very disappointed, just starting with effort," head coach Jordi Fernández said after the game, according to C.J. Holmes of the New York Daily News. "We were not a competitive group. We were not a connected group. We were not a selfless group. I saw selfishness. I saw pointing fingers, and I saw guys that didn't always play hard."

That's not the brand of ball that Fernández has preached since arriving in Brooklyn in 2024. The team's desultory effort, though, suggests that maybe what he's preaching isn't quite getting across.

"I can try to say it in Spanish, because in English, they don't understand," Fernández quipped after the game. "So I'll keep trying and find whatever language works. But yeah, our effort was not good."

Julius Randle didn't mince words when describing the state of affairs for his 3-11 Nets. (Caleb Bowlin/Getty Images)

If the coach's message is falling on deaf ears, then it's up to the players to stem the tide and turn things around. Randle appeared to be searching for a way to do so with his impassioned entreaty on the bench; with another game coming against the Orlando Magic on Friday, he says he and his teammates have to look within for the answers they're seeking.

"I think when you win, you find ways to win. When you lose, you find excuses. Seems like a lot of excuses," Randle said, according to Collin Helwig of NetsDaily. "So, you know, for me, I've got to go back into work. I've got to go back into the film. I've got to figure out — I've got to look myself in the mirror tonight, too, like everybody else, and figure out what I can do better. Not point the finger or anything. Like, I got to figure out as a man [...] that has played this game since a kid and love it, and has given a lot to me, I got to figure out how I can help the team and be better for the team. So, you know, I put it on me."
